# ARENA ENGINE — Games

The living record of game design: what exists and what is decided next. Platform rules live in `SPEC.md`, `CONTRACT.md` and `BUILD.md`; this file is the game side. Where they conflict: platform docs win on platform matters, this file wins on game-design state, because it is newer.

**In every new chat: read this file first, then `SPEC.md`.**

## The documents

- `GAMES.md` (this file) — game design state: what exists, open questions, measured facts, harness conventions
- `SPEC.md` — platform rules: architecture, pipeline, output quality, house style, studio UX
- `CONTRACT.md` — the interface every game implements: services, skills/hooks, player model, compliance checks
- `BUILD.md` — the plan: phases with pass/fail gates, definition of done, quality checklists
- *(Removed 2026-11-11, GAMES #733: the frozen `CHRONICLE-archive.md` — build chronicle through v0.8.3, the v0.9–v1.1 reconstruction, and the Measured-facts entries retired verbatim 2026-10-22 → 2026-10-28. Recover it with `git show docs-precleanup:docs/CHRONICLE-archive.md`.)*

## Current state


- **Fresh start, now with a first game.** These docs descend from an earlier, abandoned iteration; its platform philosophy is kept, its game content was cleared. Game #1 has since been designed, built as a preview, and visually locked.
- Platform: fully specified, **no engine code yet**. Start at Phase 0 in `BUILD.md`.
- **Game #1 — SPINCLASH — rules stable, frame locked, weapon roster COMPLETE at 22 AND LANDED at build roster-r12 (2026-10-25; commission queue empty, convention CLOSED 2026-10-27).** The only running code is `spinclash-preview.html` (dependency-free browser sim, a design toy, not the engine) plus its harnesses (`measure.mjs`, `chromecheck.mjs`, `ingest.mjs` DORMANT since roster-r12, and the `.qa` proof probes — `inscribe`, `fingerprint`, `leadproof`, `flipproof`, plus the landing baker `bake-rows`; roles in **Testing recipe**. **CLEANUP 2026-10-28 (owner go, pre-skills): the spent one-shot diagnostics were deleted — `natscan.mjs`, `boomdiag.mjs`, `reveal-probe.mjs`, `killstill.mjs`, `.qa/chainlaw.mjs`, `.qa/seedtrace.mjs`, `.qa/rollattr.mjs`, `.qa/cellscan.mjs`, `.qa/projectile-fix.mjs` and the four historical `fp-*.json` checkpoints, plus the dormant `scratch/arena-test.mjs` look-bench generator and its two smokes, and `scratch/HOWTO-measure.md` (its still-true Windows traps are ported into `scratch/measure-parts.md`). Every finding they produced stays in Measured facts; the artifacts do not.**). The preview sits at **v1.2** (2026-10-19): the sling is gone — replaced by the **bow** (v0.9) — and a nine-weapon batch (v1.0) plus a shared-length pass (v1.1) grew the roster to **13 weapons, all path silhouettes**; the parts representation is dead. v1.2 is the alignment session: the **projectile rule** (a weapon that fires a shot deals no spine damage — any hold) and the **launch doctrine** (radial hold → tip, tangential hold → middle) were fixed/codified, the mixed-speed shot budget repaired, `measure.mjs` brought to v1.x reality, and the docs re-aligned to the code. Same-day tooling addition (renderer/chrome-only — sim fingerprints identical): a Slow-motion ¼× chip, and the dice reveal is now the owner-designed **VS poster** (his design, copied verbatim at his word, then polished the same day on his playtest notes and its labels regrouped into fighter/weapon blocks — cast disc + written-out skill line over weapon name + trait, owner option 1; landing truth and cadence proven by `.qa/reveal-probe.mjs`). The chrome phase line is removed at owner word (its `chromecheck.mjs` guards moved to the state/canvas surfaces). A later same-day owner tune lengthened the reveal ~1s (`revealTicks` 165 → 285, ~2.4s — the second splits between a ~1.4× slower roll cadence and a longer end hold): a deliberate sim-timeline shift with battle dynamics proven untouched (fingerprint deltas exactly +120 ticks × rounds) and `.qa/fp-after.json` re-baselined. A same-day follow-up tune made the two landings **simultaneous** (`VS.land` 0.32/0.46 → 0.39/0.39, both slams at tick ~111 — owner: "i want oth weapons to appear at the same time"; renderer-only, the sim never reads `VS`, so fingerprints are untouched by construction). A same-day frame-loop addition: **hitstop** — every damage hit briefly freezes time and the killing blow gets a long beat before the K.O. card (trigger policy 1 at owner word: "implement it, and do 1 trigger policy"; renderer-layer, fingerprints proven byte-identical — see **Measured facts**). A same-day sim tune fixed the **parry loop** (machine-gun parries at point-blank): owner option 1 ("1") — `parryPush` 150 → 525 (3.5×), a magnitude-only dial (the laws fix direction and trigger, untouched), the worst loop cell halved its parries and ended ~6.4k ticks sooner; fingerprints re-baselined (see **Measured facts**).
- **Hit still landed (2026-10-21, owner option 1) — SUPERSEDED for non-kill hits 2026-10-27 (`pause-r20`): the freeze is now a true pause of the on-screen frame (owner: "i want it completely still during hitstop, so no moving at all"); the contact latch proved to be the only motion inside the freeze window (onset/release micro-steps, visible at ¼×). Kill still stands. Record in the pause-r20 Measured-facts entry.** Original bullet follows for the record — — the owner's seed-12024 symptom ("as the mace left the body somehow heavy took damage again") was a **substep-only graze**: a legal contact that lived entirely between drawn frames (detection runs per substep; the renderer draws tick poses), so live motion showed flash + HP move with no touch, against the visible-contact-first directive. Every damage hit's hitstop freeze now draws its stored contact poses — the kill still's mechanism generalised to non-kill hits; stone landings freeze the shot at its impact point. Renderer-only, fingerprints byte-identical; class size, latch proofs and the seed-12024 replay in **Measured facts**; `.qa/hitstill.mjs` retained.

- **Orbs round, gold retired from art (2026-10-27).** `build orb-r14` recut the three orbs to 32-gons and disclosed the regen-splice lesson (partial art edits splice via `bake-rows`); `build sweep-r15` flushed the orb's sigil band to the rim, swept all 28 gold shapes + the staff band to `KD` at owner option B (`KG` survives as the carrier-less fallback only), and recut the roundness class (nine true circles; seven elongated wraps exempt by the aspect gate). Owner playtest: "it looks good with the new color". Full records in the two new **Measured facts** entries; convention-close item 1a amended; artist-kit rebuild owed at the next commission kickoff.
- **Clinch spring landed (2026-10-27, `build clinch-r18`).** Sustained blade interlocks are self-dissolving now: a ramped separation force acts only while the latch holds (smoothstep 48t ramp, capped acceleration, smooth decay on release), which prevents the rim-pinned point-blank grind bind (owner: "i think it fixes it, i dont see it anymore") after two reverted eyes-first passes (`parry-r16` brake package, `chain-r17` escalation ladder). Open-space play feels nothing (latch open = force zero); some cap-prone cells lengthen toward the 300s line — balance-pass property, recorded. Record in **Measured facts**.
- **Carry-kill landed (2026-10-27, `build carry-r19`).** Parries absorb 65% of the bodies' in-flight velocity at the clang (`parryCarryKill`), which makes the rule-5 recoil visible on the head's screen path at the clang — closing the 99797 case where the axe's reversal was masked by rim-bounce carry (owner: "it looks good now"). Quick-measure red set unchanged from the sweep-r15 baseline; projectile-fix termination red improved 6/18 → 4/18. Record in **Measured facts**.

The rotational-DOF rework is **deferred until after length growth**, which changes every reach.
- **Next: the skills pass — the pass is now ON the weapon traits: the first special skill (`skill-r29`), the two seams (`seam-r30`) and the pairing step (`pair-r31`) have all landed, so what remains is a unique trait for each of the other 21 weapons and then more bots.** [AMENDED 2026-10-31: the staff's trait landed as `shotgrow-r33` — orb growth, 20% per landing, provisional; **20 weapons remain**.] [AMENDED again 2026-10-31, `nocombo-r34`: the owner's full-pass ruling — **every weapon a unique trait, every bot a unique skill, additive stacking only, NO unique combinations** (which retired `skill.lenSteps` the same landing). Order: trait mechanics first (20 remain), then the cast, then balance — *"dont think about balance etc and numbers"* until the mechanics are in place.] [AMENDED 2026-11-01, `bouncegrow-r40`: the dagger, the boomerang (now PARKED at the owner's call — its landed halo form stays canonical) and the shuriken later, 17 weapons remain.] [AMENDED again the SAME DAY, `ricolaw-r41`: the owner's eye superseded the shuriken's budget axis with the pure ricochet LAW — the slot re-opens, so 18 weapons remain, two of them (boomerang, shuriken) with landed mechanics but parked/re-open growth axes.] [AMENDED yet again 2026-11-01, #670: the owner CLOSED the shuriken's slot — *"yes shuriken is done"* — THE LAW IS THE TRAIT (doctrine: a trait may be a law, not a growth number); **17 weapons remain**, and the boomerang alone carries a parked growth question.] [AMENDED yet again 2026-11-01, `multgrow-r42`: the BOW'S RAIN landed — the launcher class's first growth axis, riding the seam-r30 cadence multiplier, together with the generalized owner-blind cross law (no same-owner shot cross anywhere); **16 weapons remain**.] [AMENDED yet again 2026-11-01, `fan-r43`: the knives' growing fan landed — **15 weapons remain**, five of them launchers (crossbow, wand, javelin, tome, scroll).] [AMENDED yet again 2026-11-01, `pinlaw-r44`: the crossbow's nails landed — **14 weapons remain**, four of them launchers (wand, javelin, tome, scroll).] Amended 2026-10-28 (this replaces the headline's "moves on to the first special skill", which was true when written and is now three builds behind); the three preparatory steps it records follow verbatim: step 1 (the length-growth coverage probe) is **done and conclusive**, step 2 (the resolver's inverted stall test plus its two falsified comments) **landed alone as `stall-r25`**, behaviour-affecting, 7 of 8 fingerprints moved, new baseline `.qa/fp-stall-r25.json`, and **step 3 — the minimal spear-only length-growth build — is BUILT as `lengrow-r26`** in the owner's chosen scope: `f.lenStep` plus its per-round reset, the twelve-step ladder, `bladeCircles` and the renderer, and `grow()` applying the rate, with `REACH` and `MINR` **left as per-weapon tables**. One correction to how that scope was described when it was picked: `REACH.spear` **was** raised, once at boot, to the **top step's** value (210.412 → **505.488**, 2.40× base), so the "slight under-budgeting of a grown spear's substeps" this bullet predicted **does not occur** — the residual is that low steps are slightly **over**-budgeted, which is the safe direction, and `MINR` is now the **ladder-wide minimum, 2.00**, because a re-baked step can pack thinner than the shipped row — r26's "MINR needs no correction" held only while radii were held. The **length-growth rule is BUILT** — see "Length growth — the spear's trait" (spear trait, **9.6%** per damaging hit **on the drawn tip** — raised twice the same day, on *"more ridiculous"* and then *"its not ridiculous enough"* — 12 steps to **+200.1%** (the spear triples) via derived shaft factors to **4.2769**. A `d`-remapped covering is bounded at **+39.5%** (swept, MISS-bound), so past that each step carries its own **re-baked** row from the house packer, length-only so it never thickens, no `lenCap` because the ladder top is the geometry ceiling and the round reset is the balance bound, `dmg` 0.7 → 0.3 provisional). The **hook machinery is still not built**, per the cast ruling that it waits for the first genuinely contextual special skill; a weapon-row *rate* is data under M2 and needs no hook. Artifact pointers in the bullets above that name since-deleted probes (`.qa/hitstill.mjs`, `.qa/reveal-probe.mjs`, `.qa/fp-after.json` and the retired `fp-*.json` checkpoints) **dangle deliberately** — the 2026-10-28 cleanup entry lists what was removed, and every finding those probes produced survives in Measured facts. **[AMENDED 2026-11-10: the pass is deep into the melee era — the magic journeys concluded (the wand's TRANSFIGURATION #710, the scroll's EMBER SPILL #719), and the flail's slot is filled: THE LONG CHAIN `longchain-r86` (#720), rebuilt as THE ADDING-LINKS CHAIN `links-r87` (#721). Traits 14 of 22; remaining: the 7 melee blanks (hammer, axe, sword, mace, nunchaku, scythe, katana) + the orb, then the bot cast, then balance — the owner's order.]**

## Game #1 — SPINCLASH

Engine id `spinclash` — kebab-case, frozen at naming (CONTRACT). Named 2026-10-15 under SPEC.md "Game naming rules". Tagline: *"Blades clash. Bots snowball."*

A physics auto-battler: two bots drop into a circular arena (inscribed in the platform's fixed square bounds) under heavy gravity, each swinging an orbiting weapon. Genre reference: earclacks (cosmicoptima) — **inspiration only**; the rules, code and visuals here are original (the reference file was purged at owner word 2026-10-22 — the lessons remain; note the v1.0 roster leans on familiar archetypes — see open question on prior-art wording).

- **Battle = best of 3 rounds**, first to 2 round wins. Video target: 1min+ of battle (platform intro/outro add to it).
- **Per-round dice roll.** Before every round, the seeded roll assigns each fighter a weapon from the roster; **duplicates allowed** (mirror duels are deliberate comedy). Fixed draw order (2 weapon draws + 1 opening-spin-direction draw + 2 launch draws — one angle per fighter — per round), so `(seed)` fully determines a battle. The direction draw opens every round **counter-rotating** — mechanism, owner decision and rationale in Core contact law rule 6.
- **Stats reset every round** — each round is a fresh snowball; escalation is carried by the series situation (ROUND 3 = DECIDER banner, scorebug always visible), not by stat carry-over.
- **Rounds end on K.O. only** — no timer, by owner decision; the harness watches stalls and non-termination and the decision is revisited only if they prove real (they currently do — open question 1).
- **Damage and parry.** Weapon touching the opponent's **body** is a damaging hit + knockback — **unless the weapon fires a projectile** (owner decision 2026-10-19: "ranged weapons (shooting projectiles), their weapons do not damage, only their projetiles"): a projectile weapon's spine shoves but never cuts; its shot is its only damage. **[AMENDED 2026-11-02, `allcut-r62` — THE PROJECTILE RULE IS RETIRED at the owner's word (*"all weapons from now on deals damage if they touch the opponets body… launchers are boring to watch in the beginning as they have a hard time hitting the opponents and gaining growth"*): EVERY weapon cuts on body touch — the ten launchers AND the shield (its tangential no-damage clause was the rule's last remnant); the spine chip rides the WDEF base damage 1 and feeds grow() like any melee hit, so every launcher-side trait trigger wakes. The 2026-10-19 decision above is history.]** Weapon touching **weapon** is a **parry**: no damage, both spins recoil (flip), bodies shoved apart along the true contact normal. Every *fresh* exact touch is one parry — a hysteresis latch (the 4px `parryPad`) makes a sustained press count once, so no timer gates the physics or the events; full trigger semantics in law 2. The **Core contact law** below is the normative text. Whether a parry should also *reward* the parrier is an open question.
- **Growth, uncapped (owner decision).** Every damaging hit grows the attacker: the fighter's skill **and** the weapon's trait both apply. Fights are meant to get ridiculous — spinning out of control is the show. The only ceilings are sim stability (omegaCap 140 rad/s; adaptive substeps against tunneling), not gameplay. **Measured 2026-10-28 (pre-skills): that ceiling is 36.5 damaging hits away, and it clamps SILENTLY.** The fastest spin observed across 30 naturals is **103.5 rad/s** = base 3.5 + exactly 100 hits × 1.0/hit (weapon trait 0.70 + Speedy 0.30), so `omegaCap` 140 bites at 136.5 hits on that axis — no readout, no event, the fighter simply stops getting faster. Owner asked what this is; explained 2026-10-28, and of the four options offered the owner picked **measure the real limit first** — done the same day, read-only: **the clamp is DORMANT (removing it changes nothing at all) and the real ceiling is structural, hp ÷ damage = 100 hits → max spin 103.5.** The sim itself is stable to ω ≈ 258 at the worst of 484 pairs. Full numbers in the spin-ceiling measurement entry. Under the **Uniform base stats** decision (below), growth is the *only* source of spin/damage divergence — weapons start equal and snowball apart. On projectile weapons the spin axis *is* the fire rate, so `+SPD / Hit` means "shoots faster".
- **Fighters (placeholder roster).** **Speedy** — weapon spins faster per damaging hit (+0.30 SPD). **Heavy** — weapon hits harder per damaging hit (+0.9 DMG). These are their one-skill-per-game hooks (CONTRACT.md §Skills).
- **Hidden forcing.** The viewer always sees the roll as random; `newGame(seed, {weapons})` forces a combo silently (the future studio config exposes this as the curator knob; `render.json` will record the truth).
- **1v1 only** (owner decision). A 3-man "pure chaos" brawl is a wanted future mode — it will need SPEC's match-shape sentence widened by one line when it arrives.

### The roster (22 weapons — COMPLETE: v1.2 data + flail 2026-10-21 + batch-4, javelin and tome 2026-10-22 + scroll and orb 2026-10-23; ALL numbers provisional until the balance pass)

Uniform bases (owner decision, below): **ω 3.5 rad/s, dmg 1** for every weapon (damage 1 landed 2026-10-28 at `dmg1-r21` by owner ruling — it was 5 through the whole v0.7→`pause-r20` era); `weaponScale` 1.3 applied at boot (provisional — one number to dial). Lengths below are pre-scale; as played ≈ len × 1.3. Every len sits inside the boot-gated band **54 (the orb) → `LT` 113.44 (the spear's length)** — six weapons sit exactly at the ceiling (hammer, axe, spear, mace, crossbow, javelin). Traits fire on damaging hits only (for projectile weapons: on landings only).

⚠ **Recorded, balance-deferred (owner 2026-10-19: "dont bother yet, the game will be balanced later on"):** the dagger's trait was +0.7 **SPIN** in the owner-locked v0.7 growth-identity decision; the undocumented era changed it to +0.7 DMG. When and why is unrecorded — it stays as-is until the balance pass. **Owner ruling 2026-10-28 (asked directly, in the pre-skills decisions): neither ratify nor revert — the skills pass decides every weapon's growth axis at once. This ⚠ is therefore a DELIBERATE deferral, not an unrecorded accident; +0.7 DMG ships unchanged until then and no sim change was made for it.** *[Reframed 2026-10-28 now that the cast is scoped: the axis question is concrete and is a **synergy** question. +0.7 DMG makes the dagger a Heavy-synergy weapon (Heavy’s +0.9 → 1.6 per damaging hit together); +0.7 SPIN — its original v0.7 identity — makes it a Speedy-synergy weapon (+0.30 → 1.00/hit, the cell that produced the measured ω 103.5 maximum) and moves the roster split from 12 +DMG / 10 +SPIN to 11/11. It is one number and no plumbing either way: a trait is weapon data.] **[RULED 2026-10-28 — +0.7 DMG stands, the ⚠ is CLOSED: the owner took the first branch on the datum (13th of 22, 53.8% holder win rate, dead average). The dagger keeps its DMG axis and is a **Heavy-synergy** weapon (1.6 per damaging hit together); the roster split stays **12 +DMG / 10 +SPIN**. **No sim change was made — the row already ships `trait: { spin: 0.0, dmg: 0.7 }`**, so ratifying it moves no byte: canonical `e401e3e33c3133b03ec192f7e5fe0199` unchanged, 3,093 lines, no fingerprint movement, no re-baseline, no stamp bump. See the dagger-axis entry in Measured facts; the +0.7 SPIN alternative and how to take it later are recorded there.]* The shield cannot damage at all (tangential spine + no projectile): a shield fighter cannot win a round, and shield+shield never terminates. Left untouched deliberately — the balance pass owns it.

**Classes as built (v1.2 + batch-4):** *melee* = radial hold, damaging spine, no shot (hammer, dagger, axe, sword, spear, mace, scythe, katana — and the **orb**, the sentinel shape: radial-hold melee with NO SHAFT, the floating business end alone at the tip of an invisible spine; its orbit is the native tip orbit — one rev per spin, reversed by every parry recoil, quickened by growth; its `len` is the orbit radius to the orb CENTRE, the first row whose art extends past its len — every consumer reads the polys, not len (REACH/SPAN from vertices, no tip launch, no headLead pivot, pop centre = bbox centre = the orb centre), audited at merge). *launchers* = fire a shot, spine never damages (the projectile rule): radial-hold **staff, wand** (tip launch) and tangential-hold **bow, crossbow, knives, tome, scroll** (middle, radially out of the belly) plus **boomerang, shuriken, javelin** (middle, tangentially in the spin direction). *shield* = tangential, no shot, no damage — the odd one out, awaiting design. *chain* = flail (2026-10-21, first of its class) + nunchaku (batch-4 2026-10-22, the first to ride the N-segment pre-wire: 2 segments, 1 free particle): radial hold, damaging spine like melee, but the spine is **segmented** — the base segment rigid, links and head hinged at joints and integrated as free particles under `flailJoint`/`flailDrag` (`flailStep`), so the end lags and whips behind the haft (flail: two ring links + spiked ball at J0/J1/J2; nunchaku: ring link + bar + stick at J0); their covering circles are baked **per segment** (flail 49 circles in 12/14/15/8 bake batches, tied 9/13/14/13; nunchaku 27 in 8+19, tied 8/19 — the runtime tie is geometric, centre-in-outer-ink, innermost first) and each rides its own segment's pose (`bladeCircles(…, f.fl)`), the only weapons whose hitboxes articulate. Hold is placement + launch point; **damage capability is decided by the projectile rule, not the hold** (owner 2026-10-19 — this replaced the v0.7.x "ranged = tangential = no spine damage" class rule, under which staff/wand wrongly damaged with their spines; fixed in v1.2).

- **Renderer facts.** Bots are ink-bordered colored discs with **only the black HP number inside**; a **bright white hit flash** marks whichever bot got hit — the frame's only hit punctuation. Weapons draw from their own collision data (verbatim path polygons + covering circles): **every weapon above every body**, grip tips tucked invisible under the own disc (the tuck rule; rim-held and handle-less weapons carry `tuck: false`). **Edge-leading mirror (owner decision 2026-10-22):** directionally asymmetric weapons mirror art AND hitbox together on spin sign (`leadFlip`, one source in the placement layer) on their declared axis — `lead: ±1` for the lateral cutting side (katana −1, scythe −1: the chisel EDGE, the hook BEAK; owner playtest: a mouth-forward hook "is spinning the wrong way from the beginning"), `headLead: ±1` for the along-chord head END (javelin +1, owner: "i think the javelin should also flip around like scythe and katana" — a d-mirror pivoting about the centred rim grip, tangential-only by boot guard) — so the business contour always faces the direction of motion; `view[i].flip` is the DISPLAYED factor — continuous only during a transition; the parry re-presentation is **animated** at owner word ("i thought something else, like a fast transition not an instant snap"):
the blade eases old → edge-on → mirrored as cos(π·p) over `CFG.flipMs` (180ms — raised from 90 at owner playtest "its still too snappy"; a mid-sweep re-parry lands the first flip and starts a second continuous one, and a hitstop overlapping the sweep freezes it with the frame), reading as a spin about its own spine — art-only, fight-phase-only (every phase entry snaps; the bell's roll is not a parry), frozen with the frame under a hitstop. The sim truth stays binary everywhere it matters: `view[i].fpSim` feeds the hitbox debug overlay (never the squash), the kill still latches the full ±1 contact pose (a damage event inside the window freezes into honest geometry; non-kill freezes are true pauses since pause-r20), and the sim/laws/audits never see the transition (fingerprints byte-identical). The disclosed art≠hitbox window (~5 drawn frames per flagged parry) is the owner-accepted trade — containment and proofs in Measured facts. The two invisible glint iterations were removed with it (`.qa/glintproof.mjs` retired; `.qa/flipproof.mjs` is the standing proof); the kill still latches the flip from the event's stored signed spin; the VS poster draws the canonical orientation (`drawFitWeapon` takes no flip).
No contact ghosts, no spin trails, no particles, no screen shake, no glow, **no game name on screen** — proof-of-contact lives entirely in the harness (owner decisions, step1c/step1d, archive); the growth tell lives only in the legend's SPD number. **Sigil (owner decision 2026-10-23):** every weapon carries exactly ONE diegetic element in the wielding fighter's cast color — shapes tagged `KC` are swapped to `COLS[f.idx]` in `drawWeapon`/`drawWeaponChain` (gold fallback for carrier-less renders; `KC` is an invalid CSS color, so an unswapped sigil fails visibly, never silently). The doctrine is prescriptive in **Weapon art** (6), the mark map lives in `roster-redesign-brief.md`; the mechanism is live at `build marks-r10`, inert until the redesign delivery tags shapes. Shots draw per kind, and **every kind inks its own hitbox disc** — a faint full-disc halo at exactly radius r under the art (owner directive 2026-10-19, visible-contact-first; the orb's original 28% halo is the shared language, `HALO` alphas are dials): disc-shaped shots: the drawn disc *is* the hitbox; arrows/bolts: the hitbox circle sits at the head, shaft and fletching trail *behind* it (the head tip spikes slightly past it forward — safe direction, ink leads the hit).
When any shot was thrown, the winner overlay's stats line grows a `shots X thrown / Y landed / Z swatted` tail. Preview tooling (chips: Pause / Slow motion / Fast forward / Restart / New seed / Hitboxes; seed row) never renders into a video; playback is a real-time catch-up loop at 1×, Slow motion ≈¼× and Fast-forward ≈4× (owner request 2026-10-19; the two are mutually exclusive — engaging one releases the other). **Hitstop (owner decision 2026-10-19 — trigger policy 1: "implement it, and do 1 trigger policy"):** every damage hit freezes sim time briefly — `CFG.hitStopMs` 60 (≈7 ticks); the killing blow gets `CFG.koStopMs` 400 (≈48 ticks) on the impact pose, *then* the K.O. beat runs its `koTicks` — with no card type: the K.O. announcement was removed at owner word 2026-10-21 ("i dont want it to be announced"); the round's end reads as the freeze, the faded death-mark disc and the scorebug tick, then the next reveal. The gate lives in the frame loop (`stopTicks` counts the accumulator down in sim ticks — set from the `hit` events in `consumeEvents`), so the sim sequence and every fingerprint are untouched by construction.
Parry/wall/bounce never stop time (no damage); same-tick stops max-merge, never stack; the freeze counts on the playback clock — fast-forward shortens it in wall time, slow-mo stretches it (constant sim-time pause); wall-clock FX (flash decay, banner) freeze with it so the impact frame holds, and `viewA` keeps its leftover fraction — no pose snap. Leftover freezes drain at `G.over` so the winner overlay decays normally. **The freeze is a TRUE pause of the on-screen frame (owner 2026-10-27, `pause-r20`):** nothing latches for non-kill hits — every freeze frame repeats the onset frame pixel-for-pixel, so a graze's freeze holds exactly what the viewer was already seeing and can introduce no gap. The 2026-10-21 hit still (contact-pose override for non-kill freezes) is SUPERSEDED by that word: its onset/release boundary micro-steps proved to be the only motion inside the window. The KILL still (2026-10-19) keeps latching the killing contact for the 400 ms K.O. hold, including a landing shot frozen at its impact point (spliced on death, so the impact was never a drawn pose). Renderer-only; born from the seed-12024 graze diagnosis in **Measured facts**. **Weapons visible at all times (owner 2026-10-21, seed 80117; final form: the single-canvas overflow band).** The canvas IS the sim square plus a 150-unit band, absolutely centred over its cssPx layout box (the wrap keeps the flow height; the band overhangs the chrome rows, pointer-transparent), at the approved disc scale U — tips past the square (worst ~146 units: long radial tip, body at the rim) draw whole into the band instead of cutting at the old canvas edge, which at the four axis directions coincides with the rim's outer edge and read as the weapon dipping UNDER the rim.
Same-day history: a margin-band zoom was reverted for its zoom-out ("i think something is off"); a second band canvas was reverted for a seam line where its fractional CSS offset put the two device-pixel grids out of agreement (owner: "you can see the line") — one backing store, one grid, no seam class. Disc pixel size, page flow and chrome untouched; renderer-only, fingerprints byte-identical. Disclosed: extreme top/bottom tips draw over the scorebug/legend rows (the out-of-bounds spectacle). The engine-era square-plus-margins frame and contract check 6's ink-in-margin question join open question 9, with the band as the preview precedent. The band's first pass positioned `#ov` against `#wrap` instead of against the centred main canvas, drawing the ring adrift by the canvas's centring offset (owner screenshot seed 46322: a shaft gap with an orphan tip at the page edge); `resize` now offsets the band by that centring offset — fixed same day, renderer-only, fingerprints byte-identical, suite green. **Render interpolation (v0.8.2, owner-approved; reference implementation: the owner's `sumo_war (2).html`, credited):** every actor draws at a pose blended between the last two tick poses — fighter positions and weapon angles (shortest-arc), shot positions — with alpha = the catch-up loop's leftover tick fraction, so motion stays even on displays whose refresh is not an integer multiple of the 120 Hz tick.
Whenever the sim is not stepping (paused, reveal, K.O. card, over) alpha = 1: those frames render the exact latest tick pose and the actors stand still (the reveal poster animates on its own phaseT clock over frozen actors). **The dice reveal — the VS poster (owner-designed 2026-10-19, copied verbatim at his word, then polished the same day on his playtest notes — smaller weapon fit, weapon name matched to the bot-name recipe, speed-line FX contained, unused `VS` fields stripped; replaces the floating-card default).** For its `revealTicks: 285` (~2.4s — extended from 165/~1.4s at owner word 2026-10-19, +1s: the extra second splits between a slower roll cadence (`land` 0.40/0.58 → 0.32/0.46 → 0.39/0.39 across the +1s and simultaneous-landing tunes — same shuffle counts over a longer window, so the steps themselves stretch) and a ~1.45s end hold, the same read-time reasoning as the label-block spread) a split-screen versus poster covers the arena disc: cream plates (`PLATE` = `TH.floor`), each tinted 0.55 with its cast color under a diagonal-stripe pattern, wipe in from the sides over the first ~26 ticks behind a giant VS (dead-centre ink divider, ROUND n above at y 128).
Each half reads as two label blocks (owner decision 2026-10-19, his option-1 pick after viewers misread the old name stack as one compound — "SPEEDY HAMMER"): the TOP block is the FIGHTER — the scorebug/legend's ink-ringed cast disc ("ring = bot" vocabulary) + name at y 258 (disc + name centre as one unit) + the bot's skill as one short prose sentence at y 306 (`FIGHTERS[i].line`, the weapon trait line's exact recipe; owner wording 2026-10-19: "we shoud write it out not just +SPD etc" — Speedy "Spins faster each hit", Heavy "Stronger each hit", "short is best", every future bot ships its own line). The blocks then pulled APART on the owner's same-day tune — "move 'round' text a bit more up, including the bot names and their skill descriptions, maybe also move the weapon names and their descritions down … im just thinking when we get other bots, they might need a bit more text" — so each block owns more air for longer prose; all layout slots (roundY/nameY/skillY/wnameY/wlineY/dotsY/box) live in the `VS` constants the reveal-probe parses, single-source. The fighter block still clears the weapon ink:
at the smaller fit box the hammer head corner reaches ≈ y 385 at rest and ≈ y 374 mid-slam (the original sketch had the block at 322/366 against the big box's 374/364 — geometry recomputed at each size). The BOTTOM block is the DICE — the weapon slot at (275|725, ~490): tinted silhouettes (deep tint, 0.34 alpha) roll on a sqrt-decelerating cadence — the roll visibly slows into its end (the owner's playtest note, baked into the easing) — hold the final candidate for `pre: 7` ticks, then the rolled weapon slams in full colour, 1.9×→1.0 over those 7 ticks, with a white shockwave ring, 14 radial speed lines (the whole FX burst is clipped to the canvas bounds — owner fix 2026-10-19; the leftmost lines used to overhang the frame edge by ~18px) and a ≤14-tick shake. Landings are **simultaneous by owner decision** (2026-10-19: "i want oth weapons to appear at the same time"): both slams at 0.39 of the reveal (tick ~111) — the midpoint of the retired 0.32/0.46 stagger (the fast fighter's card used to lock first), so the +1s tune's rhythm survives the merge; both halves' shockwave/shake/speed-line bursts fire on the same tick.
The weapon name pops at y 660 wearing the bot name's exact recipe — same 48px 900-weight face, same 5px tracking, uppercase (owner decision 2026-10-19: the two names match) — and the full trait sentence (`W.line`, auto-split into ≤3 lines) fades in below; a `· · ·` placeholder holds the slot before the landing. A tail fade (~17 ticks, `revealFrame` — fractional, so it stretched with the +1s tune, as did the wipe-in) dissolves the poster into the arena. Ownership is taught by the tinted half + the name inside it (the old "for X" line is gone). Renderer-only helpers: `drawFitWeapon` (bbox-fit into a 245×150 box — twice shrunk at owner word 2026-10-19: the 330×200 original read a touch too big, then "just a little bit smaller again" alongside the label-block spread; near-uniform scale since v1.1's shared lengths), `clamp01`, `revealFrame`, `PLATE`; the +1s reveal extension shifts the sim timeline by design (`startFight` gates on `revealTicks`) — fingerprints re-baselined into `.qa/fp-after.json` with the v1.2-reference bytes retained at `.qa/fp-pre-rt285.json` (file purged in the 2026-10-22 cleanup — this record's delta proof stands), and the delta proof bounds the change exactly (+120 ticks × rounds per battle, dynamics untouched — see **Measured facts**); cadence + landing truth proven per-tick against the real `drawReveal` by `.qa/reveal-probe.mjs`.
Interpolation bases snap at every teleport (round rolls, launch angles, shot births); the hitbox overlay rides the same blended pose as the art. **The sim and all laws live at tick poses only** — see the pose amendment under the Core contact law. Layout lineage: the owner's early sumo-war reference page (file purged at owner request; the lessons remain).
- **Vocabulary map.** The sim-internal generic for any projectile is **`shot`** (`G.shots`, `G.shotSid`, `shotFire`/`shotRim`/`shotCross` events, `W.shot` data, the hit event's `shot: true` flag, harness accumulators) — renamed 2026-10-28 at owner word ("renmae now"); it was `stone`, a legacy of the removed sling, kept for years because renaming the sim invalidates nothing but touches everything. The audience-facing word is the same **shot**, so one concept now carries one name through every layer; per-weapon names (arrow, orb, spark, bolt, boomerang, star, knife, javelin, page, scroll) appear in copy via `shot.what`. The old `stone` vocabulary survives only in dated records, the 2026-10-28 chainlaw capture, and the frozen bench's surviving generator + smoke — the pre-rename bench page itself retired at the 2026-10-28 checkpoint clean (the owner holds a copy). **The weapon row's rate is `trait`** — renamed from `ability` 2026-10-28 at owner word ("we cahnge it to trait for the weapons") so the pair reads *fighter skill* / *weapon trait*: `skill` is CONTRACT/SPEC's platform term for what makes a PLAYER behave differently, and "skill"/"ability" are near-synonyms that were colliding in prose ("weapon skills"). **Discipline: never the bare nouns.** Identifier-only and behaviour-identical (normalized byte-identity + fingerprints 8/8), no stamp bump; dated records and the removed chronicle archive keep the old word by design, exactly as they keep `stone`. No other synonyms.

### Core contact law (owner decision, 2026-10-16)

The fundamental-mechanics contract, set **before** the weapon redesign: every weapon, skill and hit rule — now and future — must satisfy these six. **Audit status (2026-10-19, v1.2):** all six were green at every full baseline from v0.8.1 through v0.8.3 (numbers in the archive), and are **re-proven green on the current 13-weapon v1.2 build** by the two sanctioned runs of 2026-10-19 — part 0 (42 battles, 6.4M substep poses) and the `--solidity` overlap gauntlet (76 battles, 1.1M rendered fight-ticks): zero violations of all six laws and all seven shot laws. The forced matrix — now **484 cells** at the 22-weapon roster — remains the one unrun scope (deferred with the balance pass). `measure.mjs` re-verifies all six against every event and every substep pose on every run ("what you see is what hits" — this is the principle's mechanics half). The shot laws extend these six — normative text and the seven-point audit live in **Projectile weapons**.

1. **Damage requires touch.** A bot takes damage only when the opponent's weapon overlaps its body disc — and the drawn disc *is* the collision circle (radius 64). Nothing else writes HP: walls, body-vs-body checks and spin growth never damage. *Status: law since v0.5, proven at every full baseline (0 phantom hits ever)* — every hit event stores its contact pose and the audit recomputes the touch from it. The projectile rule narrows the converse: a projectile weapon's spine touching the body deals **no** damage (shot only) — damage still requires touch; touch no longer guarantees damage from such a spine (law 7s audits it).
2. **Parry requires weapon-on-weapon collision.** Only the blade-vs-blade gap can trigger a parry; body proximity is irrelevant. The trigger is exact touch (surface gap ≤ 0); the 4px `parryPad` is the hysteresis re-arm margin and substep-sizing window, not a gameplay window. *Status: enforced and proven at every full baseline* — 0 parries ever fired without touch; every entry gap bites in. The one sanctioned extension: a blade touching a **shot** is a *swat*, not a parry (Projectile weapons) — audited to exact touch the same way (0 swats without touch ever). **Second sanctioned extension (owner option 1, 2026-10-21): the flutter gate.** After a parry fires, the latch cannot re-arm for `parryGate` 16 ticks even if the blades clear the pad; contacts inside the gate stay fully solid (resolver + approach-velocity kill) but fire nothing — no flip, no shove, no event. Born from the owner's seed-14052 playtest: a 4-clang vibrato burst (parries at +12/+4/+10 ticks, bottom cradle, wall-assisted) that "looks ugly for the viewer". Re-parry gaps are bimodal (flutter cluster ≤16 ticks vs legit p10 81), so 16 cuts the flutter and nothing else: post-gate scan over 20 naturals — gaps ≤16: 51 → **0**, bursts 36 → 5 (1.8 → 0.3 per battle, residuals being clean double-taps at ≥17 ticks). The trigger sentence therefore reads: every fresh exact touch **at least 16 ticks after the previous parry** is one parry; the latch — not a timer — still prevents re-fires during a sustained press, and the timer gates only the re-arm, never the solidity.
3. **Weapons are solids to each other.** No tunneling, no interpenetration — not on a rendered frame, not between frames. A per-substep resolver de-overlaps along the true contact normal (the bodies are pushed apart, then re-honoured against body-vs-body and the arena; weapons ride rigidly on bodies), kills the approaching velocity, and recoils the spins on fresh contact. Detection is guaranteed: substeps are sized so relative travel per substep ≤ half the narrowest contact window, so any real crossing spans at least two tests. Nothing gates it — the old cooldown skip was the bug. *Status: enforced and proven at every full baseline, and re-proven on v1.2 (part 0 + the solidity gauntlet)* — one representation class since v0.9 (every weapon is a path silhouette): the disclosed band was stated as ≤6px worst / ≤2% of fight-ticks (the infeasible-pin transient, attributable) — **bound FALSIFIED and mechanism CORRECTED 2026-10-28** by `--band-dump` geometry at both worst cases: measured **20.754px** (flail+scythe s104 t12272) and **14.673px** (crossbow+katana s102 t4588), both flagged visible through the 3px ink, with bodies at **exactly 2R (zero overlap)** and 45/169px of wall clearance and a 9-contact push at 90% alignment — so it is **not** a body pin and not chain-specific (the crossbow+katana pair is rigid). The real mechanism: translation-only projection on crossed ~205px-reach blades has a **fixed point ≈10px deep** that more passes do not improve, and the loop's only rotational DOF is the nudge, capped `nudgePx/REACH` (0.0588 rad) and gated one per tick per fighter, while the contact torque it accumulates (sim :1697-98) is reduced to `Math.sign` (:1713-14). The resolver rework is **deferred until after length growth**, which changes every reach and therefore every fixed point — see the Measured-facts entry of that date, judged since 2026-10-28 on the BAND class alone — the named edge-leading MIRROR class (a parry flipping a flagged weapon in the tick's last substep: sim truth, proven per occurrence, measured never drawn) is disclosed separately and does not widen the band; the dormant parts-class counters and report lines were trimmed from the harness 2026-10-19. Substep pre-resolution overlap is resolved in-substep and never rendered. Acceptance & calibration history: archive (law 3's entry) — mutation-tested with the resolver disabled at 22.83px worst, so the check has teeth.
4. **One damage per touch.** The first touch deals damage once; while the weapon stays in contact, no further damage; it becomes eligible again only after the blade clears the body by the re-arm margin (12px). Blades pass through bodies — solid to each other only (rule 3) — and that pass-through is what makes "still touching" and "leaves the body" well-defined. *Status: law since v0.5, proven at every full baseline* — the exact flag replica over every substep pose has never mismatched; re-arms only on real separation. **The projectile-rule exception (owner 2026-10-19):** a projectile weapon's spine touching the body still latches and shoves (same re-arm + knock — solidity survives), but deals no damage and emits no hit event, so the replica pairs only damaging edges — the replica mirrors the sim's exact gate (`shot || tangential`; the tangential half now covers only the shield). Law 7s proves no projectile weapon ever emits a melee hit. For shots the law is structural: a shot is consumed by the body it hits, so it can never double-damage — the audit instead proves *accounting* (every shot has exactly one proven departure; see Projectile weapons). **The sanctioned re-arm-side extension — DEEP-DISENGAGE GATE (owner option 1, 2026-10-23: \"do 1\"):** after a blade latch fires, re-arm needs not only the 12px `reArmPad` clearance but ALSO either a genuine disengagement (peak clearance since the latch ≥ `reArmDeep` 85px) or `reArmWait` 90 ticks out. Contacts inside the gate stay fully solid (the resolver is untouched — the gate events-only, the same division of labour as law 2's flutter gate): the latch holds, no fresh hit or knock fires, and a rocked blade pokes at ≥0.75s cadence instead of machine-gunning. Born from the owner's seed-24267 playtest — Heavy's javelin left and re-entered Speedy's body 3× in 95 ticks (44/51-tick intervals, five legal parries interleaved): \"the weapon going back and forth damaging speedy multiple times as the weapon leaves and reenters speedys body, this looks very ugly\". The gate rides the latch, so knock-only contacts (projectile spines, the shield) are gated identically; a buried blade can never re-arm (the pad clause fails), so \"one touch, one damage\" stays structural. The trigger sentence therefore reads: every fresh exact touch **after the blade cleared the body AND either disengaged to ≥85px peak clearance or stayed out ≥90 ticks** is one hit. measure.mjs's law-4 replica mirrors the gate exactly, the substep order included (peak updates before the check). Calibration history and the population proofs live in **Measured facts**.
5. **Parry recoils both weapons.** On a parry, both spin directions flip. With rule 6 this keeps the pair counter-rotating for the whole round: growth preserves sign, and zero-spin cannot occur (ω starts at ±weapon ω and only grows in magnitude). Future weapon traits may carry explicit exceptions to the recoil (owner's caveat) — any exception must be a named, documented weapon trait, readable where the weapon is taught (reveal poster / legend). **The standing exception: the swat** (a blade touching a shot destroys the shot and flips nothing) — named, documented, and taught on the reveal poster's trait lines (`blades swat arrows` etc.) and legend (`+SPD / Hit · arrow / rev`); on screen the swat reads as the shot popping out of existence (its contact ghost retired with all ghosts at step1d). *Status: enforced and proven at every full baseline since v0.7.2* — the audit tracks signed spins across every substep: every parry flipped both weapons, **0 unexplained sign changes anywhere** (swats included — the sign law is what proves they flip nothing), 0 zero-spins, so counter-rotation holds from bell to K.O.
6. **Counter-rotation from the bell.** Every round starts with the two weapons spinning in opposite directions (one clockwise, one counter-clockwise): a single seeded roll decides fighter 0, fighter 1 always gets the opposite. **Rolled, not fixed per cast** (owner decision, 2026-10-16) — a per-cast table would have to be kept even by hand and breaks the moment mirror duels or a large roster arrive. Counter-rotating blades mesh like gears at the contact point, so the double recoil reads as a clean clang, and the two silhouettes stay distinguishable mid-fight. *Status: enforced and proven at every full baseline since v0.7.2* — 0 same-direction openings ever, all openings at exact base-ω magnitudes (a uniform 3.5 across the roster). Before the law: 28/48 rounds opened same-direction.

Owner's words (2026-10-16): "weapons can never tunnel through each other or overlap ever … they should treat each other as solids" · "damage only happens once when a weapon first touches the opponent, as long as the weapon still touches, no additional damage is done, first when the weapon leaves the body, it is eligible to do damage again" · "when weapons collide in a parry, i want the weapons to rotate the opposite direction (there might be exceptions due to special weapon skills)" · "the bots should rotate their weapons in opposite direction of their opponents when game starts (so one rotating clockwise and the other counterclockwise)" · on the opening direction: "i think it should be rolled, imagine in the future a roster of 50 bots, it annoying to keep track of to keep it even" (2026-10-16).

Recorded caveat (so it is never "fixed" as a bug later): in an extreme pin — a body crushed against the wall by the other's blade — the resolver may not fully separate within a single substep; it iterates and pushes out over the next few. The audit's acceptance line for rendered-tick interpenetration: the disclosed ≤6px / ≤2%-of-fight-ticks pin band, any residual transient and attributable. **Second disclosed class, the edge-leading MIRROR frame (2026-10-28):** a parry flips both spins at the END of `bladeVsBlade`, and `leadFlip` mirrors a flagged weapon's covering instantly while the bodies stay put — so a parry landing in the tick's LAST substep hands the rendered frame a mirrored interpenetration no resolver pass ever saw. It is sim truth and the audits are specified to read truth (`CFG.flipMs`, canonical :129: the art sweeps old→edge-on→mirrored over 180ms "while the sim's hitbox is already at its new (true) pose … the sim/laws/audits never see it"), and it is never drawn: `.qa/chainlaw.mjs` Part F replicates the sweep and measures a worst DRAWN gap of −0.149px, sub-ink. Membership is proven per occurrence (a parry at the previous tick's last substep + a flagged weapon + the same body pose clearing the ink under the pre-flip mirror); anything unexplained stays in the band and still fails it. Full attribution in Measured facts (the `chainlaw` entry). **Whip amendment history, compressed (2026-10-21):** a drained-rotation "whip" escape was built and REVERTED the same day (the drain re-buried blades at the resolver's push-out rate and glued pinned pairs — owner: "now they are both stuck together … never separating"; the band metric at the time measured overlap DEPTH but not episode DURATION, where the glue lived). The adopted cure (owner go): the escape nudge stays instant but is capped at `nudgePx` 12px-at-the-head and gated to one add per tick per fighter (each escape spreads over ~3–12 ticks at ≤6px-head per drawn frame), paired with `parryPush` 525 → 700 so grapples end before wedges form. Measured at birth: visible overlaps 0, worst depth 0.81px, max run 27 ticks, glued battles 0/20; f.spin untouched, so law 5's sign audit stays clean by construction. Law 3's acceptance therefore reports two classes (measure.mjs since 2026-10-21): **visible** overlaps (>2.5px, through the 3px ink outline) keep the old line (<6px worst, ≤2% of fight-ticks); **sub-ink** overlaps (≤2.5px — on screen the inks cross, which is the contact itself) are a disclosed transient class. Unadopted candidates on record: renderer-side angle chase (a disclosed drawn-lag exception to drawn-equals-hitbox), and a resolver that stalls less (engineering-heavy). Full numbers in **Measured facts** (whip and separation entries).

**Chaos ceiling + wall bleed (2026-10-21, owner option 1 from a measured menu — owner wish: "i feel like i want to see more but not exaggerating scenarios where bots touch the upper rim … too much of it is boring"; sim-data change, fingerprints re-baselined).** A cap-1600 experiment earlier the same day was playtest-rejected ("its going a bit too fast for the eye to understand now": 40 top touches/battle, pace p50 1053, 45% of ticks above the old cap) and reverted byte-identically; the revert scan corrected the design premise — top-rim touches were never impossible at cap 1100 (a full-cap upward launch from mid-arena rises 432px to y≈68): the approved baseline yields **0.95/battle**, the owner's "very rarely" as literal truth. The wish was therefore "≈1/battle → a few, at approved pace", and every candidate was measured (20 seeds, throwaway CFG overrides): cap 1200 elastic 3.9/battle p50 807 · cap 1250+wr0.85 4.45 p50 774 · cap 1250 elastic 6.75 p50 843 · cap 1300 elastic 8.2 p50 877 · cap 1600+wr 0.85/0.7/0.5 ~10/battle p50 773–867. Adopted: **maxSpeed 1100 → 1200 (the chaos ceiling) + wallRest 1 → 0.9** — walls absorb 10% of the normal component per contact, the bleed that keeps the horizontal ratchet from filling the new headroom; the trampoline floor stays the only energy source (doctrine amendment in the CFG comment). Birth certificate on the shipped file: top-rim touches **3.05/battle** (per-battle spread 0–9 — clean battles stay clean, show battles get the show), pace p50 743 → **766 (+3%)**, p90 **1098 ≈ the old cap** (the fast tail reads as yesterday's maximum), ticks above 1100 **9.9%** (was 0.8%). Delta proof (`.qa/fp-pre-top.json` → `.qa/fp-post-top.json`): 0/8 identical (ceiling and walls touch every timeline); boomerang+shuriken s1 18000→12273 terminated, several cells drifted, four hold caps. Suite: chromecheck PASS · killstill ALL PASS (22 kills, gContact 22/22) · hitstill ALL PASS (413/413) · reveal-probe ALL PASS · projectile-fix PROBE PASS (C 0/243,965, D informational 4/18). `.qa/fp-after.json` re-baselined := post; pre at `.qa/fp-pre-top.json`, post at `.qa/fp-post-top.json`. `measure.mjs` NOT run (owner-go rule). Parked escalation levers for the balance pass: damage-scaled knock (the snowball-feeds-the-legs proposal) and spin-scaled parry shove — both now bound by this session's pace acceptance line: any pace change keeps p50 within a few percent of 743 and the >1100 tick share in the low double digits at most (owner verdict: "too fast for the eye to understand").

**Margin band (2026-10-21, owner order from seed 80117 — "i want the weapons to be visible at all times"; renderer-only, fingerprints byte-identical to `.qa/fp-after.json`).** The preview canvas now renders the sim square inside a 150-unit margin (transform offsets in buildArena/draw; the reveal's shockwave clip extended by the same band), so weapon ink past the square — worst case ~146 units (long radial tip, body at the rim, axis-pointing; the cut stub on Heavy's spear in the owner's screenshot, where Speedy's tip survived only by angle luck) — draws into the band instead of cutting at the canvas edge. Suite: chromecheck PASS · reveal-probe ALL PASS with zero edits · killstill ALL PASS (22/22) · hitstill ALL PASS (8/8 latch checks) · fingerprint byte-identical. Accepted cost, disclosed: the arena disc reads ~21% smaller on a fixed window (cssPx cap and window formula unchanged); the engine-era frame carries margins by design, and contract check 6's ink-in-margin clause joins open question 9 with this preview as precedent. **Reverted same day at owner word ("revert it back to before for now") — the zoom-out and rim band read wrong ("i think something is off"); then RESOLVED same day in final form: the overflow band canvas (second canvas, same scale, ring-clipped, weapon pass drawn twice) — weapons visible at all times with zero zoom, renderer-only, fingerprints byte-identical, suite green (chromecheck · reveal-probe zero edits · killstill 22/22 · hitstill 8/8). Extreme top/bottom tips draw over the scorebug/legend rows (disclosed spectacle); the cut blemish is closed.** The two-canvas band was then replaced same day by a **single band-sized canvas** (one backing store, one pixel grid): the two-canvas joint drew as a line across the ink at their fractional CSS offset (owner screenshot: "you can see the line"); disc scale and layout unchanged, fingerprints byte-identical again, suite green again.

**K.O. card removal (2026-10-21, owner word: "i dont want it to be announced"; renderer-only, fingerprints byte-identical to `.qa/fp-after.json`).** `drawKoCard` and its call deleted; the round's end now reads as the kill-still freeze, the faded death-mark disc and the scorebug tick, then the next reveal — the `koTicks` beat stays (pacing, not announcement), as does the battle-winner scrim at match end. chromecheck's staged-1–1-tie card guard and its recording ctx retired with the card; suite green post-removal (chromecheck · reveal-probe zero edits · killstill 22/22 · hitstill 8/8). SPEC's paper-theme line and the locked-frame record amended: type-on-floor remains the channel rule for banners and future in-arena moments; the K.O. announcement is gone from the reference frame. **Reverted same day at owner word ("revert it back to before for now") — the zoom-out and the rim band read wrong to his eye; the cut blemish and the candidate cures return to open status, discussion pending.**

**Pose amendment (v0.8.2, owner-approved with the render-interpolation change):** the renderer draws poses interpolated between consecutive tick poses; the laws are audited at exact tick poses. In audit language (law 3 here, law 3s in the Projectile laws, `measure.mjs`), "rendered frame" / "tick-entry pose" means the tick pose — the sim's ground truth. The interpolated draw can transiently show sub-tick in-between geometry between two clean tick poses (≤ ~0.25 rad of weapon sweep per 120 Hz tick); this is inherent to interpolation, imperceptible at these rates, and never a law violation.

### Uniform base stats (owner decision, 2026-10-16 — BUILT in v0.7)

Owner: "i think all weapons should have the same default spin speed and damage. The spin speed/damage only increases from on hit." Every weapon on the roster shares one base spin and one base damage; the only thing that moves either number is on-hit growth (fighter skill + weapon trait — both fire exclusively on damaging hits, so nothing else in the sim needs rewiring). Stats reset every round, so each round opens with both fighters at identical numbers spinning in opposite directions; divergence is purely earned, inside the round.

- **The uniform base (assistant's numbers, owner delegated the pick):** ω **3.5** rad/s, damage **1** — damage was 5 from v0.7 through `pause-r20`; the balance pass opened 2026-10-28 with the owner's ruling "dmg base for all is 1, and hp 100 stays the same" (`dmg1-r21`), explicitly a TEST of long uncapped-escalation games ("the game becomes more and more ridiculous with no cap to scaling, and then it end in pure chaos … this is just a thought we can always change it"). hp 100, ω 3.5 and every growth increment (0.7 / 0.9 / 1.6) were deliberately left untouched, so the escalation curve steepened without any other number moving. Measured consequences — rounds 1.16× longer, launcher mirrors no longer terminating, maxSpin 23.5 → 103.5 — are in the `dmg1-r21` entry in **Measured facts**; the round-ending rule (open questions 1 and 5) is now the blocking decision (**superseded later the same day, 2026-10-28: the owner ruled the non-termination deliberate until skills land — see open question 1 — so no round ending and no pressure ramp is being built, and these measurements stand as the frozen pre-skills baseline**).
- **Growth identity stays per-weapon** — but see the roster table's ⚠ note: the dagger's axis changed during the undocumented era (+SPD → +DMG), and the owner ruled 2026-10-28 that the skills pass decides every axis, so that ⚠ was a deliberate deferral — **discharged for the dagger the same day: +0.7 DMG ratified, Heavy synergy, no sim change**; the v1.0 batch copied traits wholesale from the existing classes ("melee = axe, ranged = bow; balance later"). Growth identity is therefore *provisional* now, not locked.
- **What differentiates a weapon:** length, drawn shape, hold, growth axis, and special effects (shot behaviour) — not base numbers.
- History: the v0.7 re-baseline consequences (buzzsaw vanished, heavy lean grew) and the v0.7 build record live in the archive. **[Amended 2026-10-28:]** "buzzsaw vanished" was a damage-5 fact — at base damage 1 (`dmg1-r21` onward) natscan reads maxSpin **103.5 rad/s on 8 of 30 naturals**, so the ≥25 rad/s band is crossed routinely and the buzzsaw is back. Open question 1's symptom list carries the current numbers; the ceiling's remaining headroom is measured in the skills-prep cost map.

### Fighter skill vs weapon trait — the model (owner ruling 2026-10-28: **M2**, two languages — **the first special skill BUILT as `skill-r29` and RETIRED at `nocombo-r34`**; see NO COMBOS below)

**The ruling.** A weapon's **trait** (the row field was `ability` until the 2026-10-28 rename below) is *data* — a growth rate on the shared axes, read at the one site where growth happens (`grow()`). A fighter's **skill** is a **hook** in `CONTRACT.md`'s sense: it reads the player and a small context and returns a modified value, never mutates state, never depends on another player's hook, **modifies an existing mechanic and never introduces one**, may consume the game's seeded RNG but never create or seed one, and leaves anything time-dependent to the game's own per-tick logic. One skill per player per game, expressed through one hook or several; identity stays global, skill stays local.

**Adopted lazily — this ruling changes no code.** CONTRACT's optionality rule (where a game calls a hook a player doesn't implement, the base value passes through unchanged) means the two shipped numeric skills — `Speedy {spin: 0.30}`, `Heavy {dmg: 0.9}` — stay legal exactly as they are, and the hook indirection is introduced by the **first non-numeric skill**, not before. Nothing is threaded speculatively; the canonical is untouched by this ruling (md5 `86ff2d5bffc6a3942e6c9eba12ccef09`, build `sigil-r23`).

**The consequence that shapes the pass.** Because a hook may not introduce a mechanic, anything a skill is meant to touch must exist in the *game* first: poison, frost, comet burn, damage-over-time and runtime length growth are **game-layer mechanics**, and only then can a skill modify them. The pass has two layers — **(1) authorized hook targets** (game mechanics, each owing its own probe) and **(2) the hooks themselves**.

**Why M2 and not M1 — the measured state of the one-language build.** `grow()` is the *only* sim consumer of `.skill` and `.trait`. Traits are binary across all 22 weapons (12 × `{spin: 0, dmg: 0.7}`, 10 × `{spin: 0.70, dmg: 0}`), so the unit of differentiation today is the *(fighter × weapon)* pair resolving to one of four growth vectors — the measured ω 103.5 maximum is exactly the Speedy × spin-weapon cell (+1.00/hit × 100 hits). The display hard-codes that binary: `SKILL_SHORT = f.skill.spin ? '+SPD' : '+DMG'`, so a fighter with both axes, neither, or a non-numeric skill is **silently mislabelled "+DMG / Hit"**; the ridiculousness trackers are exactly two (`G.maxSpin`, `G.maxDmg`) against a fixed three-channel SPD-DMG legend; and a shot inherits exactly one per-fighter quantity at birth (`dmg: f.dmg`), with `r`/`speed`/`len`/`turn`/`bounce` all reading the weapon row — which is what makes per-fighter shot axes the cheap spectacle (one line each at the birth site).

**Cost of any new axis** (the budget rule, from the skills-prep cost map): a fighter field plus its per-round reset · the consumer site that reads it at hit or launch time · the legend/poster, where a fourth channel is a layout change · a `maxDmg`/`maxSpin`-class tracker · the fingerprint's hashed fields · **and its own probe** — the facing pass's vacuous-unextended-run trap is the precedent: a harness that passes without covering the new flag proves nothing.

**Closed alongside it (owner pick 2026-10-28): parry rewards nothing, full stop — and no skill may reward a parry either.** That removes spin-per-parry, which the spin-ceiling measurement named the likeliest way to wake the dormant `omegaCap: 140`, and leaves **damaging hit, time and launch** as the only growth triggers. Time and launch are contextual, so either one is the first thing that forces a real hook rather than a number.

**Settled under M2 — the five rulings (owner, 2026-10-28).** **(1) Stacking: it adds.** A fighter's skill and the weapon's trait stack additively on the shared axes, exactly as `grow()` already does (`sk.spin + ab.spin`, `sk.dmg + ab.dmg`), so the numeric site is unchanged by the model ruling; a hook that modifies a *different* mechanic adds to the growth the weapon contributes rather than replacing it. **(2) Display: shown in the legend, as a short authored label.** The legend already carries a skill cell per fighter (the `class="ex"` cell between the name and the weapon columns), so this is an **authoring change, not a layout change**: `SKILL_SHORT`'s binary derivation (`f.skill.spin ? '+SPD' : '+DMG'`) is replaced by a per-fighter few-word line in the style of the existing poster prose (`line: 'Spins faster each hit'`) — the owner's example for a spear: **"gets longer"**. Two consequences: the silent-mislabel defect dies with the derivation (a both-axes / neither / non-numeric skill can no longer read "+DMG / Hit"), and the weapon column keeps its own `brief` + `line`. The SPD/DMG numeric cell is untouched — **a new *numeric* axis is still a layout change; a prose label is free.** **(3) Authorized hook targets: yes — length growth, per-fighter shot multipliers and knock.** Each is game-layer work first (a hook may not introduce a mechanic) and each owes its own probe. Length growth stays the expensive one: `W.len` is consumed at boot to build SPAN, REACH, the scaled polys and the baked covering circles, so runtime growth threads a per-fighter length scale through `spineFrame`, `bladeCircles` and the renderer, invalidates REACH (the parryGap prune bound) and MINR (the adaptive-substep budget), and cannot be caught by the boot LEN_MIN/LT gate — **the ceiling has to live inside the growth rule**. Shot multipliers are the cheap ones (`r`/`speed`/`len`/`turn`/`bounce` snapshot at birth, one line each, `dmg: f.dmg` as the precedent); knock is one hit-time read of a per-fighter field (today a global `CFG.hitKnock` 130, read at exactly two sites — `bladeVsBody` and `shotHitBody` — which makes it the **only cheap axis live on all 22 weapons** rather than on one class: the launch-snapshot axes are inert on the 12 melee weapons and runtime length growth is inert on the 10 projectile ones). *[Re-keyed by the axis-placement ruling later the same day: length growth and the shot multipliers are **weapon-row data**, not fighter hook targets — knock is the fighter-side universal axis. See **Axis placement** below.]* **(4) The spin cap: leave it.** `omegaCap: 140` stays silent and dormant (36.5 hits of headroom at +1.00/hit; stability limit ω ≈ 258 at the worst pair), revisited only if a spin-flavoured skill actually threatens to reach it — no sim change, no kept pair, no re-measurement. **(5) Elemental effects: yes, into the game layer — design deferred.** Poison, frost and comet/fire are authorized as **game mechanics**, which is what M2 requires before any skill can modify them; the owner's word on their shapes is "we will figer it out later". The art already pre-figures them: KF1/KF2 fire comet · KP1/KP2 poison · KN1 frost · KV1/KV2 fire-orange.

**NO COMBOS (owner ruling 2026-10-31, codified at `nocombo-r34`).** A skill and a trait may stack **only by addition on a shared axis**; neither may reach into the other's mechanic. There are **no unique fighter×weapon combinations**: one bot's weapon may never behave differently from anyone else's, and every skill must be **universal** (live on all 22 weapons) or **contextual in a class-independent way**, so a bot's identity survives the dice roll by construction. The ruling retired the one violation — `skill.lenSteps`, the `skill-r29` first special skill, which added ladder steps to the spear's cap for a single carrier. Owner, on the mechanic being floated again: *"if i ever did that, its a mistake on my side."* Every cap is therefore uniform (`LEN_STEPS` 12), and the rule lives where a builder will read it: the canonical's TRAIT/SKILL REGISTRY block (the FIGHTERS comment and the WHAT DOES NOT EXIST bullet). Same ruling, the shape of the rest of the pass: **weapon-trait mechanics first (20 remain), then the bot cast — every bot a unique skill — then balance**; skills and traits may add up where they share an axis. One lesson from the retired carrier survives as live doctrine: **damage-coupled growth is self-limiting**, because the holder's own damage sets the hit budget the growth has to fit inside.

**Axis placement (owner ruling 2026-10-28: "design it out").** An axis whose effect depends on the weapon **class** is carried as **weapon-row data** — a rate, exactly like `trait: {spin, dmg}` — and consumed by a game-layer mechanic: the launch-snapshot axes (`r`/`speed`/`len`/`turn`/`bounce`, live on the 10 projectile weapons) and runtime length growth (live on the 12 melee ones). A **fighter's skill** takes only what is live on all 22 weapons — spin growth, damage growth, knock (`CFG.hitKnock`, whose two consumers are `bladeVsBody` and `shotHitBody`) — or a contextual trigger that does not depend on weapon class — the class-independent contextual triggers are per-time and conditional-on-state only — **per-launch is class-dependent** (only the 10 projectile weapons ever launch), so a launch-triggered effect belongs on the weapon row, not on a fighter. **Why:** weapons are dice-rolled each round, so a fighter's identity has to survive the roll; a class-dependent fighter skill flickers (live on 12/22 or 10/22), while a weapon-keyed rate never does, because its line is only ever read while that weapon is held. **Consequence for display: no dark label can exist**, so the legend needs no conditional or dimmed state and this ruling owes **no chrome work** — the skill cell becomes the authored few-word line of ruling 2 and is always true, and the class-dependent flavour rides in the weapon's own `line`/`brief`. **M2 stays intact:** a rate on the weapon row is still *data*, the mechanic consuming it is still game-layer, and only the fighter's skill is a hook. **What it re-keys:** the length-growth spectacle ("gets longer") belongs to the **spear** — and to any other melee weapon given a length rate — not to a fighter; either fighter gets it whenever they hold that weapon, and it is still built in this pass. **Rejected with reasons** (so the fork is not re-litigated): conditional label wording ("longer reaches when melee") — free, but the label sits dark on half the rolls and the audience cannot tell why nothing happened; dimming the label while dark — cheap and idiomatic (the legend already carries `class="out"` for a dead fighter and `wp idle` + "rolling…" pre-reveal, it re-renders per frame and already resolves the held weapon, and chromecheck asserts legend *structure* only — `ccard`, `table`, `qn` — so it would cost one CSS rule, a re-run and the same treatment on the VS poster), but unnecessary once no dark label can exist. Both remain available if a specific skill is ever judged worth the flicker.

**The cast (owner ruling 2026-10-28): two generic bots, and the numeric tier is complete at two.** The cast stays at Speedy (`skill: {spin: 0.30}`) and Heavy (`skill: {dmg: 0.9}`) for now, and they are not placeholders — they are the **generic tier**: the two most legible skills possible, and SPEC’s naming rule satisfied outright ("Heavy" tells a viewer what to expect before the fight starts). Each owns one of the two universal numeric growth axes, so between them they cover the whole numeric space; there is no third generic skill left to write, which is why every later bot is **special/unique by necessity rather than by taste**. **That tier is what pays for the hook interface:** under M2 a numeric skill is *data* and needs no code, while a contextual skill (per-time, conditional-on-state, knock, length growth) is the first thing that requires hooks to exist — so **the hook machinery is not built until a special skill exists to hang on it.** **Parked with its analysis attached — knock is the cheapest available third axis.** `CFG.hitKnock: 130` has exactly two read sites, `bladeVsBody` (melee) and `shotHitBody` (projectile), so a per-fighter knock multiplier is live on all 22 weapons and satisfies the axis-placement ruling with no dark label.
The shelved shape was Heavy dealing more ("shoves harder") and Speedy taking less ("stays put"); both directions share one mechanic, one consumer pair and one probe, so the pair costs roughly one axis. When taken up it is a **sim change**: kept pair, fingerprint re-baseline, and a bound measured against `maxSpeed` 1200 / `subMargin` 1200 **before** any number lands. **Two things to watch, recorded so they are not discovered late.** (a) *A legibility asymmetry:* Speedy’s skill is visible — the weapon genuinely spins faster and compounds into something you can see — while Heavy’s currently reads as a climbing DMG figure and a shorter round, which is the weaker signal in a muted video on a phone. If the two ever feel unequal on screen, the fix is a visible expression of damage, not a bigger number. (b) *Do not tune +0.30 against +0.9 by eye:* whether they are equal in value is empirical — spin growth is bounded by the dormant `omegaCap` and buys spectacle plus hit frequency, while damage growth shortens a 100 hp round and is front-loaded — and it is owed to the sanctioned `measure.mjs` run, not to a judgement call.
**[PAID 2026-10-28 — the sanctioned run landed.]** Measured like-for-like, weapon class held constant, shield cells excluded: Heavy wins **65.6%** of melee mirrors (Speedy 34.4%; 108 cells, 2,073 rounds) and **75.1%** of projectile mirrors (Speedy 24.9%; 86 cells, 1,125 rounds). So they are not equal in value — and not parallel axes either. Damage reaches both offence paths (blades `dealt = a.dmg`; shots carry the thrower's `f.dmg` snapshotted at launch) while spin reaches neither, and since the 10 projectile weapons are exactly the 10 spin-trait weapons, Speedy holding one has `dmgGain = 0` and its shots do base damage for the whole battle. **No number moved as a result**; this is the evidence a future tuning decision would use, not a ruling. **Synergy grid, for the record:** 12 weapons carry `{spin: 0, dmg: 0.7}` and 10 carry `{spin: 0.70, dmg: 0}`, so each fighter has synergy weapons and cross weapons — four combinations, of which Speedy × spin-trait (+1.00/hit) is the cell that produced the measured ω 103.5 maximum. The dagger is the one row flagged ⚠ for the skills pass, and under this cast its axis question is concrete: +0.7 DMG makes it a Heavy-synergy weapon (1.6/hit together), +0.7 SPIN makes it a Speedy-synergy weapon (1.00/hit) and moves the roster split from 12/10 to 11/11.

**Owner ruling 2026-10-28 (post-`lengrow-r28`) — NO BALANCING DURING THE SKILLS PASS, and the three balance-shaped debts are deferred to its end.** Owner, after playtesting the triple-length spear: *"it looks good now, i think we should hold back on re run, all the stats you keep delivering about other weapons etc, i know its unbalanced etc, we are not trying to balance the game at all right now, we are making the skills/mechanics, a new balancing will come afterwards."* This is a standing rule change, not a one-off, and it binds the implementer's reporting as well as the work. **Deferred to the end of the skills pass, all three, by explicit owner confirmation:** (1) the **484-cell re-run** — it would snapshot win rates the first special skill immediately invalidates, and it costs ~7.16 h of the owner's machine to buy a number that gets thrown away; (2) the **rotational-DOF resolver rework** — deferred so it is designed against *final* geometry rather than redone when skills change reach or knock; law 3 stays **RED and disclosed** in the meantime, and the interim rule is that **nothing in the skills pass may depend on the resolver being correct**; (3) the **spear-bearing fingerprint cell** — deferred because the skills pass will move hashes anyway, so the pass should end with **one** new baseline rather than two. **Reporting rule that goes with it:** stop delivering per-weapon win rates, holder-win% comparisons and damage-vs-growth re-measurements; a Measured-facts entry now records **mechanics** — what the code does, what it costs, what is proven — and not a balance table. The one sweep that stays legitimate is the *"does this mechanic actually work"* differential (as `lengrow-r28`'s 50-cell run caught that +35% had shipped a crippled spear), reported as works / does-not-work rather than as a 22-weapon ranking. What is **not** deferred, because it is correctness rather than balance: the house gate, the build proofs and the `LEN_REMAP_CEIL` boot guard · `dmg` 0.3 staying marked **provisional** · the law-3 disclosure · the two blind axes in the fingerprint suite remaining *named* even though the cell that closes one is deferred.

**Owner ruling 2026-10-28 — WEAPON TRAITS RESET EVERY ROUND. The round is a hard reset boundary and no skill may carry trait growth across it.** Owner, rejecting a proposed first special skill whose entire mechanic was keeping the spear's accumulated length between rounds: *"i dont like this at all, each round weapons traits should reset."* This **ratifies shipped behaviour rather than changing it** — `rollWeapons` already zeroes all three growth axes at every round start (`f.spin = 0; f.dmg = W.dmg; f.lenStep = 0`, sim :1323-1325), so no code moved and no build stamp bumped. Its force is as a **constraint on every future skill**: cross-round persistence of a weapon trait is now forbidden outright, not merely unimplemented. The length ladder's top step remains the *geometry* ceiling and the round reset remains the *balance* bound — and the second of those is now an owner law rather than an implementation accident. A special skill must therefore express itself **within a round**: a higher ceiling, a faster rate, a different trigger, or a different effect. Never a carried-over state. Recorded here so the rejected shape is not re-proposed: it was cheap (~6 lines, zero new baked geometry, and the "retained step on a weapon with no ladder" hazard was already covered by the `ls ? LEN_LADDER[wId] : null` guards at sim :1117 and :2270) and it was still wrong, because it broke the round boundary. Separately measured while designing it, and worth keeping: for a carrier to be seen starting a round with retained length, he must draw the spear in **two different rounds of the same match** — about **0.6%** of matches at a uniform 1-of-22 roll. Any weapon-trait-hooked skill is roll-conditional at ~4.5% of rounds; that is a property of the random roster, not of this design, and every future special inherits it.

### Length growth — the spear's trait (owner ruling 2026-10-28, **BUILT as `lengrow-r26`, raised to +35% as `lengrow-r27`, then to +200% as `lengrow-r28` — all the same day** — rigid head, junction at 69.23, per-step RE-BAKED coverings)

**The proposal, and why it is a confirmation rather than a new direction.** Owner, 2026-10-28: *"ok, so i was thinking the spear should grow longer each time it hits an opponent, what do you think"*. That is exactly what the **axis-placement ruling** above already re-keyed: *"the length-growth spectacle ("gets longer") belongs to the **spear** — and to any other melee weapon given a length rate — not to a fighter; either fighter gets it whenever they hold that weapon, and it is still built in this pass."* So no doctrine moves. What this entry adds is the shape the earlier ruling left open: the rate, the ladder, the growth geometry, and the measured ceiling that bounds all three.

**Rate and ladder (owner pick 2026-10-28 via ask_user: "1.2% per hit, 12 steps").** **~1.2% per damaging hit, 12 discrete steps.** The sizing came from the baseline rather than from taste: 236,639 hits over 7,891 decided rounds is ~28.5 hits per round, so **~14 damaging hits per fighter per round**, and 1.012^12 = **+15.4%** — which lands inside the measured covering ceiling below with 3 points of margin. Reach goes 205.9 → **~228px** (61.44px radial grip + 144.5px covering extent × 1.154).
