Roughly one visible step per clean hit, topping out near the end of a long round. **Amended 2026-10-28, the same day, on the owner's *"i want it to be more ridiculous, so increase the growth of the spear even more"*: the rate is now `len: 0.0253` — 2.5% per damaging hit, 12 steps to **+34.96%** — build `lengrow-r27`.** The sizing logic above holds and was re-run rather than assumed: ~14 damaging hits per fighter per round still means a 12-step ladder tops out near the end of a long round, and +35% still lands inside the covering ceiling, but the ceiling is **not** the +18.6% in the table below — that measured a *uniform* stretch. Swept on the rigid map that actually shipped, with the covering held, MISS binds at **tip +39.5%** (shaft k 1.6469, miss 4.59 of the 4.60 band) and fails at +40% (4.61); POKE never binds and `minR` never moves. So +35% carries **4.5 points of margin in rate terms and 0.10px at the binding step**. Reach goes 210.412 → **261.971px** (measured, not estimated as ~228px above), i.e. **+24.5%** over base rather than the +10.8% of `lengrow-r26`. Past ~+40% the covering has to follow the growth — densify per step at boot, or re-bake the twelve rows through `inscribe.mjs --regen` — and neither was needed for this raise. **Amended again 2026-10-28 (`lengrow-r28`): the rate is now `len: 0.0959` — 9.6% per damaging hit, 12 steps to +200.1%, so the spear ends a round THREE times its shipped length.** Owner, on being shown +35%: *"its not ridiculous enough"*. Drawn tip 147.472 → **442.548px**, `REACH.spear` 210.412 → **505.488** (2.40×, so from mid-arena in the 1000px box its tip reaches the wall), top shaft factor **4.2769**. This is the raise that forced the covering to follow the growth — +35% was the last step a `d`-remapped covering could take — so all twelve steps now carry their own **re-baked** rows (`LEN_CIRCLES`, 22→50 circles per step, generated by `.qa/lengrow-bake.mjs`, never hand-edited). `LEN_STEPS` stays 12: at 9.6% a click is ~14px of visible spear, and with ~14 damaging hits per fighter per round the ladder still tops out near the round's end. **Measured on the same 50 forced-spear cells, +200% turned out to be a balance fix and not only a spectacle:** at +35% the spear won **3 of 50** battles (6%); at +200% it wins **33 of 50** (66%), close to the 65.9% holder win rate the 484-cell baseline recorded for the spear at its OLD `dmg` 0.7. The `dmg` 0.7 → 0.3 cut and the growth rate are therefore **coupled**, and +35% had left the spear the weakest weapon in the game. Undecided-at-budget went **down**, 36% → 28%, so the longer spear relieves stalls rather than causing them.

**No `lenCap` constant is introduced — the round IS the cap.** Owner, 2026-10-28: *"no cap let it snowball, we will balance laer if it gets too ridiculous"*. That is safe here for a structural reason: **stats reset every round** (the "fresh snowball" ruling), so growth can never compound across a battle, and a round delivers only ~14 growth events. Stalled rounds are self-limiting too — growth requires a damaging hit, and a stall has few. The 12th step is therefore a **technical** ceiling set by the hitbox, not a gameplay cap; the later balancing conversation is about the *rate*, not the ceiling. Note this is a genuine snowball unlike the other two axes: length raises the *contact area*, which raises the hit rate, which raises the growth rate — superlinear, where spin and damage growth are linear. That is the reason a bound was worth finding even though none was mandated.

**Length-only, NOT uniform — the owner's stated fear, and the reason the obvious implementation was rejected.** Owner: *"does uniform mean on ll sides? i was actually preferring only the lenght, im scared the spear will become "thick""*. It does: a uniform scale multiplies `d` (along the spine), `l` (across it) and `r` (the circle radius) together, so the spear would visibly fatten as it grew. **Rejected.** The chosen geometry stretches **`d` only, holding `l` and `r`** — the shaft elongates and nothing thickens.

**The measured ceiling on length-only growth.** Stretching `d` alone spreads the baked covering circles apart along the spine while their radii stay fixed, so past a per-weapon limit the union stops covering the drawn silhouette and a body inside the art can fail to take damage — precisely the *"gap hits"* the owner made top priority on 2026-10-19. Computed from each weapon's `CIRCLES_V2` rows (the spine-line half-chord `sqrt(r²−l²)` of each circle, swept for interval contiguity under a `d`-stretch `k`):

| weapon | length-only stretch before the covering splits |
|---|---|
| sword | +53.9% |
| hammer | +28.2% |
| mace | +19.9% |
| **spear** | **+18.6%** (reach 205.9 → 233px) |
| axe | +6.6% |
| katana | **+0.0%** — its spine-line circles are already exactly tangent |

The limit is set by each weapon's **baked circle spacing**, not by design intent, so it differs per row and cannot be assumed. The spear's *shaft* alone tolerates **+84%** (r 8.3 circles on a 9.0 pitch, 7.6px of overlap each); what binds at +18.6% is the **head cluster** (circles #11→#14, whose span is bridged by two purely-lateral barb circles contributing nothing on the spine line). **Caveat, recorded so the number is not over-trusted:** this is a *spine-line* proxy. True 2D coverage of the wide head fails somewhat earlier, so **+18.6% is a ceiling, not a budget** — and the ladder's top step owes a real `ingest.mjs` coverage check before it is baked. At the chosen 12 × 1.2% the design sits at +15.4%, inside even the proxy.

**The trilemma, and how the per-round reset dissolves it.** *Length-only*, *no cap* and *no gap hits* cannot all hold: length-only + no gap hits is bounded; length-only + no cap produces gap hits past ~+18%; no cap + no gap hits requires a uniform scale, i.e. thickening. The escape is that growth resets each round, so "no cap" never actually means unbounded — ~14 hits is the bound, and 1.2% × 14 fits inside the proven limit. All three are had at once. **Amended 2026-10-28 (`lengrow-r27`): the trilemma's shape stands, its numbers move.** *"gap hits past ~+18%"* was measured on a **uniform** `d`-stretch; on the rigid map that shipped, with `l` and every `r` held, the swept bound is **+39.5% tip growth** — roughly double — because the head cluster translates as a rigid body instead of being elongated, so only the shaft's circle spacing has to be bridged. And *"1.2% × 14 fits inside the proven limit"* is now **2.5% × 12 = +35%**, which fits inside +39.5% with 0.10px to spare at the binding step. All three are still had at once; the escape is still the per-round reset, and the ladder top is still the geometry ceiling. **Amended again 2026-10-28 (`lengrow-r28`): the wall was hit, and then passed by baking.** The +39.5% swept bound is real, but it bounds only a **`d`-remapped** covering — radii held while the spacing scales. Re-packing each step against its own stretched silhouette lifts it: at +200% the twelve baked rows gate at poke -0.30px and miss **2.30-4.25px**, *better* than the shipped base row's 4.25, because a fresh pack fits the stretched silhouette instead of inheriting the base spacing. The trilemma's third horn is therefore wrong as stated: "no cap + no gap hits" requires **re-baking**, not thickening. The art still never thickens (only `d` is remapped; no poly point's `l` is ever written), and the per-round reset is still what bounds the snowball.

**Pre-baked ladder, not a runtime stretch.** The canonical's inscribed-covering invariant is *"a uniform scale of an inscribed covering stays inscribed"* (:462-463) — and a `d`-only stretch is **not uniform**, so it cannot inherit that proof. Each step therefore needs its **own** covering, baked by `ingest.mjs` from its own `d`-stretched poly. Precedent exists in the canonical itself: :452-455 already rescales the hammer/axe/spear polys at boot by `LT / maxD(P)` and re-derives their coverings. Two bonuses follow — the art stays *authored* rather than deformed at runtime, and **discrete steps read better on video**: a spear that clicks visibly longer per hit is a beat a viewer can see, where continuous 1.2% is invisible until it suddenly is not. Cost is ~12 extra covering rows for one weapon (19 circles each), a per-fighter step index, and a lookup in `bladeCircles` plus the renderer. **Amended 2026-10-28 (built as `lengrow-r26`): the per-step covering is exactly as specified, but the bake is NOT `ingest.mjs`'s and the map is NOT a uniform `d`-stretch.** The owner's geometry ruling of the same day (ask_user) chose **"rigid head, junction at 69.23"** over the recorded uniform stretch, and the measurement backed the choice:
`.qa/lengrow-gate.mjs` in uniform mode first breaks the house gate at **+6.1%** (miss 5.14px against a 4.6px band), because stretching the head cluster deforms the very barb geometry its covering was packed against; under the rigid map — every point at or behind the junction `J` scales by one factor `k`, every point past it translates by one shared delta `(k-1)·J` — the head moves as a **rigid body**, its protrusion past the last circle stays at the shipped ~3.0px, and **all twelve steps pass**. So the recorded conclusion *"each of the twelve steps must be baked"* is correct for the geometry it measured and **moot for the geometry that shipped**: `LEN_LADDER` is built **at boot** from the already-`:452-455`-normalised polys, inherits the shipped covering, and remaps `d` only — no `ingest.mjs` pass, no authored rows, and `.qa/bake-rows.mjs` is not on this build's path. The two bonuses survive unchanged: the art is never deformed at runtime beyond the ruled map, and a step is still a visible click. `k` is **derived, never authored**: `k_s = 1 + ((1.012)^s - 1)·tip/J`, giving shaft factors 1.0197 → **1.2520** to deliver the owner's 1.2%-on-the-**tip** observable (tip factors 1.0120 → 1.1539, **+15.39%**).
**Amended again 2026-10-28 (`lengrow-r28`): this paragraph's original conclusion is now the shipped design, arrived at by a third route.** The original text called for an `ingest.mjs` bake of authored rows; the `lengrow-r26` amendment retired that in favour of a boot-time `d`-remap that "costs no data". At +200% the remap route is past its ceiling, so per-step coverings ARE baked — but **not** by `ingest.mjs` and **not** as authored `INGR` data. `.qa/lengrow-bake.mjs` stretches the art per step in a scratch temp, runs the sanctioned row-splice baker (`.qa/bake-rows.mjs` → `inscribe.mjs`'s raster-EDT greedy union-inscribed regen — the same packer that baked all 22 shipped rows), and splices the twelve rows into a marked `LEN_CIRCLES` block. So **no new packing algorithm enters the sim**: the ladder reads baked data and maps only the art at boot. That **reverses the r26 amendment's claim that `.qa/bake-rows.mjs` is not on this build's path** — it now is, and it is the only tool that writes to the canonical. `--verify` re-gates every step's own row against its own art (12/12 PASS). The cost is what the original paragraph predicted: ~12 extra covering rows for one weapon, here 421 circles / 11.7 KB.

**Damage lowered alongside it, provisionally.** Owner: *"make it lower, it doesnt really matter, we are also balancing the damage later"*. The spear's `trait: { spin: 0.0, dmg: 0.7 }` becomes **`dmg: 0.3`**, flagged **provisional** — it is not a balance decision, it is a placeholder pending the damage pass. Stacking is unaffected (ruling 1: skill and trait add on the shared axes).

**What it will cost when built** — consistent with the cost map already on record above, restated against today's chosen geometry: a per-fighter `f.lenStep` plus its per-round reset · **`REACH` and `MINR` become per-fighter**, which touches the adaptive-substep budget (:1245-1252, `delta` from `MINR` and `relV` from `REACH`), the `parryGap` prune bound, and the nudge cap `CFG.nudgePx / REACH` (:1724) · `spineFrame`, `bladeCircles` and the renderer · the fingerprint's hashed fields · **and its own probe**, per the vacuous-unextended-run precedent (a harness that passes without covering the new flag proves nothing). The boot `LEN_MIN`/`LT` gate at :663-664 **cannot catch this** — it checks authored `W.len` on the shared table at boot, so the ceiling has to live inside the growth rule, exactly as the earlier cost map said. And `W.len` must never be mutated: it is shared table data, so writing to it would leak across both fighters and across rounds. **Amended 2026-10-28 (built): this cost map is partly superseded by the smaller scope the owner chose.** `f.lenStep` and its per-round reset landed as described (`:1163`, and `:1251` inside `rollWeapons`, which the reveal branch calls at `phaseT === 0`) · `bladeCircles` and the renderer landed (`drawWeapon` gained an optional `polys` with `const PS = polys || W.poly.polys`, `drawWeaponOn` an optional `ls`, and both fight-view call sites pass `f.lenStep`; the reveal poster and the stills stay step 0, which is correct because a fresh fighter *is* step 0) · **`spineFrame` needed no step at all** — it is a radial hold and has no length input. **`REACH` and `MINR` did NOT become per-fighter**: the owner picked the minimal spear-only build with the budget question disclosed rather than engineered away. `REACH.spear` is instead raised **once, at boot, to the top step's value** (210.412 → **233.107**), which is the conservative bound — so the under-budgeting this paragraph feared **does not occur**; what remains is that low steps are slightly **over**-budgeted (a few extra substeps while a spear is alive), and the nudge cap `CFG.nudgePx / REACH` (`:1724`) is 9.7% smaller for the spear. `MINR.spear` needed no change whatsoever: the ladder holds every `l` and `r`, so the thinnest circle is the same circle at every step (2.20). **The fingerprint's hashed fields were NOT extended** — `f.lenStep` is absent from the `:99-100` list — and the landing entry below explains why adding it would have bought nothing. The `LEN_MIN`/`LT` boot-gate concern stands, and `W.len` is indeed never mutated: assertion I of `.qa/lengrow-build-proof.mjs` proves the shared row is untouched (`len` 147.4720, tip 147.4720, outermost circle `d` 141.8).

**Law 3 will widen, and that is expected rather than a regression.** The `--band-dump` diagnosis of the same date proved the resolver's translation-only fixed point **scales with reach** (~10px at reach ~205 on the flail+scythe worst case), so longer weapons mean a deeper residual and larger band numbers. The mitigating fact: the substep budget *also* scales with reach (:1249, `tipV = |spin| × REACH`), so tunnelling does not get worse — only the resolver's residual does. Record the pre-growth band worst (20.754px) as the comparison point.

**No hook machinery is required — this does not wait on a special skill.** Under M2 a *rate on the weapon row* is **data** and the mechanic consuming it is **game-layer**, so length growth needs no hook indirection. This resolves an apparent tension with the cast ruling above, which lists "length growth" among the contextual skills that would force hooks to exist: that listing predates the axis-placement re-key, which moved length growth off the fighter and onto the weapon row. The hook interface therefore still waits for the first genuinely contextual special skill, per *"the hook machinery is not built until a special skill exists to hang on it."*

**Status: BUILT 2026-10-28 as `lengrow-r26`, raised to +35% as `lengrow-r27` and to +200% as `lengrow-r28`, all the same day — the rigid junction map with per-step RE-BAKED coverings, minimal spear-only scope.** The canonical has since moved on — see the CURRENT BUILD bullet for its live identity; at `lengrow-r28` it was md5 `4d0467dc5faafeb4480a72e2dcbbd8e5`, 3,279 lines. The spear row carries `trait: { spin: 0.0, dmg: 0.3, len: 0.0959 }`, `lenJ: 69.23 / 113.38` and the legend *"grows 9.6% longer per damaging hit"*; `dmg` 0.3 remains **provisional** pending the damage pass, and is now demonstrably coupled to the growth rate (see the rate paragraph). A boot-time `LEN_STEPS = 12` / `LEN_LADDER` builder throws if `lenJ` does not put the junction strictly inside the drawn tip, **and now also throws if a weapon grows past `LEN_REMAP_CEIL` (1.395, the swept +39.5% wall) without baked rows** — the gap-hit failure mode is a boot error, not a silent regression. `f.lenStep` resets every round in `rollWeapons`; `grow()` advances one step per damaging hit and clamps at the ladder top (no `lenCap` constant: the ladder top is the geometry ceiling, the round reset is the balance bound — **and as of 2026-10-28 that reset is an owner law, not just an implementation fact: "each round weapons traits should reset"**); `bladeCircles` takes the step; the renderer threads it; `REACH.spear` is the top step's **505.488** and `MINR.spear` is the **ladder-wide minimum, 2.00** (a re-baked step packs thinner than the shipped row, so the base value is no longer correct — this reverses r26's "MINR needs no correction"). **Proofs: 46/46** in `.qa/lengrow-build-proof.mjs` · `inscribe.mjs` **exit 0** with the spear's base row unchanged at n=19 / poke -0.30px / miss 4.25px · `lengrow-bake --verify` **18/18 steps PASS** since `skill-r29` raised the ladder top to the carrier's cap (12/12 at r28; poke -0.30px throughout, miss 2.30-4.35px) · leadproof, flipproof, chromecheck all PASS, exit 0. **The 8-cell fingerprint suite still moves 0 of 8 and is still blind to all of it.** Note that `.qa/lengrow-gate.mjs` now proves the **ceiling of the remap route**, not the shipped ladder — `lengrow-bake --verify` is the shipped-ladder proof. **Since `skill-r29` the ladder top is per-FIGHTER: `LEN_STEPS` 12 is the base and a carrier's `skill.lenSteps` adds to it, so the spear's ladder is built to 18 and `REACH.spear` is 829.571 — see the `skill-r29` entry, including why that changes the numerics of every spear battle and not only the carrier's.** **[AMENDED `nocombo-r34` (2026-10-31): RETIRED — the cap is uniform at `LEN_STEPS` 12 for every holder, rows 13–18 are purged, and `REACH.spear` is 505.488 again. The per-fighter top, 829.571 and the +420% era are history; the `nocombo-r34` entry has the record.]** The 484-cell re-run is owed but DEFERRED** to the end of the skills pass (owner ruling 2026-10-28: *"we are not trying to balance the game at all right now, we are making the skills/mechanics"*); when it does run it covers `stall-r25` + `lengrow-r28` + whatever the skills pass lands, and its law-3 band should be expected to widen substantially, since spear reach is 2.40× base.

### Movement speed — cruise, spikes and settle (doctrine + build 2026-10-21 — REVERTED same day at owner playtest; design record only)

**REVERTED at owner playtest (2026-10-21, owner: "ok i dont like that at all, it does not look natural anymore like it did before").** The sim is back to pre-doctrine ballistics, proven by fingerprint byte-identical to `.qa/fp-pre-cruise.json`. Everything below survives as a **design record only** — not normative, not to be re-proposed as-is without the owner; if the idea ever returns, it returns as separable pieces (floor-only vs settle-only) at owner word, with the playtest verdict on record.

Owner: "i think we should have a unified default speed everyone shares, but this can never be increased only by colissions … i dont want any bot to ever go below that speed, and a sped up bot does not maintain that speed forever"; on feel: "do what you think is best for the viewer, i want satisfying 'predictable/realistic' patterns (it also looks good right now)" with one explicit prohibition — no bot may ever change direction without a cause ("a bot if falling straight down and suddenly falls to the left with no interference"). The two dials were delegated to the assistant ("do what you think is best"): **cruise 250 px/s, settle half-life 0.5 s** — rationale at the end. The doctrine is normative for the build; the mechanics stay as shipped until then.

1. **Cruise is a floor, never a steer.** Every living fighter in the fight phase holds |v| ≥ cruise. The floor scales magnitude along the current travel direction only; at near-zero speed the fallback direction is gravity's. The regulator never adds lateral velocity: **direction is authored by gravity and contact alone** (walls, floor, bodies, blades) — the owner's falling-straight-down prohibition, written as a rule.
2. **Ballistics stay ballistics.** Gravity's own speed authorship — accelerating falls, slow apexes, the floaty arc that "looks good right now" — is never damped, and the round's launch speed (430) remains the opening ballistic baseline. Settle never touches gravity-authored speed; it only drains collision-authored excess.
3. **Only collisions author excess; excess settles.** Parry shove, hit knock, body-body exchange and the floor trampoline's injection add speed above the ballistic baseline; that excess decays with the settle half-life back toward the baseline, so a sped-up bot visibly calms (~12% of the excess left after 1.5s) and nothing ratchets forever. Today nothing subtracts speed — elastic walls preserve it, injections only add — so late-round pace creeps toward the temporary cap structurally; settle is the fix. Walls stay perfectly elastic (they preserve the arc energy that makes the current look); the floor re-injects its spike at every touch, so the perpetual bounce rhythm stays the show — now with a settling peak instead of a permanent one.
4. **Scope:** fight phase, living fighters only. Reveal, K.O. and over poses are untouched; the dead stay frozen as today.
5. **Spin doctrine stands as built:** shared ω 3.5, growth on damaging hits from fighter skill AND weapon trait — re-confirmed 2026-10-21 as balance-pass material, not a doctrine change (owner: "dont bother its going to be part of balancing later").
6. **Vocabulary (SPEC one-word rule):** **cruise speed** = the shared default floor; **excess** = speed above the ballistic baseline; **settle** = the excess's decay. No synonyms in docs, UI or harness output.
7. **Dial rationale (assistant, viewer-first, recorded for the balance pass):** cruise 250 — low enough that typical apex breathing (|vx| ≈ 250–430) survives as the approved floaty arc, high enough that the crawl/hover states (apex with little horizontal, bottom-centre cradle dwells — the stall reds' habitat) become a gentle glide: no dead air on the frame. Settle 0.5s — a parry shove separates the pair at full force first and calms afterwards, so spikes read as spikes and never as a new cruise. Both numbers join open question 1's knob list pending the first playtest. With settle carrying the pace-governor job, the temporary `maxSpeed` cap's keep/lift/replace verdict loses urgency: it degrades to a pure safety ceiling.
8. **When the build is ordered:** sim change → fingerprint ritual + the full fast tier; `measure.mjs` on owner go. Expect the stall tail and mirror-length reds to move (the floor removes low-speed dwells from both); the substep budget gains a trivially small floor term (cruise is far below impulse speeds).

**Build record (same day, owner order) — and revert:** built as a per-fighter excess ledger (impulses register, half-life drains; walls mirror, the floor re-anchors so the bounce stays ballistic — a decaying floor injection would have crushed the trampoline arc inside one half-life, disclosed at build); magnitude-only floor, no steering anywhere. Owner playtest rejected it same-day ("i dont like that at all, it does not look natural anymore like it did before"); all nine edits reverted byte-identically (`fp-after.json` restored; the cruise-era bytes at `.qa/fp-post-cruise.json`). Every number, proof and the one salvageable measurement (the floor removed low-speed dwells: four capped fingerprint cells terminated — the stall tail is real and addressable, this cure's feel was not acceptable) lives in **Measured facts**, entries "Cruise build" and "Cruise build REVERTED".

### Projectile weapons — the shot system (owner decisions 2026-10-16/17/19)

**Landed at roster-r12 (2026-10-25) — the kind registry and launch doctrine as they now stand:** nine data kinds in `SHOTS_ART` (arrow · bolt · knife · orb · spark · spell · halo · nova · vortex) plus the **copy kinds** star/boom/javelin, whose throws draw the wielder's own weapon polys at the area-centroid pivot (mid-meAT for hollow arcs) with slowed spins (owner word: the shipped ~10 rev/s aliased). Knives fire a **three-knife volley** (owner ruling b: cadence ×3 = damage-rate neutral; tips at the measured anchors (17.69,−60.95)/(50.7,−73.8)/(83.71,−60.95), each along its own blade axis in **spine-frame axes, never wAngle trig**; rim clamp on out-of-arena tips). The tome cycles halo→nova→vortex in **strict rotation** (provisional implementer recommendation, playtest silent); the scroll fires the spell comet. **[2026-10-28 — launcher consolidation plus two owner rulings: all three of those special-cases are now ROW DATA (`W.shot.volley` — the anchors, in WORLD px: they equal the ×1.3-scaled poly's own extremes and sit deliberately OUTSIDE the boot weaponScale loop, which scales shot r/off/len — `W.shot.revs` 3, `W.shot.cycle` the trio) and `fireShots` now contains no `f.weapon === …` test at all. The tome trio's PROVISIONAL marker is now a ruling: owner 2026-10-28 — the three kinds become mechanically distinct CONTENT in the skills pass (the affinity locals below already pre-figure fire/poison/frost), so the shipped rotation stands as the placeholder and adding or re-ordering kinds is a data edit. The tome row's `kind: 'page'` is vestigial: the cycle supersedes it in the launcher and HALO has had no 'page' tint since this split. Recorded, not removed — a data change is a sim change until proven otherwise.]** **Affinity locals** declared in-zone (scroll precedent):
KF1/KF2 fire comet · KP1/KP2 poison · KN1 frost · KV1/KV2 fire-orange — owner word 2026-10-25: they pre-figure the skill pass. Sigil doctrine on shots: one KC accent element per kind (dyed fletching, binding band, rune nucleus), whole-body KC waived only for the orb/spark family. **[2026-10-28, sigil legibility: for the four spell-family kinds the KC nucleus is no longer a delivered shape. It is a plain 32-gon carrier disc whose radius IS the launched hitbox (`WEAPONS.scroll.shot.r` / `WEAPONS.tome.shot.r`, derived so it tracks `weaponScale`), sitting on a dark ink plate at 1.3× that radius and flagged `vis: 1` so it paints without a stroke (the mechanism `drawPolySet`'s own comment had described since 2026-10-22 but that no shape could reach, because `build()` dropped the flag — now passed through). Owner pick "grow + solid mark", applied to all four kinds; the delivered nuclei (the scroll's 4-point star, the trio's 32-gon at r 5.25) are retired verbatim (removed from HEAD 2026-11-11; tag `docs-precleanup`) and the flame layers stay delivery-verbatim. Measured reason: the delivered nucleus was 5.68 css px across with NO keyline at all, at 1.60–2.32:1 against its own flame, on a 15.4 ms dwell (≈0.93 frames).]** Launch-doctrine addenda (close item 8):
per-blade axes for fanned tangential volleys · art-centre spawns for copy kinds · rim clamp. `shot.len` survives as fingerprint-hashed flight state though no draw reads it — the legacy procedural shot draws (drawArrow/drawJavelin and the seven branches) retired at the 2026-10-25 cleanup, tombstoned at drawShot.

The sling (v0.7) established the archetype; the bow replaced it (v0.9, owner 2026-10-19: "the sling should be removed, instead make it a bow, and the projectiles should be arrows") and v1.0 generalised it into the shot system. The full sling history — its four default rules, the swat decision, the mid-spine launch era, the v0.7.x baselines — is in the archive; what follows is the current normative doctrine.

**The projectile rule (owner 2026-10-19, restating and widening the 2026-10-17 rule):** "ranged weapons (shooting projectiles), their weapons do not damage, only their projetiles." A weapon with a shot config deals **no spine damage**, whatever its hold — the radial-hold launchers (staff, wand) included. The spine stays a full solid: it latches and knocks the body (zero-damage shove), parries weapon-vs-weapon, and swats enemy shots. Its shot is its only damage.

**Firing is physics, not a scheduler.** One shot leaves per full revolution of the spin (a per-fighter radian accumulator, `fireAcc += |spin|·sdt`, releases at 2π) — **spin rate IS fire rate**, no cooldown stat, no ammo, no new vocabulary: `+SPD / Hit` on a launcher *is* faster shooting. Firing falls out of the physics because nobody pilots these bots.

**Launch doctrine (owner 2026-10-19):** "when the weapon is radial, shoot from the tip, when it is the opposite, shoot from the middle, like bow and crossbow." A **radial-hold** launcher releases at the spine **tip** (t = 1), flying outward along the spine (staff, wand). A **tangential-hold** launcher releases at the spine **middle** (t = 0.5, the grip point on the rim); there the direction splits per weapon: *shooting* weapons (bow, crossbow — `fire: 'radial'`) send the shot radially **out** of the grip, offset by `shot.off` so the head starts at the belly — a bow shoots where its belly faces, not along its limbs; *thrown* weapons (boomerang, shuriken) leave **tangentially in the spin direction**. Law 6s recomputes every launch from its stored firing pose to the hold-derived point (0 off-point ever, 6175 checked at the v1.2 probe). This supersedes the v0.7.2 "always from the middle" rule.

**Shot damage (owner decision, 2026-10-16):** the shot launches with the thrower's **current** damage — snapshotted at launch — and there is no separate projectile stat (one damage number per fighter, `a.dmg`; it grows on hit via the fighter's skill, so a Heavy's shots fatten +0.9 per hit while a Speedy's stay light and come faster).

**Shot flight and deaths.** Straight flight, no gravity, no drag — the boomerang alone curves (its locked 421×250px oval, row turn 4.0 with the 0.65 elongation modulation, anchored to its wielder — `halo-r39`). Deaths, in the fixed deterministic per-substep order rim → body → swat → shot-shot: **rim** kills (law 1 — walls never damage); the sanctioned exceptions are the shuriken star's **ETERNAL RICOCHET** (`shot.ricochet`, `ricolaw-r41` — the rim never kills a star; it mirrors forever at row speed), the boomerang's **pierce**, and the crossbow's **NAIL** (`shot.pin`, `pinlaw-r44` — the bolt sticks just inside the rim as a shot AT REST, `speed` 0, keeping its impact flight line as the drawn facing (`pinfix-r45`; the inward knock vector is stored separately as `pnx/pny`); it ends only on the foe's body (the law-4 hit, consumed), the foe's blade (swat), an in-flight enemy shot (cross) or the roll-clear; the owner walks through his own nails; nail×nail never crosses — statics cannot visibly resolve). Shot-vs-shot is ENEMY-ONLY since `multgrow-r42` (generalizing the r41 star exemption at the owner's option-A ruling): SAME-OWNER pairs skip the cross test on EVERY shot class — friendly fire does not exist; enemy pairs still cancel both. **Body** contact is a damaging hit and consumes the shot (law 4 structural — the fourth row-owned exception is the javelin's **SKEWER**, `skewer-r46`: the hit does NOT consume the spear; it impales, carries the victim to the rim, pins and bleeds them, and only the victim's own blade cutting it, an enemy cross, or the roll ends it; a killing hit consumes normally). **Blade** contact is a **swat**: the shot is destroyed and nothing else happens — no parry event, **no spin flip on either fighter** (the named, documented rule-5 exception; readable on the reveal poster and legend line). **Shot-vs-shot** cancels both — enemy pairs only (symmetric, provable). **No friendly fire** — built as *owner-blindness*: a shot is never tested against its owner's body, blade or SHOTS (`multgrow-r42`), so it can never self-destruct, self-swat or self-cross. **Law 3 turns asymmetric:** a shot has no body behind it to push, so instead of being separated it **loses every collision it makes** — never overlapping blade, body, rim or another shot on a rendered frame. Detection keeps the same guarantee as blades: substeps sized so relative travel per substep ≤ half the narrowest window — blade-tip vs shot speed is the largest relative velocity in the sim, and since v1.2 the shot-vs-shot term budgets the **worst pair** of in-flight shots (mixed speeds: spark 800 vs orb 430 — the old single-speed formula under-budgeted); the audit checks the sim's chosen k against both budgets every tick.

**Drawn shape vs hitbox.** Disc-shaped shots (orb, spark, star, boomerang) draw at exactly `shot.r` — the drawn disc *is* the hitbox. Arrows and bolts (`shot.len`) draw shaft + fletching **trailing behind** the hitbox circle, which sits at the head — the business end is what hits, the trail is paint. "What you see is what hits" survives with that stated reading.

**Audit extension (`measure.mjs`, mutation-tested at birth in v0.7; re-pointed at v1.2 and run the same day — part-0 naturals + the `--solidity` gauntlet, both green; the matrix portion awaits the balance pass).** Seven shot-law checks — 1s damage only on shot-touches-body (recomputed from the stored death pose; 0 phantom ever) · 2s swats only on exact shot-blade touch (swats flip no spin — proven by core law 5's sign audit) · 3s shots never overlap foe blade, foe body, rim, or another shot at tick-entry poses (doubles as the anti-teleport bracket: "outside at entry, biting at death") · 4s accounting — every fired shot has exactly one proven departure (landed / swatted / rim / cross / cleared at the round roll) or is still in flight at the battle end; a shot vanishing without an event is the tunneling alarm · 5s the substep budget audit — 0 violations, 0 `maxSub` engagements · 6s the launch doctrine — every launch recomputes to its hold-derived point · 7s the projectile rule — zero melee hits by projectile weapons, any hold; the core law-4 replica mirrors the sim gate (a gated rising edge is a flag-audited zero-damage shove with no hit event to pair). Shot hits do NOT pair with the bladeStuck replica (a shot is consumed — law 4 is structural) and deliberately do not feed the law-5 parry magnitude pairing (a shot hit grows the thrower *after* the same substep's parry, unlike blade hits — a pairing bug this caught in dev). Stall-timer semantics: hit/parry/**swat**/bounce; event-rate includes shot events. The combo matrix is **forced** (curator knob) — now **324 ordered cells** × 8 shared seeds with the 18-weapon roster (was 169 at 13), because natural rolls thin to a handful of rounds per cell.

### Hold styles (owner decision 2026-10-16, amended 2026-10-19 — BUILT)

How a weapon sits on the body. Owner verdict on the sling's original radial grip: **"it should not point outwards, it should be held along the edge of the body."** Two placements:

- **Radial** (the default): the spine runs straight out from the body centre, grip tucked under the disc (`SPINE_R0` 0.96). All melee weapons and the radial launchers (staff, wand). *Measured 2026-10-28 (authoring-scale pass):* the tuck is **one engine constant, identical for all 13 radial rows** — the grip mounts at `bodyR × SPINE_R0` = **61.44 px**, i.e. **2.56 px inside the rim**, and the own disc is clipped out of the weapon pass, so the overlap is hidden ink rather than a visible seam (11 of the 13 rows put their innermost ink at exactly r 61.44; the axe's butt cut starts 0.45 px forward of `d` 0, r 61.89; the orb is handle-less and floats 46.84 px clear, r 110.84). Detail budget belongs from the guard outwards.
- **Tangential**: the spine is a straight tangent chord at the body's rim point — grip centered on the rim at t = 0.5, both ends floating clear of the disc. Nine rows: bow, crossbow, boomerang, shuriken, knives, javelin, tome, scroll, shield. *Amended 2026-10-28 (authoring-scale pass item 2):* they no longer carry `tuck: false` — a tangential chord never tucks (its art straddles the rim, so the clip would notch it), and the render pass now derives that from `hold`; the nine redundant declarations were removed and only handle-less **radial** art still opts out by name (the orb). Because tangential rows are unclipped, ink inside the rim draws over the own disc — how far inside is per-artist today and is recorded as an open pick in the convention's **Placement & reach** bullet.

**Since v1.2 the hold decides placement and launch point only — not damage capability** (that is the projectile rule's job; the old "ranged = tangential" class identity is dead). A curved rim-hugging spine remains **parked**: the path representation could carry it now, but nothing needs it yet — the straight chord is the mechanics truth unless a future batch decides otherwise.

**Why the contact law holds by construction.** Placement lives in **one layer** (`spineFrame`: spine origin, unit direction, span for a body pose — plus `leadFlip`, the edge-leading mirror of 2026-10-22) consumed identically by the collision circles, the renderer and the harness's audit recomputes — the drawing cannot drift from the hitbox, its mirror included ("what you see is what hits" survives structurally, not by discipline).

### Locked frame (2026-10-15) — the channel's look

Owner-confirmed as **the vibe and look for all games on the channel**, not just this one. No live toggles; every element below is final until an owner decision reopens it. (The **weapon art** is a separate matter and is NOT locked — see below.)

- **Theme: PEBBLE.** Warm gray-taupe page (radial `#c6bda9 → #bab09b → #aca18b`), light warm-sand arena floor (`#e7dfca`) that reads *lighter than the page but never glaring*, cream surfaces (`#ede7d6`), warm ink (`#2e2b22`) and warm gray (`#4a4436`) type. The picker, nine candidates and every code reference were stripped at lock. SPEC.md carries the token record; Phase 3 lifts the table from `spinclash-preview.html` (`TH` object + `:root` tokens).
- **Arena & rim: Hairline.** The circle inscribed in the platform's square bounds; a mid-taupe band (`#a99f86`) with a dark hairline outer edge (`#8d8369`); the floor is flat (a graph-grid texture was tried and removed the same iteration).
- **Scorebug above the arena** — a plain row, no container: mini-bot discs, names in the players' deep tints, the series score big in ink. The only persistent place wins and large names appear.
- **Legend below the arena: Columns** — the frame's **single sanctioned container**: one cream card holding a real `<table>`, two rows, reading order `[mini-bot · tint name · skill code] …air… [weapon · trait code] …air… [SPD · DMG]`. Shorthand codes read `+SPD / Hit` / `+DMG / Hit`; launchers read `+SPD / Hit · <shot> / rev`; the dice-reveal poster teaches the full sentences once per round so the legend can stay terse. Auto table layout aligns every column vertically across rows and hugs the codes to their names; stat numbers live in fixed 3ch tabular boxes so growth never reflows anything (a regression that dropped those boxes once moved the table at double digits — the rule is written here so it survives future edits). Bot names and weapon names carry equal weight (800). No HP in the legend (it lives on the bots), no wins (the scorebug carries them), no icons, no "knocked out" text (the row dim carries K.O.). The table was chosen after a subgrid and a fixed-track grid both died in the owner's viewer — it renders aligned in any engine. This layout is the platform's scoreboard design for every game.
- **Type & color economy — six colors in the whole frame.** Two cast fills (Speedy `#a8892f`, Heavy `#ff6b4a`) for actors only — bots, mini-bot discs; their two deep tints (`#4a3b12`, `#6f2818`, ≥4.5:1 on page and cream) for every name in type; warm ink bold for facts (series score, weapon names, stat numbers); warm gray for explanations (codes, skills). Raw cast color never sets type. Mini-bots are cast fill + the bots' ink border, empty inside, centered on the name's line in legend and scorebug alike.
- **No containers anywhere else.** In-arena moments — round banners — are type straight on the floor (the K.O. card's type was removed at owner word 2026-10-21, "i dont want it to be announced": the round's end reads as the kill-still freeze, the faded disc and the scorebug tick); the battle winner is type on a warm scrim, with the stats tail (`shots X thrown / Y landed / Z swatted`) when any shot flew. The **sanctioned exception is the dice reveal**: the owner-designed VS poster (2026-10-19) covers the arena disc for its 285 ticks and dissolves — a moment, not chrome. Transient name moments (the winner overlay) are announcements, not chrome.

## Weapon art — the closed convention (locked 2026-10-18, CLOSED 2026-10-27)

Ratified in full at the owner vote of 2026-10-27 ("ok i vote yes") on the convention-close sheet (items 1-9 + P2/P3; the draft retired with the close). Everything below is PRESCRIPTIVE for every future weapon and batch; the descriptive history (the 2026-10-18 lock text, the two-paths era, the commission writeups) is retired verbatim (removed from HEAD 2026-11-11; recover with `git show docs-precleanup:docs/CHRONICLE-archive.md`, section "Retired at the 2026-10-27 close").

- **Path (2a/3a-3d):** the in-file poly builder via the artist-kit route is THE canonical path — *amended 2026-10-28 (hygiene pass #2): the tooling that realized the route was purged at the pre-balance checkpoint (`artist-kit.html`, `artist-kit.mjs`, `redesign-preview.mjs`, `sheet-v2.mjs`, `design-shots.json`), so the route is now PRESCRIPTIVE-ONLY — its grammar, paste ledger and design brief are retired verbatim (recover with `git show docs-precleanup:docs/CHRONICLE-archive.md`, section "Retired at the 2026-10-28 pre-balance checkpoint") and the kit-route rebuild re-seeds from there plus the landed canonical art.* `ingest.mjs` stands dormant/superseded, kept at root without privilege (KEPT — it is the live weapon-merge gate with its combat gauntlet). Runbooks retire per delivery and rebuild fresh; `sheet-v2` *was* the standing review vehicle (purged 2026-10-28 — see the Sigil item for what that costs); owner direct edits are legal and reconciled into the record at next touch.
- **Paints (1a/1b/1c/7):** five in art — wood `KW #7a4a2a`, dark wood `KD #4f3220`, steel `KS #8fa3b5`, near-white `KP #eaf6ff` (cutting/striking faces AND paper), light blue `KB #9fe0ff` (gems, stars) — plus the uniform ink outline `#2e2b22`; flat fills, no gradients/texture/glow. Gold `KG` is RETIRED from art (sweep-r15, owner option B, 2026-10-27) and survives only as the carrier-less sigil fallback; amber retired 2026-10-18. The per-weapon reference-colour class is permanent under the scroll's four rules; the spell-family affinity locals (fire/poison/frost/fire-orange) are a permanent extension pre-figuring the skill pass.
- **Sigil (6a-6d):** exactly ONE diegetic carrier-coloured element per weapon via the `KC` tag (recolor-first, never on KP, interior-ink preferred); the one-`KC` invariant is gated by sheet-v2's x1 — *amended 2026-10-28: `sheet-v2.mjs` was purged at the pre-balance checkpoint, so `x1` is review-only until the kit-route rebuild restores a tool that counts `@carrier` polys;* shot nuclei are `KC` ownership carriers inside the doctrine, review accents are tooling outside it; the realized mark map (22 rows) is recorded in the close entry in **Measured facts**.
- **Handheld spec (4a-4d):** the **length band** — floor `LEN_MIN` 54 (the orb, shortest shipped weapon) to ceiling `LT`, named for the spear, **both ends boot-gated** since 2026-10-28 (balance pass item 2; a convention before that, which is how the crossbow came to overshoot it silently in 2026-10-21); one shaft size (~11.5u full width); plain brown butt; flat butt cut; deviations only as named, disclosed owner picks; bands are guidance; per-family proportions carry no prose rules — reference implementations outrank prose; the veterans' rounded caps stay grandfathered until their next art touch (registry re-derived from the delivered art at roster-r12).
- **Placement & reach (2026-10-28, authoring-scale pass item 1 — owner "i want you to do 1 and 2"; every number below measured from the shipped canonical with its own formulas, not estimated).** Both mount rules are **rim-based — nothing is ever drawn from the body centre.** Reach is *measured* from the centre only because that is the sim's single contact coordinate (two discs, centre-to-centre), which is exactly why a reach figure reads larger than the art's own length:
  - **Radial:** the grip (spine `d` 0) mounts at `CFG.bodyR × SPINE_R0` = **61.44 px** — **2.56 px inside the 64 px rim** — from one engine constant shared by all 13 radial rows, so the overlap is not per-weapon data and a new weapon cannot get it wrong. The own disc is clipped out of the weapon pass, so those 2.56 px are *hidden ink*, not a visible seam: the shaft emerges at the rim and reads as held. **Detail budget belongs from the guard outwards** (owner decision 2026-10-18) — anything drawn inside the first ~2.5 px is eaten by the clip. Measured innermost ink: **11 of the 13 radial rows at exactly r 61.44** (hammer, dagger, sword, spear, mace, staff, wand, flail, nunchaku, scythe, katana); the **axe** at r 61.89 (its butt cut starts 0.45 px forward of `d` 0 — sub-pixel, recorded not fixed, and fixing it would cost an art re-bake); the **orb** at r 110.84, i.e. **46.84 px clear of the rim** (handle-less by design — see its row, whose older "overlaps the disc by ≈15px" comment omitted the 61.44 mount offset and is amended in place).
  - **Tangential:** the chord's **midpoint sits exactly on the rim** (64 px), perpendicular to the radius, both ends clear of the disc. Nine rows: bow, crossbow, boomerang, shuriken, knives, javelin, tome, scroll, shield. Tangential art is never clipped (derived from `hold` since 2026-10-28), so ink inside the rim draws over the own disc.
  - **The two reach formulas** (`REACH` is computed at boot from the shipped polys; these are its closed forms — `len` = authored spine, ×1.3 `weaponScale` as played, +1.5 px ink half-stroke): **radial** `reach ≈ 61.4 + 1.3·len + tip-corner + 1.5` → at the ceiling, **206–214 px**, the whole class inside an 8 px band (spear 210.4 with its point on the spine … axe 212.1 with a tapered tip ±54.6 … hammer 213.6 with a flat face ±47.0). **Tangential** `reach ≈ hypot(64, 1.3·len/2) + 1.5` → at the ceiling, **~102 px** (javelin 101.9). Because the chord is centred on the rim, more `len` buys reach by Pythagoras rather than addition — which is why the longest spines in the game (javelin and crossbow, 147.5 as played) sit near the *bottom* of the reach table. Class spread, not unfairness. Full 22-row table: the length-band entry in this section.
  - **Authors do not aim at `LT` by hand.** The heavy four are equalized at boot by measured factors off their own authored tips: mace ×1.00000 (authored maxD 113.44 — the ceiling's source), spear ×1.00053 (113.38), hammer ×1.00389 (113.00), axe ×1.01105 (112.20); the crossbow is cut down by `KX` ×0.99118 (authored 114.45). A new weapon just lands inside the `LEN_MIN`…`LT` band and the boot gate says so: proportions are the author's, the number is the engine's.
  - **The tuck clip can never hide a hitbox:** ink at r ≤ `bodyR` from the own centre cannot be inside the opponent's disc at any legal separation (centres are 2 × `bodyR` = 128 px apart at contact), so "what you see is what hits" survives the clip.
  - **OPEN — recorded, not legislated (needs an owner pick before the next tangential commission):** how a tangential chord's art straddles the rim is currently per-artist, and the shipped nine disagree. Measured radial extent from the body centre: entirely outside — knives 65.7…137.8, crossbow 66.0…130.0, bow 64.0…130.0, boomerang 63.1…135.2; straddling — javelin 56.6…71.3 (±7), scroll 50.8…77.2 (±13), tome 56.2…82.6 (7.8 inside), shield 58.4…85.7 (5.6 inside — a shield covering the body edge, deliberate). Candidates: (a) declare straddling legal and bound it (e.g. ≤ 8 px inside the rim), (b) require entirely-outside like the bow class, (c) leave it to reference implementations, as the convention already prefers for proportions. No behaviour consequence today; it is an art-direction decision.
- **Directionality (5a-5c):** `lead: +/-1` for lateral business contours — **four rows, all -1: katana and scythe (2026-10-22), dagger and staff (2026-10-28 facing pass, owner playtest on the dagger's one-sided white bevel)** — and `headLead: +/-1` for along-chord head ends (javelin +1); art and hitbox mirror together on spin sign; the 180 ms flip transition is the one accepted art-hitbox-divergence window; chains are forbidden the flags by boot throw; the flag registry stands as re-quoted at the landing (leadproof 178/178, 286/286, 182/182, **86/86, 232/232** — the last two added 2026-10-28, when the facing pass extended PROBES/Part A2/CELLS for the dagger and the staff as this harness's own header requires; **an unextended leadproof reports ALL PASS while covering nothing new**, which is what it did on that landing's first run). The 22-row facing audit that found them — KP centroid, off-axis covering circles, outline asymmetry per row — is in the facing-pass entry in **Measured facts**; it confirms the unflagged rows are unflagged for a reason (mirrored double bevels, the orb's zero off-axis circles, chains boot-forbidden) and records the mace's spiral highlights and the tangential rows' radial-axis KP as deliberately *not* facing cues.
- **Circles (roundness class, 2026-10-27):** true circles are cut as 32-gons at authored centre and circumradius; elongated wraps keep their authored contours (the aspect gate above 1.15 exempts them).
- **Bakes and gates (2b-2d):** covering circles generate from the same polys (draw and hitbox cannot drift); parameters locked canonical (1.2u inflate, min r 4, floor 2.0 plus named overrides scroll 1.6 / axe 1.8); no retroactive epsilon/gauntlet for the v1.0 builder batch — the standing suite is the proof regime; partial art edits splice via `.qa/bake-rows.mjs`; the inscribe gate runs after any art edit; leadproof/flipproof re-quote for flagged weapons.
- **Legibility (1d/P3):** silhouette-first and exaggeration stand as guidance; the old squint-at-120px and min-feature gates are retired; the old lock's "squash test" naming is amended to "squint test" (verified against the archive text at the close).
- **Amendment clause (P2):** carried from the 2026-10-18 lock — the owner's in-session word overrides any line here, and the amendment lands in the record the same day.

## Design principles for all games

- **Structure before flavour.** Boredom is fixed by mechanics that generate swings, not by scheduled events or character complexity.
- **Reversals must be attributable.** A swing should be caused by something the audience can point at — a contact, a capture, a raid — never by a hidden number or a scheduled event. (The dice roll is exempt by design: it is an *initial condition*, shown openly before the round, never a mid-fight swerve.)
- **Quiet frame, loud fight.** The owner's settled taste across every visual round: warm, clean, undistracting chrome; all motion and color budget spent inside the arena. Effects never obscure match state; ridiculousness comes from mechanics, not decoration.
- **Hitboxes are the contract with the viewer.** The audience's trust rests on "what you see is what hits": the drawn silhouette *is* the collision shape, every registered contact is provable on the frame it's shown, and solids never silently pass through solids. Owner, 2026-10-16: "for the viewer perfect hitboxes is the most important." SPINCLASH's **Core contact law** is this principle written as rules; a headless audit re-verifies it after every change. It ranks above visual polish — a beautiful frame that lies about contact is a broken frame.
- **Fundamentals before furniture.** Settle the mechanics contract (what damages, what collides, what is solid) before investing in anything drawn on top of it — owner, 2026-10-16: "we need to get the fundamental mechanics right before going further with the weapons." Weapons get redesigned repeatedly; the contact law they must obey does not. (Distinct from *Structure before flavour*, which is about what cures boredom.)

## Open design questions

1. **SPINCLASH balance pass — deferred until all mechanics are working** (owner, 2026-10-16: "dont bother about balancing now, we do that once all mechanics etc are working"; reconfirmed 2026-10-19 for the dagger and shield: "we will do a balance later and figure it out"). **Scope widened to the balance & SKILL rework (owner, 2026-10-22: "cant we do this in one big balance and skill 'rework', cause i know that needs some work done, right now i just wanne make a full roster of weapons, and get the desired looks of the weapons, so we can finally close the weapon design convention"):** the rework owns every mechanical/numerical question — the scroll's burst:3 shape (spread vs sequential vs every-Nth; it ships art-first as a single-shot launcher until then), the skill-vs-trait model (open question 3), the shield's purpose (open question 4), everything below. The sanctioned `measure.mjs` re-proof is deferred to the same window by the same word ("we will talk about this once the weapons are done") — ~15 sim-data changes and 7 weapons have landed since the 2026-10-19 runs; the law re-proof stands owed on the owner's machine before or at the rework — **and as of 2026-10-28 the chain-pose fix it needed has LANDED in `measure.mjs` (the `chainlaw` entry) — the run is all that is owed, and it is expected to read 12 PASS with contact law 3 red on its worst-depth clause alone. The rework inherits NO item from that attribution: the single-frame 10.09px katana+shuriken crossing was itself attributed the same day (owner: "fix it now") to the edge-leading MIRROR frame — the owner-accepted `CFG.flipMs` trade, sim truth, measured never drawn (Part F worst drawn gap −0.149px) — and law 3 now names and discloses that class while judging the band on everything else (band worst 1.738px, 0 unexplained visible overlaps). The resolver was deliberately NOT changed: re-resolving after the flip is the one-substep-teleport class the owner rejected 2026-10-21.** **Mission until then: complete the roster, land the looks, close the weapon art convention.** **Owner ruling 2026-10-28 (post-facing-pass): the non-termination is DELIBERATE and stays frozen — "the round ending is all deliberate, cause once we implement skills for each weapon, the game will be completely different, i know shield has no purpose yet etc. dont worry". Everything in the symptom list below is therefore the intended pre-skills baseline, not a work item; the mission until the skills pass is to make the tree clean, not to make rounds end.** Recorded symptoms, frozen until then: **shield+shield never terminates** (the shield cannot damage); **pure-projectile mirror cells routinely pass 300s** (probe: 3/18 no winner in 300s — bow+crossbow ×2, wand+bow; the sling mirror's old 116s was the mild version); the stall alarm was red at the last full audit (5.29s worst vs the 5s line); the buzzsaw (≥25 rad/s) — **the "absent since v0.7" claim is RETIRED 2026-10-28: it was a damage-5 fact, and at base damage 1 natscan reads maxSpin 103.5 rad/s on 8/30 naturals, so the band is crossed routinely** (the ceiling's remaining headroom is in the skills-prep cost map); the dagger's growth axis flipped (+SPD → +DMG) — **no longer "with no recorded decision": owner ruling 2026-10-28 defers every weapon's growth axis to the skills pass, so the flipped value ships by deliberate deferral** (roster table ⚠ note); fighter×weapon asymmetry at 22 weapons is unmeasured. Knobs for the eventual session: growth rates per weapon/trait, the physics triple (gravity 1400 since the 2026-10-21 bounce tune, was 1500 / chaos ceiling 1200 with wallRest 0.9 since the same-day top-rim tune, was cap 1100 perfectly elastic / knock 130–150), the parked escalation levers (damage-scaled knock; spin-scaled parry shove — both bound by the pace acceptance line: p50 ≈ 743, >1100 tick share in the low double digits at most), the clinch-spring triple (clinchRamp 48 / clinchDecay 3 / clinchAcc 2200 — landed 2026-10-27, the bind-prevention law), shot parameters (speed, radius, cadence, turn, bounce), `weaponScale` 1.3, the temporary observer cap keep/lift/replace verdict (urgency reduced by the settle doctrine — it degrades to a safety ceiling), playback speed, the movement-speed doctrine's dials (cruise 250 / settle half-life 0.5s — **tried 2026-10-21, reverted at playtest: "does not look natural anymore"; do not retry as-is**; the stall-tail improvement it produced — four capped cells terminating — is on record as measured evidence for any future pace work), and possibly a spin-growth payoff (buzzsaw revival). **Measured at `dmg1-r21` (2026-10-28): the projectile-mirror question is now a TERMINATION question — with base damage 1, `projectile-fix` records 9/18 battles with no winner in 300 s (was 4/18), and the failures are exactly the both-sides-ranged cells (bow+crossbow 3/3, staff+wand 3/3, wand+bow 2/3, boomerang+shuriken 1/3); every cell with a melee damage-dealer still ends in 84–146 s. 4/20 naturals turned pathological (seeds 15/34/37/40), seed 40 hitting the 420 s natscan cap with no winner. See the `dmg1-r21` entry in Measured facts. Re-measured at `lead-r22` (2026-10-28, after the facing pass and the pre-skills cleanup): `projectile-fix` D is unchanged at **9/18** and unchanged cell-for-cell (bow+crossbow 3/3, staff+wand 3/3, wand+bow 2/3, boomerang+shuriken 1/3); natscan's no-winner naturals are seeds **33 and 36** (2/30, total 891,003 ticks), and its "pathological seeds" list is a wall-clock report field rather than a sim fact (15/34/40 unloaded, 15/33/34/36/37/40 under load).** **[Amended 2026-11-02, `allcut-r62`: the two launcher-side stall MECHANISMS — the shield's cannot-damage and the knock-only launcher spine — are RETIRED by owner ruling, so those symptom classes (shield+shield non-termination, the pure-projectile mirror 300s+ family) are expected to shrink; unmeasured per #674, the sanctioned re-proof stays deferred to the balance rework. First visible evidence: the wand+scroll s4321 chromecheck exercise, undecided through a full 180s sim at r61, COMPLETED inside the 45s fast-forward wall at r62.]**
2. **Parry reward — ANSWERED (2026-10-28, owner: parry should not reward anything; re-confirmed for skills the same day, owner pick "Forbid it — parry rewards nothing, full stop").** Parry stays a pure denial valve: no growth, no position, no tempo — and **no skill may reward a parry either**. Consequence, measured: spin-per-parry was the spin-ceiling entry's likeliest way to wake the dormant `omegaCap: 140`, so the clamp stays asleep on that axis and the remaining growth triggers are damaging hit, time and launch. The **comeback-valve half is NOT answered by this** — if a comeback mechanism is ever wanted it has to come from somewhere other than the parry.
3. **Skill vs weapon-trait model — ANSWERED (2026-10-28, owner ruling verbatim: "M2").** Two languages: a weapon's **trait** is data (a growth rate on the shared axes), a fighter's **skill** is a `CONTRACT.md` **hook** that modifies an existing mechanic and never introduces one. Adopted **lazily** — the two shipped numeric skills stay legal and no plumbing is added until the first non-numeric skill lands. The doctrine, the measured case against M1, the per-axis cost checklist and the five decisions that follow all live in **Fighter skill vs weapon trait — the model** in the SPINCLASH section.
4. **Shield design** — what is a weapon that cannot damage *for*? Current state is a placeholder that stalls mirrors; the balance pass decides (damage-on-parry? shove-only win condition? cut it?). **MEASURED 2026-10-28** (first sanctioned `measure.mjs` run, COMPLETE at 484 of 484 cells): the shield holder won **0 of 569 rounds in either hand** — 308 rounds as Speedy's weapon across 21 cells, 261 as Heavy's across 21 cells (the partial figure was Heavy 0 of 229), and `shield+shield` completed **zero rounds in 8 battles**, all eight running to exactly the 420.0s budget with 0/8 terminated — under KO-only rules it cannot win at all, and because `grow()` fires only on a damaging hit, its `trait: { spin: 0.0, dmg: 0.7 }` **can never activate**, nor can its wielder's fighter skill; both are inert data. It is also the mechanism behind question 1's `shield+shield` non-termination — neither side is capable of a KO, so that cell can only ever exhaust the 420s budget. The balance pass still decides, but now against a number rather than a suspicion. **[AMENDED 2026-11-02, `allcut-r62` — the MECHANISM half is ANSWERED by ruling: the shield cuts on touch like every weapon, so it CAN KO, and its `trait: { spin: 0.0, dmg: 0.7 }` plus its wielder's fighter skill are LIVE (the smoke caught its first-ever grown-damage hits). What a now-damaging tangential wall is FOR stays with the balance pass; the 0-of-569 record above is a pre-r62 fact.]**
5. **Boomerang persistence — empirically settled (2026-10-19, `.qa/boomdiag.mjs` + `.qa/natscan.mjs`).** The orbit-forever/accumulation fear did not materialize: boomerangs die reliably by rim/cross/swat/landing, in-flight counts stay ≤ 9 across every natural seed, and the mirror terminates (~83s). No lifetime rule is needed unless future measurement says otherwise; what remains of it is the general projectile-mirror length question → open question 1. **RE-OPENED at `dmg1-r21` (2026-10-28):** every figure above was measured at base damage 5. At damage 1 the in-flight counts reach **15–28** (was ≤ 9), the four pathological naturals (seeds 15/34/37/40) each contain a boomerang round, and boomerang+shuriken fails to terminate in 1 of 3 probe seeds. The 'no lifetime rule needed' verdict therefore holds at dmg 5 only; at dmg 1 it is open again, folded into open question 1.
6. **The roster — the FIGHTER cast SCOPED (2026-10-28, owner ruling verbatim: "the cast stays at two for now, as those two are the most generic type bots with their skills +dmg and +spd, later we are going to have other bots with more special/unique skills").** Speedy and Heavy are no longer placeholders awaiting archetypes: they are the **generic tier**, one per universal numeric growth axis — Speedy owns spin (+0.30), Heavy owns damage (+0.9) — and between them they cover the whole numeric space, so the tier is complete at two **by construction, not by schedule**. All growth beyond them is the **special/unique tier**, which is what pays for the M2 hook interface; the doctrine, the parked third axis and the two things to watch are in **Fighter skill vs weapon trait — the model**. The WEAPON side is complete (22, 2026-10-23 — the commission queue is empty; future weapons would be new commissions). **[Amended 2026-10-28, `pair-r31`: the ruling stands — the cast is still two — but its *cost* changed. The pairing step is BUILT, so `newGame(seed, { cast: [i, j] })` already selects 2 of N and the sim has no remaining 1v1 identity assumptions (slot = side, idx = actor, `G.cast[slot]` the map). A third bot is now a `FIGHTERS` row + an owner-chosen colour + widening the seven two-entry arrays (`SPAWN`, `COLS`, `TINTS`, `view`/`VIEW`, `flashV`, `flipAnim`, `C0`/`C1`), not a structural change. A 3-way brawl still is.]** Prior-art note: the v1.0 batch leans on earclacks-adjacent archetypes (sword, shuriken with ricochet, …) — SPEC.md's Prior art section may need a sentence when the roster settles.
7. **Weapon art convention — CLOSED 2026-10-27.** The lock text lives prescriptively in this file's Weapon-art section; the ratification record and realized mark map live in the close entry in **Measured facts**. Nothing here is open any more.

8. **Vocabulary at engine time — ANSWERED early (2026-10-28, owner word "renmae now").** The sim-internal `stone` was renamed to **`shot`** ahead of the Phase 8 rebuild rather than at it: 425 tokens across 11 live files, behaviour-identical (see the rename entry in **Measured facts** for the two-half neutrality proof). Per-shot on-screen names stayed (`arrow`, `orb`, …). What remains for the engine port: nothing — the sim, the harnesses and the audience word now agree. Dated records keep the old word by design, as does the frozen bench's surviving generator + smoke (the bench page itself retired at the 2026-10-28 checkpoint clean; the owner holds a copy).
9. **Later:** 3-man brawl mode, engine type scale, intro/outro designs, audio palette (the parry clang, the K.O. boom, and per-shot-type cues are the obvious ones), weapon reach vs arena bounds (hammer-class reach ≈ 231px as played vs the 500 half-width — **unreconciled since 2026-10-28: the shipped polys compute 213.6 for the hammer with the canonical's own `REACH` formula, and the full 22-weapon reach table is in the length-band entry in Measured facts** —**NARROWED 2026-10-28 (authoring-scale pass):** every sim-space explanation is now ruled out — all 22 `REACH` values are ≤ 213.6 including the chain path bounds, the pop-in is clamped (`pop = Math.min(1, fightPop)`) so the rest silhouette is the largest the art ever draws, and the boot equalization factors are ≤ 1.1% (the hammer was authored *shorter* — len 110, 143 px as played — than today's 147.5, so the pre-2026-10-21 spine cannot explain it either). But 231 / 213.6 = **1.0815**, and the renderer's one such multiplier is the disc scale **`U = OUT / CFG.size`**: at dpr 2 with a ~541 cssPx layout box (a window ~565 px wide or ~776 px tall) `U` ≈ 1.08 and the hammer's tip draws 230.7 **device** px from the body centre. Best available reading: the figure was measured **on screen, not in sim px** — it needs the owner's window size to confirm. The sim-space arithmetic behind check 6 stands either way: a body centre can sit 436 px out (500 − `bodyR`) and the hammer's tip then reaches 649.6, so blades do overhang the rim and the boundary decision is real.— CONTRACT compliance check 6 will refuse over-bounds action at engine time; the boundary decision joins Phase 3 layout work; the 2026-10-21 length ceiling caps this for every future weapon; the same day's preview margin-band experiment — 150 units so weapon ink past the square never cuts, owner: "weapons visible at all times" — was built, proven renderer-only, and reverted the same day for its zoom-out feel, then resolved in final form as the **overflow band canvas** (second canvas, same scale, ring-clipped: weapons visible at all times with zero zoom), which is the standing preview precedent for check 6's engine-era ink-in-margin question).
10. **Body-solidity for no-damage spines — considered and REJECTED (owner, 2026-10-21).** The proposal: make launcher- and shield-class spines solid to enemy bodies (a barrier identity — "the bow keeps bodies off the archer", a purpose for the shield), analysed including its fortress consequence (spin growth = fire rate AND armor: a grown Speedy-bow rings off approaches past ω ≈ 20–30). Owner verdict: "no i think it is bad, viewers should be able to understand why bows etc dont do damage in close combat" — the shove-without-damage pass-through is precisely what teaches the projectile rule on screen; a body-barring ring would muddy that lesson. No-damage spines stay pass-through with latch + knock; the shield's purpose remains open question 4. Recorded so it is never re-proposed as new. **[SUPERSEDED 2026-11-02 by the owner's own `allcut-r62` ruling — no-damage spines no longer exist, so the pass-through lesson this protected is moot; the solidity REJECTION itself was never reversed (spines still ride the ordinary latch + knock + resolver, no barrier ring). History stands.]**
