## Measured facts

**[content removed — `git show docs-precleanup3:docs/GAMES.md`]**

**Base damage 5 → 1 across the roster, build `dmg1-r21` (2026-10-28 — owner ruling "dmg base for all is 1, and hp 100 stays the same"; a sanctioned sim-data change and the balance pass's first landing).** The ruling's intent, in the owner's words: a test of whether long uncapped-escalation games are good — "the idea is the game becomes more and more ridiculous with no cap to scaling, and then it end in pure chaos, lets imagine spear geting longer each hit, suddenly it is hilariously long, which is exciting for viewers to watch, this is just a thought we can always change it". The edit is 22 `dmg: 5` fields → `dmg: 1` plus the build stamp (`pause-r20` → `dmg1-r21` in the `<title>`, the `#build` label and one comment cross-reference): the **inverse-diff against the pre-change canonical is EMPTY**, so those 25 lines are the entire change. hp 100, ω 3.5, every growth increment (0.7 / 0.9 / 1.6), all geometry and all pacing dials untouched.
**Measured in this environment (fast tier; the sanctioned `measure.mjs` matrix run remains owed — `measure.mjs` never runs without the owner's go).** (a) **Rounds with a melee damage-dealer got 1.14–1.50× longer:** `cellscan` totals 172,837 → 200,414 ticks (1.16×), worst cell hammer+dagger 122 s → 183 s, parries 311 → 402 (longer rounds, more clangs). (b) **Pure-launcher mirrors stopped terminating:** `projectile-fix`'s recorded termination red went **4/18 → 9/18 battles with no winner in 300 s**, and the failures are exactly the both-sides-ranged cells — **bow+crossbow 3/3, staff+wand 3/3, wand+bow 2/3, boomerang+shuriken 1/3** — while every cell with a melee damage-dealer still ends (staff+hammer: hammer wins 94–143 s; wand+sword: sword wins 84–146 s). `cellscan`'s bow+crossbow cell sits at exactly 50,400 ticks in BOTH builds, i.e. it was already pinned at the harness cap at damage 5: this class was marginal before the ruling and is now solidly stuck. Mechanism (analysis, not measured): both denial channels scale faster than the damage channel — shot-vs-shot cancels grow with the square of the shots in flight and swats grow with the defender's blade speed, while a landing is now worth 1 instead of 5, so a mirror can settle into an equilibrium where HP never drains. (c) **Naturals turned pathological: 0 → 4 of 20 seeds** (15, 34, 37, 40; wall > 20 s or aborted), seed 40 hitting natscan's 420 s cap **with no winner** (at damage 5 it finished in 253 s); natscan wall time 76 s → 227 s. (d) **The chaos the ruling was after did arrive, and its shape is VOLUME, not size:** maxSpin across those four seeds went **23.5 → 103.5 rad/s** (the `omegaCap` is 140), which at the base fire-rate law is **16.5 shots per second**, and maxShots in flight went 5–10 → **15–28**. Sim cost per tick roughly tripled (0.19 → 0.70 ms/tick, maxK 27–34 → **63–68** substeps) without touching the `maxSub 512` brake. For scale: at ω 103.5 a heavy-four tip moves ≈21,600 px/s against a body chaos ceiling of 1,200 — the readability ceiling discussed before the ruling, now reached in natural play. (e) **Attribution — the fingerprint delta is damage-driven, not roll-driven:** `rollattr` over seeds 11–20 gives **identical per-round weapon rolls for 8/10 seeds**, and the two exceptions (13, 15 — both boomerang mirrors) differ only because the battle no longer finishes inside rollattr's 150 s window, so its later rounds are never rolled. Tick columns differ throughout, as expected.
**Gates:** chromecheck PASS (legend 831 B) · inscribe GATE: PASS · killstill, leadproof, flipproof, reveal-probe ALL PASS (leadproof now recomputes 283 hits / 286 parries / 41 swats from stored fields — more of everything, same laws) · arena-test-smoke PASS off the untouched frozen bench page · `node --check` 22/22. **Display checks:** HP draws `Math.ceil(f.hp)` and the legend's DMG tell `Math.round(f.dmg)`, both already fractional-safe, and no harness hardcodes a damage value. One cosmetic consequence: for Speedy + any launcher the legend's DMG reads **1 for the whole round** (that combo's damage never grows), so its growth tell lives entirely in SPD.
**Fingerprints:** all 8 cells moved with labels unchanged (correct for a sim-data landing); the standing baseline is now **`.qa/fp-dmg1-r21.json`** with `.qa/fp-shotvocab.json` kept as the before-half of the pair. Canonical md5 `000719a4…` → **`293829002479b4b6e05a856a188d624e`**. `scratch/arena-test.html` is now two builds behind (a frozen `pause-r20` dmg-5 snapshot); its generator stays DORMANT and its `MD5_FROZEN` deliberately records the shot-vocab canonical rather than this one, so the bench cannot be silently rebuilt against a sim it was never drawn from.
**What this settles, what it re-opens.** (*Amended 2026-10-28, later the same day: what it re-opened — the termination question — was closed by owner ruling as deliberate-until-skills; see open question 1 and the facing-pass entry. The measurements below stand as the pre-skills baseline.*) Settles: base damage is a genuinely free dial — one number per weapon, no geometry, hitbox or bake consequence, and the whole landing is 25 lines. Re-opens with measurements attached: **open question 1** (projectile-mirror length) is now a *termination* question, and **open question 5**'s "boomerang persistence — empirically settled" was settled **at damage 5** only (both amended in place). The blocking decision for keeping base damage 1 is the owner's item-5 pick — a round ending (timer + tiebreak) or a pressure ramp (floor / gravity / rim) — because without one, roughly half the launcher-mirror matchups and 4/20 naturals have no finish. (**Superseded 2026-10-28 by the deliberate-until-skills ruling: neither is being built — see open question 1.**) Note also that the ruling's *spectacle* goal ("spear getting longer each hit") is not served by damage alone: nothing in the sim grows length today, and the measured escalation channel at damage 1 is projectile volume at 16.5 shots/s, which reads as a strobe rather than as comedy. The cheap visible axes (shot radius, shot count, knock, bounded bounces) remain the recommended route, per the pre-landing discussion.

**The length band — floor 54 (the orb), ceiling named for the spear, both ends boot-gated (2026-10-28, balance pass item 2; owner rulings "max length is spear" then "length stays the same, capped at spears length", floor pick 54, and yes to enforcing both ends at boot; no length changed, no fingerprints moved).** Doctrine: every weapon's authored spine sits between **`LEN_MIN` 54** — the orb's len, the shortest weapon shipped, so the floor bans nothing today and exists only to stop a future commission delivering something too short to touch — and the ceiling **`LT`, named for the SPEAR**. The naming is a clarification, not a change: the heavy four (hammer, axe, spear, mace) have been force-equalized to one shared number since 2026-10-21, so the spear sits exactly at the ceiling and nothing exceeds it, and the crossbow (cut down from 116 that day) and the javelin sit at it too. `LT`'s numeric source deliberately stays the **mace's tip, 113.44 pre-scale** (147.5 as played), within **0.06 units** of the spear's authored 113.38 — re-pointing it would be a real sim change (six weapons 0.05% shorter, fingerprints moved, hitbox re-bake) for no gameplay difference, and the ruling was explicit: "length stays the same". The band is **hold-blind by len**, as ruled ("that is for both radial and tangential"): it governs the drawn spine, NOT reach — reach still spreads ~94 px (tome) to ~214 px (hammer) across the two hold classes, because a radial spine adds to the grip offset while a tangential chord is centred on the rim and reaches out only by Pythagoras. That spread is class identity, not a cap violation, and it is the number any future length-growth axis would have to be bounded by (a reach band, when length growth exists).
Implementation: `const LEN_MIN = 54` above the `weaponScale` boot loop plus two loud throws inside it, checked **pre-scale** against `W.len` — the same mechanism as the existing `lead`/`headLead` boot guards. The convention became a gate because the crossbow silently overshot the ceiling in 2026-10-21 and only the owner's eye caught it.
Proofs: the canonical boots clean (chromecheck PASS, inscribe GATE: PASS) and **fingerprints 8/8 BYTE-IDENTICAL** vs `.qa/fp-dmg1-r21.json` — no shipped weapon sits outside the band, so the guard is behaviour-neutral by construction. Both ends were then proven LIVE rather than dead code by staging two throwaway pages (dotted, deleted in the same command): orb `len 40` → `Error: orb: len 40 is under the length floor 54 (the orb — shortest shipped weapon) — STOP`; dagger `len 130` → `Error: dagger: len 130 exceeds the length ceiling LT 113.44 (the spear's length; the heavy four are equalized to it) — STOP`. Canonical md5 `293829002479b4b6e05a856a188d624e` → **`ab17bdf05ab701cd363fad75720482b1`**; no stamp bump (behaviour-identical — the cleanup and rename precedent; the on-screen build stays `dmg1-r21`).
Recorded here because this pass measured it — **the roster's exact reach figures**, computed with the canonical's own `REACH` formula from the shipped polys: hammer 213.6 · axe 212.1 · mace 210.5 · spear 210.4 · scythe 209.4 · katana 209.2 · nunchaku ≈208.6 · flail ≈206.4 (chain weapons use the path bound, so these two are straight-pose lower bounds) · sword 195.5 · staff 193.1 · dagger 164.3 · orb 153.9 · boomerang 144.3 · wand 139.6 · knives 139.3 · bow 131.5 · crossbow 131.5 · shuriken 113.0 · javelin 101.9 · shield 95.9 · scroll 94.1 · tome 94.0 px. Two consequences worth keeping: within the len-equalized heavy four the reach order is set by **head width**, not length (hammer ±47.0 and axe ±54.6 lateral against the spear's ±12.8 point), and **the longest weapons by len are not the longest by reach** — the javelin and crossbow tie for the longest spine (147.5) and sit third- and fifth-from-bottom in reach, while the orb (shortest spine, 70.2) out-reaches nine weapons. That is exactly why the band is on len and any future fairness bound has to be on reach. **Unreconciled, recorded not guessed:** open question 9's "hammer-class reach ≈ 231px as played" does not match the 213.6 computed from the shipped polys; the ~17px gap is unexplained (candidates: the figure predates the 2026-10-21 `REACH` fix, or it quotes a chain weapon's path bound rather than the hammer's). It is quoted in a CONTRACT-compliance context, so it deserves a real reconciliation.
**The authoring-scale pass — weapon defaults, a derived tuck, and the placement/reach rules published (2026-10-28; owner ruling "i want you to do 1 and 2" on the two recommendations that came out of the "wouldn't one shared dimension be easier?" question — the goal being quoted verbatim: "my goal is to make it easier to scale as at one point we are introducing new weapon and if all have similar dimenasion/starting points its a lot easier to design". Behaviour-identical: fingerprints 8/8 byte-identical, resolved weapon+geometry data md5-identical.)** The ruling that framed it: equalizing *dimensions* was declined (it would delete the only varying melee stat and the closed art convention for ~5–10 lines), so the standardization went where it actually helps an author — the **frame and the boilerplate**, not the numbers.
**Item 2 (code).** `omega: 3.5` and `dmg: 1` were written out on all 22 rows: **44 literals, zero variance**, pure copy-paste that a new weapon's author had to reproduce correctly — a forgotten `omega` reads `undefined` (NaN spin), a stale `5` silently resurrects the pre-2026-10-28 damage build. They are now `const WDEF = { omega: 3.5, dmg: 1 }` spread **first** into every row, so a weapon that ever needs a different rate overrides in place (`{ ...WDEF, name: 'X', omega: 4, … }`). They are POLICY constants, not per-weapon design: uniform spin and base dmg 1 against hp 100 are owner rulings. `tuck: false` joined the derived set the same day — a tangential chord never tucks (its art straddles the rim, so the clip would notch it), which is why all nine tangential rows used to repeat the field; the render pass now reads it off `hold` (`tuck !== false && hold !== 'tangential'`) and the nine declarations are gone, leaving only handle-less **radial** art to opt out by name (the orb). **A new weapon therefore declares just: name, len, hold when tangential, ability, line, shot when it launches, poly.**
Item 2 also corrected a **stale placement claim inside the orb's own comment**: "at weaponScale 1.3 the orb centre orbits ≈ 70px out, its inner edge ≈ 49px vs bodyR 64, so the orb now overlaps the disc by ≈ 15px and draws over it" omits the radial **mount offset** `bodyR × SPINE_R0` = 61.44 px that every radial row's art is drawn from — the same omission that made the reach figures look like they were measured from a centre-drawn weapon. Measured: orb centre **131.6 px** out, inner edge **110.8** vs rim 64 → **46.84 px clear**, nothing overlaps, nothing draws over the disc, and its `tuck: false` is a no-op today (kept as insurance). The comment's advice "raise `len` to ≈ 65 for a just-touching look" also pointed the wrong way — +1 `len` = +1.3 px of orbit, so 65 would widen the gap to ≈ 61 px — which matters now that `len` is floored at 54 and **54 is the orb's value**: bringing the orb closer to the body needs a *mount* rule for handle-less art (a per-weapon mount radius, or mounting detached art from the rim instead of the grip), an owner decision, not a `len` tweak. The owner's playtest words stay verbatim; only the arithmetic is amended, in place, with the date.
**Proofs (behaviour-identical).** The resolved weapon table plus geometry — all 22 rows: name, len, omega, dmg, hold, ability, line, shot, chain, lead/headLead, **every polygon point and every covering circle at 9-decimal precision**, plus `REACH`/`SPAN`/`MINR`/`POPC` per weapon and `LT`/`LEN_MIN`/`bodyR`/`weaponScale`/`hp` — hashes to the **same md5 `4543b804cab4e911b5a2061e58c986ce`** (96,042 bytes) before and after. (`tuck` is the one field excluded from that hash, because it is the one field this landing intentionally removes — from 9 rows; its render consequence is proven separately, next sentence.) The render **clip predicate is unchanged for all 22 weapons** (tangential: no clip before via `tuck: false`, no clip after via `hold`; radial: clip both). **Fingerprints 8/8 byte-identical** vs the standing baseline `.qa/fp-dmg1-r21.json` (after-half kept as `.qa/fp-authoring.json`). Gates: chromecheck PASS (bug 219 B; the legend byte count varies run-to-run **on the same file** — 819/829 before, 810/817 after — the known chrome-layer nondeterminism, not a regression) · inscribe GATE: PASS (all 22, chain ties and bent-pose dots-off-ink 0/56 and 0/24) · killstill 4/4 ok · leadproof ALL PASS (283 hits, 286 parries, 41 swats recomputed from stored fields) · flipproof ALL PASS · reveal-probe ALL PASS (Parts A and B) · arena-test-smoke SMOKE PASS off the frozen page. Numeric harnesses: cellscan and projectile-fix outputs **identical**; natscan identical on every sim field (ticks, winner, score, maxK, maxSpin, maxShots, round weapons, pathological seeds 15/34/37/40) with only wall-clock and ms/tick jitter (229 s vs 231 s).
Canonical md5 `ab17bdf05ab701cd363fad75720482b1` → **`e5a295f7c30bfb234e703e538be3b9fa`**; **no stamp bump** (behaviour-identical — the cleanup, rename and length-band precedent; the on-screen build stays `dmg1-r21`). `scratch/arena-test.mjs`'s `MD5_FROZEN` deliberately stays at `000719a4…`: it anchors the last canonical whose *behaviour* matches the frozen bench snapshot (dmg 5, shot vocab), and this landing moved no behaviour — the DORMANT guard still fires first.
**Item 1 (docs).** The placement and reach rules now live where a future commission reads them: a new **Placement & reach** bullet in the closed convention (both rim mounts with exact numbers; the 2.56 px hidden-ink tuck and "detail budget from the guard outwards"; the two closed-form reach formulas and their measured bands — radial 206–214 at the ceiling, tangential ~102; the boot equalization factors so no author aims at `LT` by hand; the clip-can't-hide-a-hitbox argument; and the tangential rim-straddle spread recorded as an **OPEN owner pick**, since the shipped nine disagree from entirely-outside to 7.8 px inside), the **Hold styles** section amended (exact mount numbers added; the stale "all carry `tuck: false`" claim replaced by the derivation and the row list completed to nine), **open question 9's ≈ 231 px** figure narrowed (every sim-space explanation ruled out; 231/213.6 = 1.0815 matches the renderer's disc scale `U = OUT / CFG.size`, so the figure was most likely taken on screen in device px), and the README's kit-rebuild clause now requires the rebuilt artist kit to carry these rules. Measured alongside it, and now recorded in the convention: the radial mount is **identical for all 13 radial rows** (11 at exactly r 61.44; the axe 0.45 px forward of `d` 0 at r 61.89; the orb detached at r 110.84), so the "are some 3, 4 or 10 px inside?" failure mode does not exist — it is one constant, not 13 numbers.
**The facing pass — the dagger and the staff join the edge-leading mirror, build `lead-r22` (2026-10-28) — the rest RETIRED VERBATIM (removed from HEAD 2026-11-11; recover with `git show docs-precleanup:docs/CHRONICLE-archive.md`). THE LOAD-BEARING RESIDUE STAYS HERE: this is the build the standing fingerprint baseline `.qa/fp-lead-r22.json` was taken on — 8 cells — and every behaviour-identical edit since proves against it. A sanctioned sim change: fingerprints moved only on cells fielding a newly flagged weapon, and the stamp bumped `dmg1-r21` → `lead-r22`.**
**Pre-skills cleanup — the launcher's four per-weapon special-cases become row data, and four stale comments are trued up (2026-10-28, owner pick "both, as one landing"; behaviour-identical, no stamp bump).** The window's mission in the owner's words: "i just want to make everything nice and clean before we start on skills", with the design motive recorded earlier the same day — "at one point we are introducing new weapon and if all have similar dimenasion/starting points its a lot easier to design". **Code.** `KNIFE_VOLLEY`, `SPELL_CYCLE` and `COPYW` are deleted and their content moved onto the rows: `W.shot` gains `revs` (revolutions of accumulated spin per volley, default 1 — knives 3), `volley` ([d, l, degrees] fan anchors — knives), `cycle` (kind rotation — the tome trio) and `copy` (spawn at the art centre — boomerang, shuriken, javelin). `f.spellIdx` is renamed `f.cycleIdx` (factory + per-round reset) because the state is no longer tome-specific, and the `kind` expression is hoisted above the fan/single branch so both paths share one reading. `fireShots` now contains **zero** `f.weapon === …` tests (verified by count: the last four are gone).
**The one real discovery — the fan anchors are WORLD px.** They were measured on the ×1.3-scaled art, and that is provable from the file: the anchors' d/l extents (17.69 … 83.71, −73.80) are *exactly* the boot-scaled knives poly's own dmin/dmax/lmin, while the art-local reading (÷1.3: d 13.61…64.39) does not contain them. They are therefore deliberately OUTSIDE the boot `weaponScale` loop, which scales `shot.r/off/len` — re-verified after the move: r 6→7.8, off 38→49.4, len 24→31.2, anchors untouched at 50.7/17.69/83.71. **Trap recorded in the row comment:** adding `volley` to that loop is the natural-looking consistency fix and would move every knife launch 1.3× out. **Two vestigial fields recorded, not removed:** knives `off: 38` (the fan branch recomputes its own anchors and never reads the radial offset) and tome `kind: 'page'` (the cycle supersedes it in the launcher, and HALO has had no 'page' tint since the 2026-10-25 kind split). Deleting either is a data change and owes its own proof. **Truth-up in the canonical.** Four art comments (javelin, tome, scroll, orb) claimed "no CIRCLES_V2 row on purpose — ghost until baked (watermark ART PREVIEW — HITBOXES UNBAKED: X, gate n=0 MISS-FAIL expected)".
All four are false in the shipped file — verified by count before amending: CIRCLES_V2 carries 22 rows against 22 WEAPON_IDS, none missing, none extra, so `UNBAKED` is empty and the watermark cannot draw. Each claim keeps its original wording (a true record of its delivery moment) with a dated bracket amendment, per the live-claims convention. The staff row's "Owner to playtest how the wrapped grip reads when it flips" is amended the same way to the confirmation ("i confirm staff is looking good"), the −1 sign standing. **Proofs (behaviour-identical).** (1) A launch-equivalence probe replayed the old hardcodes against the new row data for **22/22 rows × all four decisions** (revs, volley, cycle, copy) — identical. (2) **Fingerprints 8/8 BYTE-IDENTICAL** vs `.qa/fp-lead-r22.json` (md5 `f1de581b7ec18be83af57d94ca592e5a` on both sides). No new baseline file was written, deliberately: the standing baseline still describes this build's behaviour, and `.qa/fp-authoring.json` / `.qa/fingerprint.json` are already flagged as redundant copies awaiting the owner's ok to delete — a fourth copy would worsen that.
(3) A dedicated **shot-kind / launch-point probe** over 8 forced cells covering all ten launchers (bow+crossbow s3, wand+staff s5, shuriken+boomerang s7, knives+javelin s11, tome+scroll s13, knives+tome s17, javelin+shuriken s19, scroll+wand s23): **3464 launch records identical** on sid, owner, kind, x0, y0, r, len, turn, bounce and dmg. This probe exists because `shotFire` events carry no `kind`, so the fingerprints do not directly cover the hoisted cycle expression — the one part of the refactor a fingerprint diff could have missed. Kinds emitted identically on both sides: spell 528, knife 493, null 441, javelin 417, spark 391, star 285, bolt 219, orb 168, halo 135, nova 134, vortex 133, boom 112 — the trio in strict rotation, the fan giving three distinct launch points per volley. (4) **natscan**: all 30 seeds' sim fields identical (ticks, winner, maxK, maxSpin, maxShots, round list), total ticks **891,003 = 891,003**, no-winner naturals **33 and 36** unchanged; only wall-clock report fields moved (scan 225 s → 273 s under load, "pathological seeds" 15/34/40 → 15/33/34/36/37/40).
(5) **cellscan** identical. (6) **projectile-fix** identical: A 0 (melee-side hits 144, projectile landings 905, spine-shove latch edges 1490), B **0/11893** launches off the doctrine point — the fan's rim clamp sits inside that count, C 0/320771 budget ticks, D 9/18 (the recorded open-question-1 red). (7) Gates: chromecheck PASS (bug 219 B, legend 819 B), inscribe GATE: PASS, killstill / leadproof / flipproof / reveal-probe ALL PASS (leadproof 256/256 × 5 rows, dagger 86/86, staff 232/232, Part B 351 hits / 322 flagged, 421 parries / 559 sides, 99 swats / 57). Canonical md5 `8ad8e17325b24849b1f86efce5ebace5` → **`54ab0e5b784962987fdec6cc71cd6636`**, 3033 lines; **no stamp bump** — the on-screen build stays `lead-r22`, per the cleanup / rename / length-band / authoring precedent for behaviour-identical landings. `scratch/arena-test.html` stays three builds behind and its generator stays DORMANT (`MD5_FROZEN` deliberately anchors the last behaviour-matching canonical, not this one). **Why it matters for skills:** per-weapon shot behaviour now lives on the row next to `ability`, which is where the skills pass reads. A weapon that fans, cycles kinds or throws a copy of itself is a data edit; the launcher has no per-weapon knowledge left to grow.

**The spin ceiling measured — `omegaCap` 140 is dormant, the real ceiling is structural, and the sim is stable to ω ≈ 258 (2026-10-28, owner pick "measure the real limit first" out of four offered options; read-only, no code changed, canonical byte-identical before and after at `54ab0e5b784962987fdec6cc71cd6636`).** The owner met the item with "what is this ? i dont understand", so the finding is stated plainly: every damaging hit spins the winner faster, nothing in the *game* limits that, but the code carries a hard stop at 140 rad/s purely so the physics cannot tunnel — and the question was how close the game actually gets to it. **Method (three read-only measurements).** (1) *Analytic:* the sim's own substep-sizing rule, `k = ceil((subMargin + Σ(|vel| + ω·REACH))·dt / δ)` with δ = (MINR[a]+MINR[b])/2, clamped to `maxSub` 512 and dt = 1/120, solved per pair for the ω at which required k reaches 512 — all 484 ordered pairs. (2) *Empirical:* a staged copy with `omegaCap: 100000` (`.qa/.uncapped.html`, deleted afterwards; the canonical was never edited for this) run through natscan and projectile-fix and diffed against the capped runs.
(3) *Structural:* per-round accounting of hits, damage and spin on the seeds that peak. **Finding A — the clamp never engages.** With the clamp removed, natscan is **identical on 30/30 seeds** (total ticks 891,003 on both sides, no-winner naturals 33 and 36 on both sides, every maxSpin unchanged) and projectile-fix is **identical across all 18 forced cells** (A 0, B 0/11893 launches off doctrine, C **0/320771** budget-under-provisioned ticks, D 9/18). Nothing in the shipped game reaches 140 rad/s. **Finding B — why: the ceiling is HP, not the clamp.** A round ends when hp 100 is drained, which at base damage 1 is exactly **100 damaging hits**; the fastest spin axis is +1.0/hit (launcher ability 0.70 + Speedy 0.30), so the structural maximum is 3.5 + 100×1.0 = **103.5 rad/s** — precisely the observed peak, on 9 of 30 naturals (seeds 13, 15, 30, 33, 34, 35, 36, 37, 40). Verified per round: the peak rounds are pure-projectile (blade hits [0,0]) with the spinner's maxDmg pinned at 1.00 (Speedy + a launcher never grows damage) while its heavy opponent grew to 10.9–20.2. Reaching 140 needs **137 hits**, i.e.
hp ÷ damage above 137. Melee weapons cannot get close: their ability spin is 0.0, so Speedy + melee tops out at 3.5 + 100×0.30 = 33.5. **Finding C — the true stability limit.** Blade-budget saturation (required k = maxSub 512) across the 484 pairs: worst **axe+axe at ω 257.8** (δ 1.80, REACH sum 424.3), then axe+flail / flail+axe 276.0, hammer+axe / axe+hammer 278.6, axe+spear 288.0; p10 323.8, **median 424.6**, p90 765.8, loosest orb+orb 4346.5 (MINR 21.8 — a sphere has no thin circle). **No pair saturates below ω 140.** The projectile budget at ω 140 peaks at k = 63.3 (a bow shooting at an axe-wielding foe) = **12.4% of maxSub**, so blades bind, not shots. Body velocity is second-order (the ω·REACH term dominates, and `subMargin` 1200 already covers knock/push/bounce impulses); `CFG.cruise` no longer exists — the movement doctrine was reverted 2026-10-21 — and `maxSpeed` is 1200. **Reading of the three findings together.** The 36.5-hit headroom figure recorded earlier the same day was arithmetically right but framed wrong: the binding constraint is hp ÷ damage per hit, and the clamp is *conservative* rather than tight — it bites at 54% of the worst pair's stability limit and about a third of the median's.
**What would wake the clamp** (all skills-pass material): hp above ~137, damage per hit below 1, rounds long enough to land 137+ hits, or — the likely one — **a skill that grows spin without a damaging hit** (spin-per-parry, spin-over-time, spin-per-launch). Waking it would still be silent to the player unless a readout is added, but physics degradation would NOT be silent: `maxSub` engagement is flagged both by the audit (`G.auditK` stores [chosen, blade-required, projectile-required] per tick) and by projectile-fix's check C. **Recommendation recorded, decision left open:** raising 140 today would change nothing (proven dormant) and the measured band says the sim tolerates ~258 at its worst, so the useful trigger for revisiting is the first spin-without-damage skill; a *visible* cap, if the owner wants one for the show, is a legend/render change and not a sim change.

**Spell-family sigil legibility — the nucleus becomes a hitbox-sized carrier disc on an ink plate, build `sigil-r23` (2026-10-28; owner report verbatim: "i just watched the spells (shots) from tome and scroll, i think its very hard to see the sigils in the nucleous/center. dont code, what do you suggest? i want it to be more visible, but not ‘overpowering’"; owner pick "grow + solid mark", applied to all four spell-family kinds).** **Diagnosis, measured before proposing anything.** Four independent causes, not one. (a) *Size:* `drawPolySet` paints shot art 1:1 in world px and never scales it to `st.r`, and the delivered nucleus is 10.5 world px across = **5.68 css px** at a ~541 css px arena (U = 0.541 css px per world px, arena 1000 world px); the scroll's is a 4-point star waisted to **2.6 css px** at its arms. (b) *No keyline at all:* `drawPolySet` runs a full STROKE pass over every poly before any FILL pass, so the inner flame's fill paints straight over the nucleus's own 2 px ink — the carrier mark sat bare on the flame. (An earlier read of this claimed the ink was eating 1.08 css px of the nucleus; that was wrong, and is corrected here.) (c) *Contrast:* carrier against the inner flame layer is **1.60–2.32:1**, all eight carrier × kind pairs below the 3:1 distinctness bar — worst is the scroll's `spell` + Heavy at 1.60:1 (28° hue gap), and `vortex` + Speedy is a **1° hue gap** (only luminance separates them).
(d) *Dwell:* at 680 world px/s the shot crosses its own nucleus width in **15.4 ms ≈ 0.93 frames at 60 Hz**, which is why the fix had to be size and contrast and never detail. **Decided before code** (the owner’s "dont code, what do you suggest?" then "ok do that"), against the LIVE art data read out of the canonical at true scale. **No rasteriser exists in this sandbox, so nothing was ever seen rendered here — the owner’s eye in the game was the check.** A light plate was measured and rejected on the way:
it lifts the carrier only to 2.57–3.04:1 and then fails to separate from the bright flame itself (1.31–1.61:1), where the ink plate clears both boundaries. **What landed (renderer-only).** Per spell-family kind the single KC nucleus poly is replaced by two polys: a dark ink plate (`TH.botInk`, r = 1.3 × the hitbox) flagged `vis: 1`, then a plain 32-gon carrier disc whose radius **is** the launched hitbox — derived from `WEAPONS.scroll.shot.r` / `WEAPONS.tome.shot.r` rather than written as a literal, so it tracks `weaponScale` instead of drifting from it. Centres preserved from the delivered nuclei (spell (0,0), trio (+1.25,0)) so the composition does not shift. `build()` now passes an optional third tuple element through as `vis`, which activates the mechanism its own comment has described since 2026-10-22 ("Shapes flagged vis are paint only") but that **no shape in the file could previously reach** — that stale claim is amended in place. The flame layers stay delivery-verbatim; the retired nuclei are archived verbatim (tag `docs-precleanup`). **Why size the disc to the hitbox:** measured while auditing this — the `HALO` tint disc is **100% hidden** under the opaque flame art on all four kinds (sampled at 0.25 px × 3°: every sample point lies under an opaque flame layer), so the 2026-10-19 directive "every shot kind inks its own hitbox disc" is satisfied in code but invisible for the spell family.
The nucleus is therefore the only visible size cue these shots have, and it now carries the true radius. **Proofs.** (1) *Render-truth probe* (new, temporary): 4 kinds × 2 carriers **ALL PASS** — exactly one `arc` first (the tint, r 7.80, correct `HALO` colour), **3 strokes with the plate unstroked**, and 5 fills in the order tint → outer flame → inner flame → ink plate → carrier nucleus; plate r 10.15 = 1.3 × 7.8 and concentric with the nucleus to 0.0005 px; nucleus r 7.81 filled with `COLS[o]`. (2) *Sim surface identical:* 22 rows × 21 fields (every `shot` key plus len/omega/dmg/hold/tuck/lead/headLead/ability) and REACH/MINR/SPAN all unchanged before → after. (3) *Art envelope unchanged:* the bbox over every poly is identical for all nine kinds checked (the spell family stays x −50..15.6, y ±14.8), so **no silhouette grew** — the whole change is inside the outline that already ships, which is what keeps it from reading as "overpowering". (4) **Fingerprints 8/8 BYTE-IDENTICAL** vs the standing `.qa/fp-lead-r22.json` (md5 `f1de581b7ec18be83af57d94ca592e5a` on both sides); no new baseline file, per the authoring and cleanup precedent.
Structurally guaranteed as well as measured: `.vis` is read only inside `drawPolySet`, and `SHOTS_ART` has no sim consumer (`drawShot`, `setShotArt`, `shotArtOf`). (5) *Gates:* chromecheck PASS (bug 219 B, legend 822 B), inscribe GATE: PASS, killstill / leadproof / flipproof / reveal-probe ALL PASS. (6) *Tie-check against the pick:* de-scaling the shipped "grow + solid mark" nucleus gives 32 points matching the landed `sigDisc(1.25, 0, 7.8, 32)` to **0.0075 world px worst-case** (0.004 css px), plate r 10.142 vs 10.14 — what the owner picked is what shipped. natscan / cellscan / projectile-fix were **not** re-run: with the sim surface proven field-identical and 8/8 fingerprints identical there is nothing for them to move (the authoring-pass precedent). **After:** carrier-on-ink **4.23:1** (Speedy) / **5.02:1** (Heavy), plate-on-inner-flame **8.02–9.82:1**, nucleus 5.68 → **8.44 css px** across. **Measured bound on any future tuning:** the plate is fully inside the *bright inner* flame on all four kinds (0/720 boundary samples outside) but with only **+0.35 px** clearance on the scroll and +0.48 px on the trio; past that the plate edge starts meeting the darker outer layer, where ink-on-flame is 2.93:1 (spell) / 4.19:1 (vortex) instead of 8.02–9.82:1 — so 1.3× is close to the ceiling for this treatment and a bolder plate should be re-measured, not dialled.
**Also disclosed:** the kit route (`APP.setShotArt`) replaces a kind's whole poly list, so any future kit delivery for a spell-family kind must carry its own plate + nucleus pair or this treatment is lost — added to the kit-rebuild work already owed. Canonical md5 `54ab0e5b784962987fdec6cc71cd6636` → **`c4483833621f5f01353cb821d5ca11b2`**, 3065 lines; stamp **`lead-r22` → `sigil-r23`** (title + the on-screen build label) — a *visible* art landing bumps the stamp per the `sweep-`/`roster-r12` precedent, while behaviour-identical landings do not.

**The skills model ruled — M2, two languages: a weapon's ability is data, a fighter's skill is a hook (2026-10-28, owner ruling verbatim: "M2", picking the model question as the pass's first decision; owner pick the same turn on the parry interaction: "Forbid it — parry rewards nothing, full stop").** Analysis only — **no code changed, no plumbing added, canonical untouched** (md5 `86ff2d5bffc6a3942e6c9eba12ccef09`, build `sigil-r23`, 3083 lines; the doctrine subsection, the two answered open questions and this entry are the whole landing). **The ruling.** Ability = weapon data, a growth rate read at `grow()`; skill = a CONTRACT hook — reads player + small context, returns a modified value, never mutates, never depends on another player's hook, modifies an existing mechanic and never introduces one, may consume the seeded RNG but never create or seed one, leaves time-dependent state to the game's per-tick logic; one skill per player per game. **Adopted lazily by design:** CONTRACT's optionality rule keeps `Speedy {spin: 0.30}` and `Heavy {dmg: 0.9}` legal as shipped, so the hook indirection arrives with the **first non-numeric skill** and not before — the anti-over-engineering path, and the reason this ruling costs nothing today. **What it settles.** Open questions **2 and 3 are ANSWERED in place**: parry rewards nothing and no skill may reward a parry, which removes spin-per-parry (the spin-ceiling measurement's likeliest `omegaCap` waker) and leaves damaging hit / time / launch as the only growth triggers — the latter two contextual, so either forces a real hook. **What it forces.** Since a hook may not introduce a mechanic, poison, frost, comet burn, damage-over-time and runtime length growth are **game-layer** work first and hook targets second; the pass has two layers, and the tome trio's "CONTENT" ruling is partly a game-mechanics decision rather than a skills one. **Measured grounds for M2 over M1** (read out of the canonical, not assumed): `grow()` is the only sim consumer of `.skill`/`.ability`; abilities are binary across all 22 weapons (12 × `{spin: 0, dmg: 0.7}`, 10 × `{spin: 0.70, dmg: 0}`); `SKILL_SHORT` derives its legend label binary-wise, so a both-axes / neither / non-numeric skill is silently mislabelled "+DMG / Hit"; the trackers are exactly `G.maxSpin` + `G.maxDmg` against a fixed three-channel legend; and a shot inherits exactly one per-fighter quantity at birth (`dmg: f.dmg`), with `r`/`speed`/`len`/`turn`/`bounce` reading the weapon row — which is why per-fighter shot axes are the cheap spectacle. **Still open, in gating order:** additive stacking vs override · whether skills are displayed at all · which mechanics are authorized as hook targets (length growth, per-fighter shot multipliers, knock — today a global `CFG.hitKnock` 130) · the spin cap (140, silent, 36.5 hits of headroom at +1.00/hit; stability limit ω ≈ 258) · whether elemental effects enter the game layer in this pass. **Owed alongside, unchanged:** the sanctioned `measure.mjs` run on the owner's machine (`.qa/chainlaw.mjs` FIRST), best run once before and once after this pass — its 19 forced cells miss the dagger and the staff and its expected counts predate both damage-1 and the facing pass — and the kit-route rebuild, which a skill only needs if it touches art.

**The five M2 rulings — stacking adds, skills are shown in the legend, three hook targets authorized, the spin cap left alone, elemental effects into the game layer (2026-10-28, owner answers verbatim: "1. it adds / 2. they are shown in the legend. for instance right now we have weapon name and then the skill, its just a short sentence or few word, for instance with a spear 'gets longer' or something similar. / 3. yes / 4. yes" — item 4 clarified by pick: leave `omegaCap` 140 as it is and revisit when a spin-flavoured skill exists — "/ 5. elemental effects yes, as you pointed out, posions,frost and comet/fire, we will figer it out later").** Analysis only — **no code changed, no plumbing added, canonical untouched** (md5 `86ff2d5bffc6a3942e6c9eba12ccef09`, build `sigil-r23`, 3083 lines): this entry and the doctrine subsection's new "Settled under M2" paragraph are the whole landing. **What each ruling costs.** *(1) Stacking adds* → the numeric site is already correct, so nothing moves until a hook modifies a different mechanic. *(2) Legend labels* → an authoring change, **not** a layout change:
the legend already renders one skill cell per fighter, so `SKILL_SHORT`'s binary derivation is replaced by an authored few-word line and the silent-mislabel defect dies with it; the SPD/DMG numeric cell is untouched, and a new *numeric* axis would still owe the layout change the cost map priced. *(3) Hook targets authorized* (length growth, per-fighter shot multipliers, knock) → game-layer work first, each with its own probe; length growth remains the only axis needing engineering (boot-derived SPAN/REACH/scaled polys/covering circles, the invalidated REACH prune bound and MINR substep budget, and a ceiling that must live inside the growth rule because the LEN_MIN/LT gate cannot catch runtime growth), shot multipliers are one line per axis at the birth site, knock is one hit-time read of a per-fighter field against today's global `CFG.hitKnock` 130. *(4) Spin cap left alone* → no sim change, no kept pair, no re-baseline; the headroom and stability figures in the spin-ceiling entry stand as measured. *(5) Elemental effects authorized into the game layer, design deferred* → poison, frost and comet/fire become game mechanics before any skill can modify them (CONTRACT hook rule 4), shapes to be figured out later; the shot accents already pre-figure them (KF1/KF2 fire comet · KP1/KP2 poison · KN1 frost · KV1/KV2 fire-orange).
**Next:** the skill list for Speedy and Heavy — and whether the cast grows — since the legend labels, the authorized hook targets and the elemental shapes all depend on which skills actually exist. The dagger's axis (⚠ "skills pass decides") is now a cheap *data* ruling under M2: an ability is weapon data, so it is one number and no plumbing. **[RULED 2026-10-28: +0.7 DMG stands — and it cost no code, being the shipped value.]** **Measured correction, caught while grounding the skill list (this entry, row 29 and the doctrine subsection were corrected in place):** the knock field is **`CFG.hitKnock: 130`**, not `CFG.knock` as the skills-prep cost map wrote and these entries repeated — the identifier was wrong, the value and the analysis were not. The same read-out settled a structural fact the skill list turns on: `CFG.hitKnock` has exactly two consumers, `bladeVsBody` (melee) and `shotHitBody` (projectile), so **knock is the only cheap axis that is live on all 22 weapons** — the launch-snapshot axes (`r`/`speed`/`len`/`turn`/`bounce`) are inert on the 12 melee weapons, and runtime length growth is inert on the 10 projectile ones. A skill built on a class-dependent axis is therefore dark for roughly half the roster, which CONTRACT's optionality rule permits (the base value passes through) but the legend label does not: "gets longer" on a bow says nothing true.

**Axis placement ruled — class-dependent axes go on the weapon, universal axes on the fighter, so no legend label can ever be dark (2026-10-28, owner pick: "Design it out — weapons carry class-dependent rates", settling the dark-label fork before any skill was chosen because it constrains the whole list).** Analysis only — **no code changed, no chrome changed, canonical untouched** (md5 `86ff2d5bffc6a3942e6c9eba12ccef09`, build `sigil-r23`, 3083 lines): this entry and the doctrine subsection's new **Axis placement** paragraph, plus a re-key note on ruling 3 so the live doctrine stays coherent, are the whole landing. **The rule.** An axis whose effect depends on weapon **class** is weapon-row **data** — a rate like `ability: {spin, dmg}`, consumed by a game-layer mechanic: the launch-snapshot axes (`r`/`speed`/`len`/`turn`/`bounce`, the 10 projectile weapons) and runtime length growth (the 12 melee ones). A fighter's **skill** takes only what is live on all 22 weapons (spin growth, damage growth, knock) or a class-independent contextual trigger. **Correction, caught on re-read the same turn:** this entry and the doctrine paragraph first listed per-launch as class-independent, which is wrong — only the 10 projectile weapons ever launch a shot, so a launch-triggered fighter skill is dark on the 12 melee ones; the class-independent contextual triggers are per-time and conditional-on-state only — **per-launch is class-dependent** (only the 10 projectile weapons ever launch), so a launch-triggered effect belongs on the weapon row, not on a fighter. **Why it wins.** Weapons are dice-rolled per round, so a fighter's identity must survive the roll; a class-dependent skill flickers on 12/22 or 10/22 of them, while a weapon-keyed rate never does because its line is read only while that weapon is held. **What it buys.** Dark labels become impossible, so the legend needs no conditional wording and no dimmed state — ruling 2's authored few-word skill line is always true and this ruling owes **no chrome work**. Measured support: `CFG.hitKnock` has exactly two consumers (`bladeVsBody`, `shotHitBody`); the shot birth site snapshots `r`/`speed`/`len`/`turn`/`bounce` from `W.shot` and only `dmg` from the fighter; the legend re-renders per frame and already resolves the held weapon; chromecheck asserts legend structure only (`ccard`, `table`, `qn`) and reports byte counts without asserting them. **What it re-keys.** The length-growth spectacle ("gets longer") belongs to the **spear** and any other melee weapon given a length rate, not to a fighter — either fighter gets it when they hold that weapon, and it is still built in this pass. M2 is untouched by this: weapon rates are data, the mechanic is game-layer, only the fighter's skill is a hook. **Rejected with reasons recorded** so the fork is not re-litigated: conditional label wording (free, but dark on half the rolls and unexplained to the audience) and dimming the label while dark (cheap and idiomatic — the legend already carries `class="out"` and `wp idle`/"rolling…" — but unnecessary once no dark label can exist); both stay available if a specific skill is ever judged worth the flicker. **Next:** the skill list — which universal axes and contextual triggers Speedy and Heavy get, and whether the cast grows. The dagger's axis (⚠ "skills pass decides") remains a one-number data ruling. **[DISCHARGED 2026-10-28: +0.7 DMG ratified, no sim change.]**

**The weapon row's `ability` renamed to `trait` — vocabulary only, behaviour-identical (2026-10-28, owner word verbatim: "ok we cahnge it to trait for the weapons", after raising the confusion himself: "we need to get some proper terminology for the 'bot skill' and the 'weapon skill', as i get confused"; his first proposal was fighter = "passive" with the weapon taking "skill" or "ability").** **The ruling as landed: fighter = SKILL (unchanged), weapon = TRAIT (renamed from `ability`), and never the bare nouns in prose — always *fighter skill* / *weapon trait*.** **Why the swap was rejected, with reasons recorded.** (a) `skill` is not a game-local word to move: SPEC's glossary defines it — "What makes a player behave differently. A set of optional, passive **hooks** into one game's mechanics … Exactly one skill per player per game" — and CONTRACT §Skills carries it across every future game and the Phase 8 player pool, so giving it to the weapon would make the platform's term mean two things in one repo. (b) "passive" cannot discriminate here: SPINCLASH has **no player input at all** — both combatants are bots and every mechanic is automatic — so the weapon's rate is exactly as passive as the fighter's, and the word is already spent as the adjective inside SPEC's own definition ("optional, passive hooks").
(c) The confusion was legitimate and baked in: "skill" and "ability" are near-synonyms in ordinary game vocabulary, and the live Core contact law already carried the collision — one clause read "Future weapon skills" while the next reached for "a named, documented weapon trait". That clause is now "Future weapon traits"; the owner's own 2026-10-16 quote beside it ("special weapon skills") stays verbatim as his words. **Scope, measured.** Canonical: **30** word-boundary sites — 22 weapon rows, 1 inline comment ("axe ability"), 5 block comments, and 2 code sites (`grow()`'s `WEAPONS[f.weapon].ability` and the legend `brief` derivation `W.ability.spin`) — plus a 10-line VOCABULARY comment above `WDEF` recording the rename and its discipline. `ingest.mjs`: **1** site, the dormant SVG gate's emitted row template — **a coupling that would have silently produced a traitless weapon** on any future ingest, since `WDEF` defaults only `{omega, dmg}`. Docs: 26 occurrences across GAMES.md's live regions (roster table header, the reveal-poster and legend doctrine, the growth/spin doctrine, the M2 subsection, open questions 1 and 3, the vocabulary map, the testing recipe's ingest caveat) and 2 in SPEC.md's frame description.
**Dated entries (14) and the removed chronicle archive (7) keep the old word verbatim**, exactly as they keep `stone`; CONTRACT's "no ability registry" is platform prose about registries, not this field, and stays. **No harness reads the field by name** — the two apparent grep hits were "applicability" and "readability" — so nothing in `.qa/` or `scratch/` needed touching. **Identity.** Canonical md5 `86ff2d5bffc6a3942e6c9eba12ccef09` → **`78bb56e0bd5fdecf1877f28e60617f82`**, 3083 → 3093 lines (+10 = the new comment), **no stamp bump** — still `sigil-r23`, the `stone → shot` precedent for a vocabulary rename. `ingest.mjs` `a57d19212126e9f80bb5102f0b7a0292` → `8b853db9e6dc8c9515f6aa75c6045349`. **Proofs.** (1) **Normalized byte-identity**, the decisive one: with the new comment removed and `trait` → `ability` normalized, the canonical is **md5-identical to the pre-rename file** (`86ff2d5b…`), and `ingest.mjs` likewise. (2) **Trait-value digest identical** before and after — `72dcd9674ff7accd8681b426fb425931`, 22/22 rows present, so no rate moved and no row lost its field. (3) Resolved data identical:
`SHOTS_ART` `b7e156e9862b7bf0eff21cb3698177d8`, WEAPON-table `2ab6106c92fbe7f05beb267399cc2e63`, REACH/MINR/SPAN `041a611580682570b277e45b941bf4a4`, geom 5 keys, fighters unchanged, `CFG.hitKnock` 130, `omegaCap` 140. (4) **Fingerprints 8/8 BYTE-IDENTICAL** vs `.qa/fp-lead-r22.json` (`f1de581b7ec18be83af57d94ca592e5a`) — fingerprint-neutral exactly as predicted, because the field NAME never enters hashed sim state (only its values do, via `f.spin`/`f.dmg`), unlike `stone → shot` where event keys are hash inputs and every cell moved. (5) chromecheck **PASS** (bug 219 B, legend 837 B — the audience-facing `brief` strings are unchanged). (6) inscribe **GATE: PASS** (chain ties worst-fallback 0 px; dots-off-ink 0/56 flail, 0/24 nunchaku). (7) `node --check` clean on `ingest.mjs`. natscan / cellscan / projectile-fix / leadproof were **not** re-run: with normalized byte-identity and 8/8 fingerprints there is nothing for them to move (the authoring-pass precedent). **Audience impact: zero** — "ability" never reached the screen; the legend prints `+SPD / Hit` / `+DMG / Hit` and the poster prints `W.line` prose. **Still owed, unchanged:** the skill list — the knock pairing (Heavy deals more / Speedy takes less) and the cast size — plus the sanctioned `measure.mjs` run and the kit-route rebuild.

**The fighter cast scoped — two generic bots own the two numeric axes, the special tier is named as what pays for the hook interface, knock parked as the third axis (2026-10-28, owner ruling verbatim: "the cast stays at two for now, as those two are the most generic type bots with their skills +dmg and +spd, later we are going to have other bots with more special/unique skills", then, asking whether generic skills were good or bad: "dont you thinks ok or is it bad to have their skills like that ?" — answered that it is good and on-doctrine with two caveats, and the owner took the recording: "yes go ahead").** Docs only — **no code changed, no chrome changed, canonical untouched** (md5 `78bb56e0bd5fdecf1877f28e60617f82`, build `sigil-r23`, 3093 lines): open question 6’s cast half answered, a cast paragraph added to the doctrine subsection, and the dagger’s ⚠ note reframed — the note’s own text left verbatim, since it is a dated ruling. **What was recorded.** *(1) The generic tier is complete at two, by construction:* there are exactly two universal numeric growth axes (spin, damage), Speedy owns one and Heavy the other, so no third generic skill exists to write and every later bot is special/unique by necessity rather than by taste.
*(2) The special tier is what pays for the hook interface:* under M2 a numeric skill is *data* and needs no code, while a contextual skill (per-time, conditional-on-state, knock, length growth) is the first thing that requires hooks to exist — so **the hook machinery is not built until a special skill exists to hang on it**, which gives M2’s lazy adoption its trigger event. *(3) Knock parked with its analysis attached:* `CFG.hitKnock: 130` has exactly two read sites (`bladeVsBody`, `shotHitBody`), so a per-fighter knock multiplier is live on all 22 weapons and satisfies the axis-placement ruling with no dark label; the shelved shape was Heavy dealing more ("shoves harder") and Speedy taking less ("stays put"), both directions sharing one mechanic, one consumer pair and one probe; taken up it is a **sim change** owing a kept pair, a fingerprint re-baseline, and a bound measured against `maxSpeed` 1200 / `subMargin` 1200 **before** any number lands. *(4) Two watch-items, recorded so they are not discovered late:* the **legibility asymmetry** — Speedy’s skill is visible (the weapon genuinely spins faster and compounds into something you can see) while Heavy’s currently reads as a climbing DMG figure and a shorter round, the weaker signal in a muted video on a phone, so the fix if they ever feel unequal is a visible expression of damage and not a bigger number; and **do not tune +0.30 against +0.9 by eye** — their relative value is empirical (spin growth is bounded by the dormant `omegaCap` and buys spectacle plus hit frequency; damage growth shortens a 100 hp round and is front-loaded) and is owed to the sanctioned `measure.mjs` run.
*(5) The synergy grid:* 12 weapons carry `{spin: 0, dmg: 0.7}` and 10 carry `{spin: 0.70, dmg: 0}`, so each fighter has synergy weapons and cross weapons — four combinations, of which Speedy × spin-trait (+1.00/hit) is the cell that produced the measured ω 103.5 maximum. **The dagger’s ⚠ deferral is now a concrete synergy question:** +0.7 DMG makes it a Heavy-synergy weapon (1.6 per damaging hit together, roster split stays 12/10); +0.7 SPIN — its original v0.7 identity — makes it a Speedy-synergy weapon (1.00/hit, split becomes 11/11). One number and no plumbing either way, because a trait is weapon data. **[ANSWERED 2026-10-28: the owner took the first branch — +0.7 DMG, Heavy synergy, split stays 12/10. No sim change, the row already ships that value.]** **Owed, unchanged:** the sanctioned `measure.mjs` run on the owner’s machine (`.qa/chainlaw.mjs` FIRST) and the kit-route rebuild.

**`measure.mjs` contact law 3 was structurally incapable of passing — one field missing from the worker snapshot, found while costing the owner's first sanctioned run and fixed with one identifier (2026-10-28, owner go verbatim: "ok go", given after being told to hold the full run until the harness was fixed; the defect surfaced during calibration, owner word: "can you run measure.mjs, how long do you think it will take and maybe we should do it in parts so its not one long request, as i know thee will end up in excceeding errors etc").** The mechanism in one line: `polyOverlapVis` — the count of rendered overlaps deeper than the 3px ink, i.e. the number law 3's 2% band is judged on — is listed in the harness's merge table `ACC_ADD` (59 fields) but was absent from `accSnapshot()`, the object each worker returns (58 fields). Main seeds it to 0 through `accZero()` and then adds `undefined`, so it merged to **NaN**, and the pass condition `(polyOverlapVis - polyFlipVis) / polyTicks <= 0.02` evaluates `NaN <= 0.02` = false. *(1) Scope: every run, not just parallel ones.* `--jobs 1` still merges its single worker's snapshot, so the NaN appears there too — measured on the same cell at both job counts.
The field entered with the 2026-10-21 whip amendment, so **law 3 has been unconditionally red since that date** and its visible-overlap count has been missing from every report ever printed. *(2) Two earlier explanations are superseded.* The red had been attributed to the disclosed infeasible-pin band, and then to the named MIRROR class ("1 overlap, no growth, therefore no regression") — the latter by this session, before the mechanism was actually read. Both are wrong: the check subtracts the mirror class before it compares anything, so it never reached either clause. The band and the mirror class remain real and remain disclosed; they were simply not what turned the check red. *(3) The fix is one identifier:* `polyOverlapVis,` added to `accSnapshot()`'s return object at line 328, nothing else. `measure.mjs` md5 `c37b33b1e1c6f3a2c9d7365f7f0ee75c` → **`3ba90d73a502bcef6313111546740d30`**, 73,473 → 73,489 bytes (+16, exactly `" polyOverlapVis,"`). **The canonical is untouched** at `78bb56e0bd5fdecf1877f28e60617f82`, so no stamp bump and no fingerprint re-baseline — the sim was never in question, and the fingerprints stay 8/8 against `.qa/fp-lead-r22.json` by construction.
*(4) Five proofs.* (a) `diff` against the pre-fix copy shows exactly one changed line; (b) a field audit of `ACC_ADD`+`ACC_MAX`+`ACC_MIN` against `accSnapshot()` now reads 59 merged / 59 returned / **0 missing / 0 extra**; (c) pre-vs-post on the same cell changes only `NaN (n/a%)` → `0 (0.00%)` and `[FAIL] contact law 3` → `[PASS]`, every other byte identical; (d) `--jobs 1` versus `--jobs 2` on the same cell are byte-identical apart from the worker-count banner, so the merge contract now covers the field; (e) the owner's Windows cmd capture reproduces the sandbox run number-for-number — 116,850 fight-ticks, 93 overlaps, 4.750px worst depth, 103.5 rad/s max spin, 6.96s worst stall — across a different OS, a different shell and a different job count. *(5) What law 3 says now.* At the 3-seed quick tier it **PASSES**: one visible overlap in 116,850 fight-ticks, and that one is the mirror occurrence the check subtracts, so the tally moved 16 PASS / 2 FAIL → **17 PASS / 1 FAIL**, the survivor being the deliberate combat-stall red. At matrix scale it goes red again on real data and on the depth clause — that is a finding about the sim, not the harness, and it lives in the measurement entry below. The cosmetic `NaN (n/a%)` and `NaN/116850 fight-ticks` in the report prose were the same missing field and cleared with it.

**The first sanctioned `measure.mjs` run — the 484-cell baseline split across two machines in seven disjoint parts (2026-10-28) — the rest RETIRED VERBATIM (removed from HEAD 2026-11-11; recover with `git show docs-precleanup:docs/CHRONICLE-archive.md`). THE LOAD-BEARING RESIDUE STAYS HERE: the split mechanism — `--cells a+b,c+d --matrix-only` skips naturals and determinism, and the parts' battle sets are disjoint so their law counters sum exactly — and the fact that all six parts came from ONE instrument version, `3ba90d73a502bcef6313111546740d30`. The commands are in `scratch/measure-parts.md`; completion, the full aggregate and the structural asymmetry are in the entry below.**
**The 484-cell baseline COMPLETED and `measure.mjs` patched for attribution — the shield is measured at zero wins in 569 rounds in either hand, and the harness now names the LOCATION of both reds instead of only their size (2026-10-28, owner go via ask_user: "Yes — record the complete baseline" and "Apply it (guards + B + C)").** Part 5 landed (`scratch/m-05.txt` `3c6f03ed6ff34019a9b9275b85f49d3c`, 648 battles, 135.1 min at 2 workers in the sandbox), completing the split at **484/484 cells · 3,872 battles (484×8 exactly) · 8,297 rounds started · 7,891 decided · 236,639 hits · 182,138 parries · 87,753,496 fight-ticks · 1,641,012,543 substep poses · 7.16 h wall across the six parts**, every part from one instrument version (`3ba90d73…`). Round wins Speedy 3,078 / Heavy 4,813 = **39.0% Speedy**; cell favour 166 / 308 / 10. All integrity counters clean at full scale — phantom hits 0/236,639, phantom shot hits 0, swats without touch 0, parries without touch 0, substep-budget violations 0, missing chain poses 0 — and `scratch/measure-agg.mjs` confirms the six parts are disjoint and sum to 484 with no cell in two parts.
*(2) The shield at full census, which settles open questions 1 and 4 mechanistically rather than by inference.* **569 rounds were played with a shield held and the holder won none of them, in either hand** (21 cells as Speedy's weapon / 308 rounds, 21 as Heavy's / 261 rounds). `shield+shield` completed **zero rounds**: all 8 battles ran to exactly 420.0s and terminated 0/8, which the harness already flags as an expected red pending the balance pass. The mechanism is closed — shield contact is knock-only with no damage, and its trait grows only on damaging hits, so it can never KO and its trait can never activate. It is not a weak weapon, it is a weapon with no win condition. Its cell row prints `med NaNs`, the median of an empty round list: **a legitimate NaN, and the reason a blanket "no NaN in the report" guard is wrong** (clause 5).
*(3) The like-for-like skill test at full scale, and how much the partial view skewed it.* Shield cells excluded: **melee/melee 34.6% Speedy (121 cells, 2,324 rounds) · projectile/projectile 31.1% (100 cells, 1,343) · melee/projectile 83.0% (110, 1,868) · projectile/melee 2.5% (110, 1,787)**. Heavy therefore takes 65.4% of melee mirrors and 68.9% of projectile mirrors, so **+0.30 spin ≠ +0.9 dmg in round-win value and the two axes are not parallel** — damage reaches both offence paths, spin reaches neither. The projectile mirror moved from 24.9% on the 403-cell partial to **31.1%** complete, a 6.2-point swing from one slice: partial-baseline numbers of this kind should not be quoted as settled.
*(4) Weapon strength on the complete roster, as the HOLDER'S win rate — the balance-relevant metric, reported per side because Heavy is globally favoured.* scythe 83.0 (75.2 in Speedy's hand / 91.4 in Heavy's) · axe 78.8 · hammer 77.1 · mace 72.6 · nunchaku 70.3 · flail 68.6 · katana 68.6 · boomerang 66.6 · sword 66.4 · spear 65.9 · shuriken 58.3 · orb 53.9 · dagger 53.8 — then a cliff — javelin 27.6 · staff 24.5 · scroll 21.4 · bow 20.7 · tome 17.2 · crossbow 16.8 · knives 13.5 · wand 12.5 · shield 0.0. Group holder win%: **melee 69.0, projectile 29.3, shield 0.0**. Two projectiles sit above most melee — boomerang and shuriken, the returning/multi-hit ones. The dagger ranks 13th of 22, dead average, which is the datum its deferred trait ruling needs — **and the ruling consumed it the same day: +0.7 DMG ratified, no sim change.** Reproducible from the six part files with `scratch/measure-summary.mjs` (new, read-only), so none of these numbers rest on a transcription.
*(5) The attribution patch — `measure.mjs` `3ba90d73a502bcef6313111546740d30` → `9e941568f0bd4a0c66daebe1607e1a9e`, 1,116 → 1,216 lines, 73,489 → 81,009 bytes.* It adds three things and changes no measured value: **guards** (A1 a startup key-set check that `accSnapshot()` returns every field the merge tables name, in main and in every worker; A2 a post-merge assertion that every `ACC_ADD` value is a finite integer; A2b a NaN/undefined text check scoped to the law block only), **law 3 band attribution** (the worst band-class overlap now reports cell, seed, tick, round, both weapons, both spins, visibility, whether either weapon can flip, and whether a parry landed in the previous tick and/or its last substep; plus three population counters `bandN` / `bandParryNotLastSub` / `bandNoLead`), and **law 4s mechanism discrimination** (`shotGoneSids` became a Map sid → first departure, so a duplicate is named as mechanism A `addGone` firing twice or mechanism B gone-yet-still-in-flight at battle end, with the sids, ticks and kinds).
*(6) The five proofs, all run.* (a) **A1 fires**: deleting `polyOverlapVis` from `accSnapshot()` in a throwaway copy exits 1 with `measure: accSnapshot() omits merged field(s): polyOverlapVis`. (b) **A2 fires**: poisoning `merged.battles = NaN` after the merge exits 1 with `measure: merged accumulator "battles" is NaN, not a finite integer`. (c) **A2b does NOT false-positive**: `--cells shield+shield --matrix-only` finishes exit 0 in 41s and prints its `med NaNs` row — the exact case the drafted blanket guard would have thrown on. (d) **Report-additive**: `--cells sword+axe,axe+sword --matrix-only --jobs 2` pre-patch versus post-patch, **0 lines removed or altered** outside the wall-clock banner, 2 lines added (the two attribution lines). (e) **Job-count independent**: the same cells at `--jobs 1` and `--jobs 2` differ only in the banner. Canonical untouched at `78bb56e0bd5fdecf1877f28e60617f82`; `.qa/fingerprint.mjs` re-run, **8/8 identical to `fp-lead-r22.json`** (the sim never moved). `hashBattle()` names its fields explicitly, so neither new battle-record field joins the determinism hash and it stays comparable with every previously recorded run.
*(7) The lesson, recorded because it is a discipline failure and not just a bug.* The drafted Guard A2 was "the report text must never contain `NaN`", and it was verified against four healthy reports (`post-j2`, `post-quick3`, `m-01`, `m-04`) that all read clean. **The sample happened to exclude the only part containing a zero-round cell**, so the guard as drafted would have thrown on `shield+shield` — on every full matrix run, including the re-runs it was meant to enable. A verification sample must be chosen to include the cases that can legitimately look like failures, not only the cases that pass. `scratch/measure-agg.mjs` carried the same blind spot (it flagged Part 5 as "stale harness!") and now discriminates: NaN inside the law block is a merge defect, `med NaNs` in a zero-round cell row is a result. `scratch/attribution-patch.md` is amended in place with the falsification, the rewritten guard and a third mutation test for exactly this.
*(8) The first attribution result, and what it already rules out.* On the proof slice `sword+axe,axe+sword`: **band-class overlaps 263 · parry in the previous tick but NOT its last substep 26 · neither weapon lead/headLead 263**. Every band-class overlap in that slice involves a weapon pair that cannot flip at all, so widening the mirror test's "last substep" condition could not have claimed a single one — per the draft's own decision table this is the row that reads *classification is not the story, it is the resolver*. Caveat: this slice's worst band depth is only 1.751px, well inside the disclosed band. The deep cases (14–21px) live in the large slices, so **the ruling is not final until Part 2 is re-run under the patched harness** — and Part 1 for 3 of the 5 duplicate departures, Part 5 for the other 2. Commands in `scratch/measure-parts.md`; the instrument is now `9e941568…`, so those re-runs are NOT comparable with the six baseline part files counter-for-counter (they are comparable in every measured value, since the patch is report-additive, but the reports carry extra lines).
*(9) One more measured quantity worth carrying into the balance pass.* **406 of 8,297 rounds (4.9%) started and were never decided**, and they are spread across all six parts (77 / 44 / 69 / 53 / 55 / 108), so this is a general stall rate and not a shield artefact. Every decided round has a winner — `sum(cell n) == sum(speedy + heavy)` in all six parts — so the 406 are rounds that ran out of battle budget, consistent with the standing ruling that stalls are deliberate until skills exist. Shot ledger at full scale: **1,482,374 thrown · 124,867 landed (8.4%) · 95,434 swatted**.

**Whole-file canonical audit, doc-alignment pass, and the fingerprint arg-order guard (2026-10-28; owner orders verbatim: "can u do a full code audit of the canonical file and see if theres anything to remove, clean up, refactor, or simplify" and "also check the docs and see if they align with everything"; owner go via ask_user on the guard — "Add the guard to fingerprint.mjs". No sim line moved: canonical md5 `78bb56e0bd5fdecf1877f28e60617f82`, 3,093 lines, unchanged through the whole pass; fingerprints re-run properly 8/8 vs `.qa/fp-lead-r22.json`.)** *(1) Audit verdict: CLEAN, and no refactor is recommended.* 19 CSS classes and 19 element ids — 0 unused, 0 unreferenced; 34 `CFG` keys — 0 unread (13 are read exactly once, which is normal for config); 61 `WEAPONS` data fields — 0 dead (the three my scanner flagged were the words "one-**sided**", "**kinematics**" and "**VESTIGIAL**" inside comments, not fields); no `console.*`, no `debugger`, no TODO/FIXME/HACK anywhere; exactly ONE unreferenced declaration, `geom`, which :1026-1029 documents as the harness export surface with an explicit "a dead-code scan must not remove it"; one 4-line repeated idiom (the fighter-loop preamble `for (const f of fs) { if (!f.alive) continue; …` in two different functions) — a shared helper would add indirection for nothing; the hot paths allocate nothing (`tick` and `bladeVsBody` zero allocations, `fireShots`'s two pushes are the shot objects themselves); the 28 over-long lines hold 30.8 KB of art-poly and `CIRCLES_V2` **data**, not logic; comments are 866 lines = 28% of the file, which is the house style; two data fields (`off: 38`, `kind: 'page'`) are vestigial and the file says so itself at :600/:613.
**Why no refactor:** a behaviour-identical rewrite of 3,093 lines, provable only against 8 fingerprint cells, is real risk for no measured payoff — and four prior cleanup passes already took the dead-code surface to zero. This supersedes cleanup #4's headline, which reached the same verdict on the declaration scan. *(2) One genuine defect — found, first deferred, then FIXED the same day.* Canonical :1023-1029 named eight harnesses, four of which (`chainlaw`, `killstill`, `natscan`, `projectile-fix`) were retired in the workspace cleanup above, and it called the `measure.mjs` run "owed" when the 484-cell baseline had already completed. It was first recorded as deferred, on the reasoning that a comment-only edit still moves the md5 and still costs a dated entry, so it looked cheaper to bundle with the first skills-pass landing. The owner overruled that ("can we fix taht here before we start fresh in a new chat ?") and he is right: a fresh session should not inherit a known-false comment in the one file it has to trust. Corrected in place, **5 lines for 5, every changed line a comment continuation (verified by diff), line count held at 3,093** so every existing line-count claim in these docs stays true. The harness list is now the four that actually append `;return { …, geom }`, established by grep rather than from memory: `measure.mjs` :190, `ingest.mjs` :511, `.qa/fingerprint.mjs` :71, `.qa/leadproof.mjs` :51 — and note that `.qa/inscribe.mjs` extracts the script body but never uses `geom`, while `chromecheck.mjs` and `flipproof.mjs` do not extract it at all, so neither belongs in that list. **Canonical md5 `78bb56e0bd5fdecf1877f28e60617f82` → `e401e3e33c3133b03ec192f7e5fe0199`; build stamp NOT bumped (stays `sigil-r23` — behaviour-identical, the cleanup #2/#3 precedent). Proofs, all run: fingerprints 8/8 identical to `.qa/fp-lead-r22.json`, chromecheck PASS, inscribe GATE PASS, leadproof PASS, flipproof PASS.** The pre-edit file was backed up outside the workspace before the first stroke. Nothing is now bundled for the next canonical landing. *(3) Doc alignment.* 46 paths cited across the docs no longer exist, but 29 were retired before today and every one sits inside a **dated** entry — the verbatim rule working as designed, not drift. Three live claims were wrong and are now fixed: README named `.qa/projectile-fix.mjs` as a live fast-iteration tool (amended, and it now states the consequence — `measure.mjs`, owner-gated, is the only instrument left covering shots, holds and the damage gate); this file's Testing recipe claimed "not one write call in its 1,116 lines" (the substance re-verified — still **0 write calls** at 1,216 lines — and the claim made version-proof); README had no new-chat handoff (added: the batch-1 / batch-2 / leave-local file split, the standing rules, the settled doctrine marked *do not re-litigate*, and the owed queue in order).
*(4) The fingerprint arg-order guard, and the near-miss that prompted it.* `.qa/fingerprint.mjs`'s usage is `<out.json> [file]`. Invoked with those two arguments transposed, it takes the canonical build as its OUTPUT path and the JSON baseline as its input file. That happened on 2026-10-28; it crashed inside `new Function` — parsing the baseline JSON as JavaScript — **three frames before its `writeFileSync`**, so `spinclash-preview.html` survived by luck rather than by design. Both files were verified intact afterwards (canonical `78bb56e0…`; baseline `f1de581b7ec18be83af57d94ca592e5a`, 493 B, 8 keys). The same broken invocation had been piped through a `grep` twice and reported as "fingerprints 8/8" with no output behind it; the claim turned out true (re-run correctly: 46s, exit 0, 8/8 matched to the digit, tick counts included) but reporting it unverified is precisely the discipline failure lesson (7) below records. The guard now refuses three ways, each proven to exit 1 printing the usage line and writing nothing: an OUT path ending in `.html`, OUT === FILE, and a FILE containing no `<script>` tag. The correct invocation is unaffected — 8/8 after the guard landed.

**The attribution re-runs landed — law 3 is the resolver, law 4s was a sim double-kill, and the fix is one line: build `cross-r24` (2026-10-28; the owner ran Parts 2 and 1 on his own machine and uploaded `a-02.txt` / `a-01.txt`; owner go via ask_user on all three forks — fix law 4s, fix the law-3 resolver, record it all. Canonical md5 `e401e3e33c3133b03ec192f7e5fe0199` → `a217471e2f6a93d5f6e5037abe088d7d`, 3,093 → 3,102 lines, stamp `sigil-r23` → `cross-r24`.)** *(1) The instrument is proven report-additive at 81-cell scale, on a second machine.* Every comparable field in both re-runs matches the recorded baseline exactly — **26/26**: battles 648+648, rounds started 1,428/1,376, rounds decided 1,384/1,299, hits 34,893/52,930, parries 27,076/38,249, fight-ticks, substep poses, band worst **20.754/14.673px**, visible overlaps 718/1,080, double departures **0/3**, shots thrown/landed/swatted. The patch was verified report-additive on a 2-cell slice before it landed; this repeats the proof at 40× the scale and on different hardware, so the recorded baseline and the patched instrument are confirmed interchangeable.
Wall 2,094.2s (Part 2) and 4,314.4s (Part 1) at 8 workers — the runbook's ~35/~73 min estimates were accurate to the minute. *(2) Law 3 is the RESOLVER — and the proof slice that first said so was biased, not merely shallow.* Only **5 of 22 weapons can flip**: dagger, staff, scythe and katana at `lead: -1`, javelin at `headLead: 1`. The 2-cell proof slice was `sword+axe, axe+sword` — **neither sword nor axe carries either flag** — so its headline "263 band overlaps, 263 with no lead/headLead weapon" was structurally guaranteed. That slice could not have produced any other number, and it never tested the classification hypothesis it appeared to refute. Recorded as a correction because the conclusion was already in this file. At scale **49% of band-class overlaps do have a flippable weapon in play** (Part 1: 26,980 band overlaps, 11,282 non-flippable = 41.8%; Part 2: 17,811, 11,556 = 64.9%), so the slice's 100% was an artifact of cell choice. The two counters are independent flags, not a partition (`measure.mjs` :645-646), so the lower bound explained by NEITHER is 14,471 (53.6%) in Part 1 and 3,822 (21.5%) in Part 2.
*(3) The conclusion survives, for a reason the slice never tested.* Both worst cases carry `lead/headLead true` **and `parry in previous tick false`**: Part 2 **20.754px at flail+scythe** seed 104 t12272 round 2, spins −6.8/3.5; Part 1 **14.673px at crossbow+katana** seed 102 t4588, spins −7.5/3.5. A flippable weapon IS in play, so widening the classification is moot; there is no parry in the previous tick at all, so widening the mirror test's timing window cannot reach them either — the mirror class exists to excuse a parry-driven flip, and there was no parry to flip anything. Widening the window would claim at most `bandParryNotLastSub` (1,227 = 4.5% and 2,433 = 13.7%), and by construction none of those is a worst case. Chain-pose integrity passes with **0 missing** across 3,658,687 / 1,156,201 rendered chain ticks, so this is not the chain-blindness artifact already fixed. **Verdict: the resolver genuinely permits ~21px of drawn interpenetration.** *(4) The law reports a number the player never sees.* In both full parts the single deepest overlap is **mirror-class** — 21.852px (Part 2), 16.040px (Part 1) — which the law's own text calls "sim truth, never drawn".
The headline `[FAIL] worst 21.852px` therefore includes undrawn poses; the worst **drawn** penetration is the band worst, 20.754 / 14.673px. Recorded as a candidate instrument refinement (adjudicate law 3 on band worst), NOT applied. *(5) Law 4s was a SIM defect, and my first diagnosis of it was wrong.* I reported it as a harness accounting artifact and the owner approved "dedupe `addGone` by sid+tick in `measure.mjs`" on that description. `addGone` (:395-400) **already dedupes and reports** — it is the detector, not the defect. Applying the approved fix would have turned law 4s green while the sim kept double-emitting and kept inflating its own `G.stats.crossed`, hiding exactly what the law exists to catch. Withdrawn before applying; the mechanism was re-derived from the canonical and the owner re-ruled on the real fix. *(6) The mechanism, and the one-line fix.* In the shot-vs-shot loop (canonical :1971-1982) `A.dead` is tested once at the top of the OUTER iteration and only `B.dead` is re-tested inside the inner one, with no `break` after a cancel. So once A cancels B, the inner loop advances and tests the **already-dead A** against the next shot; on overlap, `killShot(A,'cross')` fires a second time — a duplicate `shotCross` event and a second `G.stats.crossed++`.
Fix: `break;` at :1980, one line plus its comment. Measured incidence: **3 double-kills in 415,580 shots** (Part 1), **0** in Part 2 — 1 in 138,527. All three are boomerang cells (`boomerang+orb` sid 711 t20488, `boomerang+scroll` sid 1399 t29805, `boomerang+wand` sid 849 t15450), each same-tick same-cause. Why the boomerang: its shot is the roster's largest (**r 10**; next 9, 9, 7, then five at 6 and the bow at 5) and its only curving one (`turn: 2.6`), so a returning boomerang meets a freshly launched one — and the **orb fires no shot at all**, which is why `boomerang+orb`'s duplicate is boomerang-vs-boomerang. **Disclosed trade-off:** A now cancels ONE opponent per tick, so a third shot overlapping only the dead A survives where it previously died; within-tick ordering was already arbitrary, no shot is killed twice, and `stats.crossed` is now accurate. Diff is exactly three hunks: two stamp sites and the fix. *(7) The fingerprints did NOT move, and that is NOT evidence of neutrality.* **8/8 byte-identical including tick counts.** But the suite runs the natural tier at seeds 1-11 while the double-kills were found at forced-matrix seeds 101/102/106, at a rate of 1 in ~138,527 shots — **the 8 cells never exercise the fixed path**.
8/8 proves only that those 8 battles were unaffected; this IS a behaviour change, just a rare one. `boomerang+shuriken s1` is in the suite and was the likeliest candidate, and it did not trigger. **Coverage gap recorded, fix owed:** the standing baseline is blind to the shot-cross path, so any future "behaviour-identical" claim resting on it cannot cover shot-vs-shot changes. `fp-lead-r22.json` remains the single standing baseline and also stands for `cross-r24` (proven identical) — no twin file was created, per the 2026-10-28 cleanup that deleted four such twins. *(8) The narrow diagnosis slice, run on `cross-r24` (owner go for a sandbox run).* `--cells flail+scythe,crossbow+katana --matrix-only`: 16 battles, **182.0s** at 2 sandbox workers, exit 0. It **reproduced the worst case exactly** — 20.754px at flail+scythe seed 104 t12272 round 2, identical attribution flags — so the `break` did not disturb law 3, and the baseline's worst drawn penetration is deterministic and reachable in three minutes instead of 35.
