Three new facts: **`neither weapon lead/headLead` = 0 of 1,120** band overlaps in these two cells, so at the deep end 100% involve a flippable weapon (the strongest form of (3)); `substep pre-resolution overlaps … worst 28.73px` against a rendered worst of 20.754px, so **the resolver IS acting — it cuts 28.73px down — but 20.754px survives to the rendered frame**; and `0 crossed` in 16 battles, which independently explains (7).
Every other law PASSes, chain-pose 0 missing, termination 16/16. *(9) Part 5 is not needed.* Mechanism **B = 0** and Part 2 has no duplicates at all, so neither of the runbook's two triggers fired; the two remaining baseline duplicates stay unattributed but mechanism A is boomerang-specific and Part 5 contains `shield+boomerang`. *(10) What the resolver fix still needs, and the honest state of the owner's ruling.* The owner ruled "fix the resolver now". The slice shows the resolver is not inert — it reduces 28.73px to 20.754px and then stops — so the work is to find why separation stalls at that depth, which needs the pose at t12272 (both body positions, both weapon angles, the overlapping polygon pair and the penetration vector). The harness reports the depth but not the geometry, so the next step is either a read-only probe that replays flail+scythe seed 104 to that tick, or a temporary geometry dump behind a flag in `measure.mjs` plus a 182s re-run. **No canonical resolver code has been written**; the only canonical change landed here is the one-line shot-cross `break`. Note the consequence the ruling carries: a resolver change moves fingerprints and makes the 484-cell baseline non-comparable, so it implies a full re-run (7.16 h across two machines) before the next balance reading can be trusted.

**Law 3's resolver diagnosed with `--band-dump` — three hypotheses refuted, the real mechanism is a translation-only fixed point, and the disclosed band bound is FALSIFIED (2026-10-28; owner rulings via ask_user: "Instrument measure.mjs behind a flag, re-run the slice", then on routing "pick the oone you think is best").** *(1) The flag landed in the instrument, not the canonical.* `--band-dump` attaches the resolver geometry of each battle's deepest band-class overlap to the report: both poses, the deepest circle pair and its normal, body/wall pin state, one read-only replica of the canonical's projection pass (sim :1676-1701, faithful to the accumulated-delta ordering and the raw-position axis prefilter), and the full 16-iteration loop run under both stall-test variants. It replicates the canonical's `separateBodiesPos` and `clampToArenaPos` per pass; it omits the rotational nudge so both variants compare on projection alone. `measure.mjs` 1,216 → **1,379 lines**, md5 `9e941568f0bd4a0c66daebe1607e1a9e` → `3be7f0de37e444096c6355435cd560cb` (the flag alone) → **`ec0b819901a0f3a09dcd5e68722d46bf`** (the corrected disclosure text of point 9). **The canonical was never opened: md5 stays `a217471e2f6a93d5f6e5037abe088d7d`, build `cross-r24`, 3,102 lines, and no fingerprint moved.** *(2) Report-additivity proven twice, not asserted.* `bandWorstHere` rides the per-battle result object and never an accumulator, so the dump touches no `ACC_ADD`/`ACC_MAX`/`ACC_MIN` field and the A1/A2 guards are uninvolved.
Flag OFF after the flag edit is **byte-identical** to flag OFF before it on the same 2-cell slice (16 battles, `flail+scythe,crossbow+katana`, modulo the wall-clock field) — so the 163 added lines perturb nothing. The later disclosure-text correction was proven separately: it changes **exactly one report line**, and every measured number on that line is unchanged (238756 fight-ticks / 1134 overlaps / 0.47% / 222 visible / 0.09% / worst 20.754px). Flag ON versus flag OFF: **0 lines removed or changed**, 8 pure additions. The dump also self-checks: it recomputes `parryGap` on the captured pose and reports `recomputed gap -20.754px vs measured -20.754px — FAITHFUL`, so a future drift between the replica and law 3's own measurement prints instead of silently misleading. *(3) The geometry at the worst case.* flail+scythe seed 104 t12272 round 2, the exact 20.754px already attributed: flail body (430.729, 884.388) spin -6.8 `stuck 1` reach 204.04 nudgeCap 0.0588 rad, 56 circles; scythe body (480.091, 766.289) spin 3.5 `stuck 1` reach 207.44 nudgeCap 0.0578 rad, 35 circles. Deepest pair **A#33 (r 25.2) vs B#6 (r 8.4)**, normal (-0.9933, 0.1159), contact (307.768, 841.564). One projection pass: **9 contacting pairs, gross push 17.465px, net 15.714px, ALIGNMENT 90.0%**, post-pass deepest -10.153px, torque A 777.23 / B -834.24. *(4) Hypothesis one REFUTED — the inverted stall test is a real bug but not this bug.* Sim :1708 reads `if (deep - gapPrev > -0.02)` while its own comment at :1671-75 says "keep projecting while progress is being made".
Progress is the penetration SHRINKING (`deep` rising toward 0), so the intended test is `deep - gapPrev < 0.02`; as written the loop declares a stall on large progress and continues on regression, so it always exits after two passes (one, plus one after the nudge). Running both variants read-only on the actual pose: **as written 2 passes → -10.156px; as commented 3 passes → -10.157px.** Same fixed point, so the sign error costs nothing here. It is recorded as a latent defect to carry into the skills-pass landing, where the re-run happens anyway. *(5) Hypothesis two REFUTED — no chain mechanism.* The Part 2 worst case is `crossbow+katana` at 14.673px and **neither weapon is a chain weapon**, so nothing flail-specific can be the general cause. Separately, `flailSync` is just `flailPose`, which runs spine-local → world ("particles re-seated on the (possibly moved) pose", sim :1385-86, :1360-70), so the chain travels WITH the body and the resolver's push is not undone. A first reading had this backwards; corrected before it was written down. *(6) Hypothesis three REFUTED — it is not an infeasible body pin.* Bodies sit at **exactly 128px = exactly 2R, body overlap 0px**, with **45.42px and 168.968px** of wall clearance, and the multi-contact push is 90% aligned rather than self-cancelling.
The v0.8-step1 concern that "opposed laterals cancel" is not what is happening at the worst case. *(7) What IS proven: the resolver is translation-only and has a geometric fixed point.* Inside sim :1676-1732 `wAngle` is only ever READ (the two `bladeCircles` calls); the sole writes are the nudge at :1724-25, capped at `nudgePx/REACH` = 0.0588 rad and gated to one add per tick per fighter. Two crossed ~205px-reach blades cannot be separated by translating the bodies — they slide along each other — and translation-only projection on this pose converges to **≈10px and no further**, under either stall test. The loop already accumulates the contact torque at :1697-98 and then **discards its magnitude**, reading only `Math.sign(tqA)`/`Math.sign(tqB)` at :1713-14. The information needed to open the scissors is computed and thrown away; a rotational DOF inside the loop is the principled fix. *(8) One thing is NOT closed.* The rendered depth (-20.754px) is DEEPER than the resolver's own fixed point on that same pose (-10.15px), so the rendered frame is not a converged resolver output. Candidates:
the loop entering that substep from a much deeper pose and exiting after two passes, or dynamics re-deepening between the last resolver call and the tick-entry record. Settling it needs a per-substep trace around t12272, not a single-pose dump, and it was deliberately left open rather than guessed at. *(9) The disclosed band bound is FALSIFIED and was corrected in the two places that state it.* The band promised "infeasible pin configs (bodies touching + buried wide head) may show **<=6px for <=2 ticks**, escaping via rotation; traced seeds 7/8, probe-verified". Measured: **20.754px and 14.673px, both flagged visible** (past the 3px ink), in a band population of 1,120 overlaps across just two cells. Corrected in `measure.mjs`'s law-3 report line — the text the owner reads on every run — and in `ingest.mjs`'s header comment. Two canonical comments are falsified by the same evidence and are **OWED to the skills-pass landing rather than edited now**, so the canonical takes one sanctioned edit instead of two: :1673 "when progress stalls the config is an infeasible pin", and :1720 "a smooth sweep whose burial stays sub-ink while it lasts".
the chronicle archive (since removed; tag `docs-precleanup`) :1220 carried the same retired claim and stayed verbatim per the standing rule. *(10) Routing — the owner delegated the choice, and the rework is deferred.* The resolver fix is a rework, not a one-liner, and its fixed point scales with **reach**; the first skills item is length growth, which changes every reach in the roster, so a rotational resolver designed now would be re-tuned immediately. The band is 0.09% of rendered fight-ticks visible and has never been reported in play. Decision: **correct the disclosure now (done, doc-only, no baseline cost, no fingerprints moved), design the rotational-DOF resolver after length growth, and carry the inverted stall test into that same landing so one 484-cell re-run covers both.** Law 3 stays RED and disclosed until then. Evidence kept: `scratch/d-off2.txt` (the byte-identity control) and `scratch/d-on2.txt` (the geometry block); the two superseded first-pass outputs were deleted at the owner's ruling.

**[content removed — `git show docs-precleanup3:docs/GAMES.md`]**

**The length-growth coverage probe — the ladder is safe BECAUSE it is pre-baked, and the runtime-stretch shortcut is now refuted by measurement (2026-10-28; owner pick via ask_user: "start with the coverage probe"; the two routing questions that followed were both delegated — "do what you think is best" and "again whatever you think is best". No sim line moved: canonical md5 `a217471e2f6a93d5f6e5037abe088d7d`, build `cross-r24`, 3,102 lines, no fingerprint disturbed. The probe is read-only, writes nothing, runs no battles and never invokes `measure.mjs`.)**
*(1) The instrument.* `.qa/lengrow-proof.mjs`, **274 lines, md5 `218f4f84da1f6d699e8dbbb3902279be`**, runs in ~3s. It loads the canonical through the house `leadproof.mjs` loader, rebuilds the spear's runtime poly (**5 drawn shapes, 19 covering circles, runtime len 147.47**) and sweeps the stretch factor `k`. Two metrics: `eps(k)`, the house sliver metric defined exactly as `ingest.mjs` :462-469 defines it; and `touch(k)`, the deepest a body disc (`CFG.bodyR` 64) can overlap the drawn silhouette while missing **every** covering circle — the "gap hit" the owner made top priority on 2026-10-19 (*"they dont wanna see these 'gap' hits"*), and what law 1 looks like from the viewer's seat.
*(2) The threshold this probe started from was the wrong one, and only measurement showed it.* The 3px ink outline is **not** a usable criterion here: the shipped spear **already** carries a **2.97px** uncovered tip protrusion, leaving **0.03px** of headroom, so "does it cross the ink?" is answered *yes* at k=1.0001 and carries no information. The honest criterion is the **delta versus the shipped weapon**. The sweep's `verdict` column still labels against the absolute ink, for continuity with the 2026-10-19 priority — it must be read against the baseline row, not against 3px, and the file now says so in its own header.
*(3) The measurement.* `eps` / `touch` / Δtouch-vs-shipped: **k=1.0 → 2.00 / 2.92 / — (SHIPPED BASELINE, already a gap hit)** · k=1.0489 (+4.9%) → 2.57 / 3.18 / +0.26 · k=1.10 (+10.0%) → 2.82 / 3.42 / +0.50 · **k=1.1539 (+15.4%, the design point) → 2.68 / 3.78 / +0.86** · k=1.186 (+18.6%, the spine-line proxy limit) → 3.13 / 3.92 / +1.06 · k=1.25 → +1.42 · k=1.40 (+40%) → 5.17 / +2.25. Degradation is **~0.07px per ladder step**, monotonic and unbounded in `k`.
*(4) The mechanism is derived, not merely observed.* Under a `d`-only stretch that **holds `r`**, the art tip outruns the circle that was packed to cover it, and the protrusion grows in closed form: **`protrusion(k) = (tipD − lastD)·k − lastR`**. With art tip `d` 147.47 and outermost circle `d` 141.8 `r` 2.7 that is **`5.67k − 2.7`** → 2.972px at k=1 (measured 2.92) and **3.845px at the design point (measured 3.78)**; the 0.07px residual is the 1px candidate grid. Corroborated independently by the `touch@` column, whose deepest gap hit marches outward along the spine from x=208 to x=230 across the sweep. **The entire degradation is the tip** — nothing else in the silhouette loses coverage.
*(5) Verdict: PASS for the recorded design, FAIL for the shortcut.* The **pre-baked ladder is safe**: `ingest.mjs` packs circles against *each step's own* stretched silhouette, so the tip circle is resized per step and the protrusion stays at the shipped ~3.0px — **zero degradation across all 12 steps**. A **runtime `d`-stretch is not viable**: +0.86px at the design point, growing without bound. This is the number that justifies baking twelve coverings instead of stretching one polygon, and it upgrades the design note *"Pre-baked ladder, not a runtime stretch"* from an invariant argument (a `d`-only stretch is not uniform, so it cannot inherit *"a uniform scale of an inscribed covering stays inscribed"*, :462-463) to a **measured** one.
*(6) One disclosed conservatism.* `eps` here is computed on the **plain even-odd interior** of every sub-polygon — `ingest.mjs`'s own `covers()` test for `type === 'poly'` — rather than on ingest's inflated grid-and-narrow-column-fill solid. This probe's solid is therefore a **subset** of ingest's, so its `eps` can only **under-report** a hole that ingest would catch. That is valid for the `k`-sweep because the identical definition is used at every `k`, and the sweep is the design input; `touch` is unaffected, being measured against the drawn silhouette. The ladder's top step still owes a real `ingest.mjs` bake, which will supersede this interior.
*(7) A process defect in producing this entry, disclosed.* A `write_file` of a rewritten probe **returned success and did not land**; the run whose numbers are recorded above was executed by the **previous 263-line version**. Detected from three formatting markers in its output that only the old file produces (`bodyR 64` rather than `64px`, the shape/circle-count line, and bracketed coordinates). No number changed — the older body is the better-documented one, and its conservatism is exactly point (6) — but the new verdict block had been patched onto a body that had not been re-read, so the body was then read, every identifier the verdict uses was confirmed present, and the file was re-run end to end with `node --check`. Standing lesson reconfirmed: **a tool reporting success is not the file saying so.**
*(8) Where this leaves the build.* Step 3 (the **minimal spear-only** build the owner scoped: `f.lenStep` plus its per-round reset, the ladder, `bladeCircles`/`spineFrame`/renderer, and `grow()` applying the rate — with `REACH` and `MINR` left as per-weapon tables and the substep budget's slight under-budgeting of a grown spear disclosed rather than fixed) is **unblocked**, and its geometry route is now fixed by measurement rather than by preference: **each of the twelve steps must be baked.** Owed before the ladder ships: that real `ingest.mjs` bake of the top step.

**The resolver's inverted stall test fixed, with the two comments it had falsified — landed alone as `stall-r25`, and it is NOT behaviour-neutral (2026-10-28; this is step 2 of the skills-pass order the owner picked — probe → resolver fix landed alone → length-growth build — with both routing questions delegated, "do what you think is best" and "again whatever you think is best". Canonical md5 `a217471e2f6a93d5f6e5037abe088d7d` → **`cd5f4de10e15c35fc4e97ac579f47673`**, build `cross-r24` → **`stall-r25`**, line count **held at 3,102**. The pre-edit file was backed up outside the workspace before the first stroke.)**
*(1) The defect, verified in source before the edit rather than from the earlier diagnosis.* `deep ≤ 0`, more negative being deeper, so **progress is `deep` RISING toward 0**. At :1708 the test read `if (deep - gapPrev > -0.02)`, and TRUE is the branch that either nudges-and-continues or breaks at :1729. That expression is true on large progress and on mild regression, and false only when penetration regresses by more than 0.02px — so the loop **declared a stall exactly when it was converging**, and always exited after two passes (one, plus one after the nudge). The corrected `< 0.02` makes pass 1 fall through to :1731 on real progress, nudge on the genuine stall at pass 2, and break at pass 3. That reproduces the "2 passes vs 3 passes" the `--band-dump` diagnosis had already measured on the worst-case pose, so the mechanism is confirmed twice by independent routes.
*(2) The edit, diff-verified.* Three hunks and nothing else: the :1671-75 comment **5 lines → 5**, the :1708 test **1 → 1**, the :1715-22 comment **8 → 8**. Fourteen changed lines on each side, of which exactly **one** is not a comment continuation — the sign fix itself. Substituting `cross-r24` back for `stall-r25` in the result reproduces the backup's diff and no more, so the stamp bump is fully accounted for. Both corrected comments now carry the measurement that falsified their predecessors: a stall is *"NOT an infeasible pin but this translation-only loop's fixed point (~10px)"*, and the nudge *"does NOT hold burial sub-ink — measured 20.754px / 14.673px, both visible past the 3px outline"*.
*(3) The fingerprint result: 1 of 8 identical, 7 moved.* `natural s7` is byte-identical, hash **and** tick count (`56589794…:12696`). The rest: `hammer+dagger s5` 13777 → **18000** · `staff+hammer s1` 12399 → **11704** · `boomerang+shuriken s1` 16943 → **15436** · `shield+hammer s4` 14964 → 14964 (same length, different trajectory) · `bow+crossbow s2`, `wand+bow s3`, `natural s11` 18000 → 18000 (undecided before and after).
*(4) What that does and does not license.* `CAP_SEC` 150 × `tickHz` 120 = 18000, so a `:18000` suffix means the battle ran the full budget **undecided**; on these 8 cells undecided goes 3 → 4. But two cells decide **faster** (−695 and −1507 ticks) and one holds its length exactly while changing trajectory, so the effect is **mixed, not a systematic stall increase** — and a single cell flipping in a set of eight cannot move the recorded 4.9%-undecided headline in either direction. **Only the 484-cell re-run settles it, and that is owner-gated.**
*(5) Why the fix stands regardless of the fingerprint cost.* The loop now does what its own comment always claimed: project while progress is made, nudge on a genuine stall. Before, it exited after two passes every time, so the resolver was doing **less work than designed** and firing its rotational kick on convergence rather than on stall. Reverting to preserve a baseline would be letting the baseline dictate correctness — and the rotational-DOF rework that follows has to be built on a loop whose stall test means what it says.
*(6) Law 3's headline is not expected to move, and that was already measured.* The `--band-dump` pass ran both variants read-only on the worst-case pose: **as written 2 passes → −10.156px; as commented 3 passes → −10.157px.** Same fixed point. So this fix changes trajectories broadly (7 of 8 fingerprints) while barely touching the resolver's convergence depth on the very pose that produced law 3's 20.754px — consistent with the standing diagnosis that the real defect is the **missing rotational DOF**, not the stall test. **Law 3 stays RED and disclosed.**
*(7) New baseline, and the inventory consequence.* **`.qa/fp-stall-r25.json`** written and adopted as standing. `.qa/fp-lead-r22.json` is **KEPT** as the pre-fix record of `cross-r24` — a dated entry cites it, and it is the only way to reproduce the 7-of-8 move. `README.md`'s Batch 1 is capped at the owner's 10-file upload limit, so both baselines cannot sit in it: `fp-stall-r25.json` takes slot 8 and `fp-lead-r22.json` moves to Batch 2 marked as no longer a valid equivalence target. Workspace 25 → **26 files**.
*(8) Proofs run, and one recorded detail corrected.* Fingerprints regenerated against the final file (34s), and the stamp bump proven **sim-neutral** by diffing the pre-stamp and post-stamp JSONs — identical, as expected, since `<title>` and the `#build` node live outside the sim script. `leadproof` **ALL PASS** · `flipproof` **ALL PASS** · `chromecheck` **PASS** · `inscribe` **GATE: PASS**. **Correction:** `inscribe.mjs` ends in `process.exit(fail)` at :391, so its exit code *is* the failure count — it exited **0** here, which is the clean pass. The "`inscribe.mjs` GATE PASS at exit 2 as designed" in the 2026-10-28 cleanup entry does not match the source; exit 2 would mean two failures. `ingest.mjs` was **not** re-run — with no SVG argument it prints usage, and nothing in this landing touches poly or covering data.
*(9) Docs.* Live claims amended in place with dates: `GAMES.md`'s Current-state build bullet (new stamp, md5, baseline, and the stamp sequence extended), its "Next: the skills pass" bullet (steps 1-2 done, step 3 scoped), the length-growth **Status** line (which had promised the resolver items would ride along in *its* landing — superseded in mechanism, preserved in intent, since one re-run should still cover both), and `README.md`'s canonical row, baseline row, standing rule 4 and owed items (1)-(3) plus its Closed list. Everything else naming `cross-r24`, `a217471e…` or `fp-lead-r22.json` sits inside a **dated** entry and stays verbatim per the standing rule. `docreflow` FIRST then `navgen`, both `--check` clean afterwards.
*(10) Owed now.* The **484-cell re-run**, batched to cover `stall-r25` and the length-growth build in one pass rather than two (owner-gated; ~7.16 hours across two machines) · the **rotational-DOF resolver rework**, after length growth, since its fixed point scales with reach · the **fingerprint shot-cross coverage gap**, which `fp-stall-r25.json` inherits unchanged because it runs the same 8 cells · and the ladder's real **`ingest.mjs` bake** of the top step.
*(11) An instrument defect found while proving this landing: `chromecheck.mjs`'s byte counts are NOT reproducible, and only its PASS/FAIL is meaningful.* During verification its reported `legend=` length moved **806B → 821B** between two runs, which looked like a user-visible regression from the sign fix. It is not. Isolating stamp-vs-sign gave the opposite ordering (pre-edit 830B, stamp-only 822B, final 807B), and five consecutive runs of the **same byte-identical file** (md5 re-verified before the loop) gave **810, 823, 810, 809, 806** — a 17-byte spread, wider than the delta being investigated. Cause: canonical **:2275**, `let seed = 1 + Math.floor(Math.random() * 1e5)` in the app-state section, which `chromecheck.mjs` **does not stub** (it stubs `performance.now` to 0 and drives `requestAnimationFrame` with synthetic timestamps, but leaves `Math.random` live). Each run therefore rolls a different matchup, and the legend's weapon cards render different names. The **sim is unaffected** — :96 declares *"SIM — pure. No DOM, no Math.random, no Date/performance below this line until the RENDERER section"*, the RENDERER banner is at **:2000-2001**, and :2275 sits inside it, i.e. outside the pure-sim region by design; `fingerprint.mjs` also passes explicit seeds, which is why the fingerprints are stable to the byte while this one diagnostic wanders. **Standing rule from this: never use `chromecheck`'s `bug=`/`legend=` byte counts as a change detector; run it for its assertions.** All five runs of the final build PASSed, and the **pre-edit content PASSed 3/3 as well, at 820 / 842 / 835B** — a range that *overlaps* the final build's 806-823B, so the two builds are indistinguishable on this metric, which is precisely why it proves nothing. `bug=219B` was stable across every run of both; only the legend wanders, because only the legend renders the rolled weapon names. A fix was considered and **deliberately not built**: pinning the seed would make the counts reproducible but freeze `chromecheck` onto one matchup forever, trading away the random-coverage it currently gets for free. The cheap correct version is a printed `--seed` (default random, reproducible on demand), which is a QA-tool change with no bearing on this landing and is recorded as an option rather than an owed item.

**The length-growth build landed as `lengrow-r26` — the spear's trait is live, the geometry is the rigid junction map, and the house fingerprint suite cannot see any of it (2026-10-28; step 3 of 3 of the skills-pass order the owner picked, built on his "build now" and "start it". Canonical md5 `cd5f4de10e15c35fc4e97ac579f47673` → **`fc8ca35c3204261b703555f585f70c75`**, build `stall-r25` → **`lengrow-r26`**, lines 3,102 → **3,205** (+103). The pre-edit file was backed up outside the workspace before the first stroke.)**
*(1) The geometry ruling, and the measurement that superseded the recorded design.* The owner's ask_user pick was **"Rigid head, junction at 69.23 (recommended)"** plus **"Build it now"**, which replaced the uniform `d`-stretch this section had recorded. The gate settled it rather than taste: uniform mode first breaks the house gate at **+6.1%** (miss 5.14px against a 4.6px band) because it deforms the head cluster whose barb geometry the covering was packed against, while the rigid map — shaft points at or behind `J` scale by one factor `k`, head points past `J` translate by one shared delta `(k-1)·J` — holds the tip protrusion at the shipped ~3.0px and passes at every step. Consequence for the recorded plan: *"each of the twelve steps must be baked"* was correct for the geometry it measured and is **moot** for the geometry that shipped, so the ladder is built **at boot** from the already-normalised polys and `.qa/bake-rows.mjs` is not on this path.
*(2) What landed, file-verified.* Spear row `:554` — `trait: { spin: 0.0, dmg: 0.3, len: 0.012 }`, `lenJ: 69.23 / 113.38`, legend *"grows 1.2% longer per damaging hit"* · `LEN_STEPS = 12` and the `LEN_LADDER` builder `:916-943`, which **throws** if `lenJ` does not put the junction strictly inside the drawn tip · `f.lenStep` initialised `:1163` and reset `:1251` inside `rollWeapons` · `grow()` advancing one step per damaging hit, clamped at the ladder top · `bladeCircles(x, y, ang, wId, out, fl, fp, ls)` reading `LEN_LADDER[wId].circles[ls]`, with `_pA.ls`/`_pB.ls` and all seven call sites passing the step · `drawWeapon(g, …, polys)` with `const PS = polys || W.poly.polys` and **both** poly loops switched to `PS` (the `weaponScale` `:667` and `REACH` `:838` derivation loops deliberately **not** switched, so the shared tables stay base-step) · `drawWeaponOn(g, …, acc, ls)` resolving `polys[ls]` · both fight-view draw sites passing `f.lenStep` (`:2461` overlay/art, `:2476` hitbox overlay) · `REACH.spear` raised to the top step. `spineFrame` needed **no** step (a radial hold has no length input); the reveal poster and stills stay step 0, which is correct because a fresh fighter is step 0. Both script blocks re-parse clean under `new Function` after the stamp bump.
*(3) The built ladder, measured — `.qa/lengrow-build-proof.mjs`, 41 assertions, all pass, exit 0.* Step 0 **is** the shipped objects by identity · 13 entries, 19 circles at every step · **456 `l`/`r` values bit-identical across the whole ladder** and the drawn lateral envelope unchanged to 1e-12, so it never thickens — the owner's stated fear, disproved numerically · the drawn tip grows exactly `(1.012)^s`, worst deviation **2.2e-16**, 147.472 → **170.17px (+15.39%)** · shaft factors 1.0197 → **1.2520**, derived as `k_s = 1 + ((1.012)^s - 1)·tip/J` and never authored · every head point translates by one delta and every shaft point scales by one factor · no gap opens (shaft end 120.74 ≥ head rear 108.98 at the top step) · `REACH.spear` 210.412 → **233.107** (+22.70px) and `MINR.spear` held at **2.20** · the shared row is never mutated (`len` 147.4720, tip 147.4720, outermost `d` 141.8) · `grow()` clamps at 12 and further hits do not overrun the baked geometry · `bladeCircles` at step 7 puts the outermost circle at x **716.08** vs **703.24** at step 0 while emitting the **same 19 radii**, and at step 12 matches the ladder table exactly (725.935) · a non-grower ignores a bogus step entirely (25 sword circles identical at steps 0 and 9).
*(4) The house gate at every step.* `inscribe.mjs` on the landed canonical exits **0**, all 22 weapons inside band, and the spear's own base row is unchanged at poke **-0.30px** / miss **4.25px** — direct proof the ladder never touched the shipped data. `.qa/lengrow-gate.mjs --mode rigid`, fed the **exact thirteen shaft factors read out of the live build** with `--jfrac 0.6106015170224026`, passes every step: n=19 and minR 2.20 constant, poke **-0.27…-0.30px** (band +0.12), miss **4.20…4.29px** (band 4.6). Caution for future readers: the gate's `stretch%` column reports the **shaft** factor, so its top row reads +25.2% for what is a **+15.4% tip** growth — the two are not interchangeable, and the shaft number is the derived one. leadproof (30s), flipproof (24s) and chromecheck (31s) all PASS, exit 0.
*(5) The fingerprint result is 8/8 byte-identical, and it proves nothing about this build.* `.qa/fingerprint.mjs` regenerated against the landed file returned the same eight hashes **and** tick counts as `.qa/fp-stall-r25.json`; both files md5 `d120f6c525a1bd5f3d35459af699a171`. That is a coverage artefact, not neutrality, and the cause is structural: six of the eight cells are forced non-spear matchups, and neither natural cell ever rolls a spear — measured, `natural s7` rolls orb+orb then bow+boomerang and `natural s11` rolls shield+javelin, so **zero of the eight cells can observe length growth**. This is the same failure mode as the `cross-r24` shot-cross gap already on record, giving the suite a second blind axis. It is also why `f.lenStep` was **not** added to the hashed field list at `:99-100`: with no cell exercising it, hashing it would have moved nothing while implying coverage that does not exist. Both gaps are owed.
*(6) The differential that replaces it.* A **growth-off control** — `trait.len` zeroed so `grow()` never advances, `REACH.spear` restored to its base 210.412 — against the built sim, same seed, forced spear, three cells, restored afterwards and verified restored. Because step 0 of the ladder is the shipped data by identity, the control reproduces the pre-build geometry exactly, so any divergence **is** the growth. All three diverge, and early: `spear+hammer s9` at **t=286** of 14,689 rows · `spear+sword s21` at **t=290** of 18,000 · `spear+scythe s33` at **t=286** of 16,222. The growth side reaches the **ladder top (step 12)** in all three real battles; the control's max step is 0 in all three. One balance signal, single-cell and explicitly not a trend: in `spear+sword s21` the control **decides** 0-2 while the growth side runs the **full 18,000-tick budget undecided** — the longer spear made that particular battle less decisive, which sits on the stall axis the owner has already ruled stays deliberate until skills land.
*(7) No new baseline file was written, and why.* Because nothing moved, `.qa/fp-stall-r25.json` still describes the shipped build for the eight cells it covers, and the house rule is explicit that a landing which moves no fingerprint does not earn a twin file — the `sigil-r23` and `cross-r24` precedents. A byte-identical `.qa/fp-lengrow-r26.json` was generated, confirmed identical by md5, and **deleted** rather than adopted. `.qa` stays at **10 files** and the workspace at **28**, which matters because README's Batch 1 is capped at the owner's 10-file upload limit.
*(8) One probe defect, found and fixed, disclosed.* The round-reset assertion first **FAILED**, reporting spear `lenStep` still 10 at `G.round` → 2. The defect was in the probe's sampling point, not the sim: `G.round++` happens at the end of the `ko` phase and sets `phase='reveal', phaseT=0`, but `rollWeapons` — which zeroes `lenStep` at `:1251` — only runs on the **next** tick, via the reveal branch's `if (G.phaseT === 0) rollWeapons(G)` at `:1212`. Reading on the transition tick sees last round's step. The probe now ticks once more and reads post-roll (`phase reveal, phaseT 1`, `[10, 0] → [0, 0]`), and additionally asserts the reset is **not vacuous** — a step was genuinely carried in, max 10. Same lesson as the earlier `grow()`-before-first-roll crash in this probe: a harness that samples the wrong tick reports a sim bug that does not exist.
*(9) The four disclosures the owner asked to be told.* **(a)** The rate he authored is on the **observable tip** — 1.2% per damaging hit — and the shaft rate that delivers it is **derived, never authored**: **1.8868% per hit** on the shaft, compounding to 1.2520 at the top, because the rigid head contributes no length of its own. **(b)** `POPC`'s pop-in centre was **not** remapped; it is a cosmetic scale centre and pop-in happens at round start where `lenStep` is 0, so there is no functional impact — but a grown spear's pop-in scales about the base centre. **(c)** There is no `lenCap` constant: the **ladder top (12) is the geometry ceiling** and the **round reset is the balance bound**, exactly as ruled. **(d)** `f.lenStep` is **not** in the fingerprint's hashed fields, per (5).
*(10) Docs.* The length-growth section's heading, its "pre-baked ladder" paragraph and its cost map carry dated amendments and its status line is rewritten BUILT; the Current-state build bullet is rewritten for `lengrow-r26`; the "Next" bullet records step 3 as landed and corrects its own under-budgeting prediction; the standing-baseline paragraph carries the no-move/no-new-file note plus the vacuity warning. `scratch/docreflow.mjs` FIRST, then `scratch/navgen.mjs` (the heading changed, so the nav moves). README's canonical slot swaps to the new md5/stamp/line count and its baseline text is corrected to say the standing baseline is **unchanged**, and why.
*(11) Owed now.* The **484-cell re-run**, batched to cover `stall-r25` and `lengrow-r26` in one pass (owner-gated, ~7.16 hours across two machines) · **a spear-bearing cell in the fingerprint suite**, without which no future "behaviour-identical" claim can cover length growth · the **shot-cross coverage gap**, still open · the **rotational-DOF resolver rework**, whose translation-only fixed point scales with reach and whose spear reach just grew **10.8%** · **law 3's band widening** on the grown spear, expected by the `--band-dump` finding and still unmeasured · the first genuinely contextual **special skill**, which unblocks the hook machinery · elemental design · the kit-route rebuild.

**The spear now TRIPLES in length — `lengrow-r28`, per-step re-baked coverings, and the balance hole the raise accidentally closed (2026-10-28; owner, shown the +35% build: *"its not ridiculous enough"*, then picked **+200% (triple)** from a gate-proven menu via ask_user and delegated the step count with "you decide". Canonical md5 `f50612a2e954e7fd45b819234bada1ba` → **`4d0467dc5faafeb4480a72e2dcbbd8e5`**, build `lengrow-r27` → **`lengrow-r28`**, lines 3,217 → **3,279**. The pre-edit file was copied to `/tmp/canon-pre-r28.html` before the first stroke and was reused as the r27 comparison build in (8).)**
*(1) Why a rate change alone could not deliver it, measured before the number was offered.* With the covering held — radii untouched, spacing scaled by the rigid map — `.qa/lengrow-gate.mjs --mode rigid` last passes at shaft **k 1.6469 = tip +39.5%** (miss 4.59 of the 4.60 band) and fails at **+40%** (4.61). POKE never binds anywhere on the sweep (-0.26…-0.30px against +0.12) and `minR` never moves, because the head cluster translates rigidly. So the binding constraint is **MISS**: the shaft circles spread apart faster than their held radii can bridge. `lengrow-r27`'s +35% was already at that wall, which is why the menu offered to the owner was not "pick any number" but four gate-proven magnitudes, each with its circle count and substep cost, plus the two mechanisms that could go further.
*(2) The route taken: re-bake with the house packer, so no new algorithm enters the sim.* Two mechanisms could pass +39.5% — densify the covering at boot (interpolate circles where the remapped spacing outruns the held radii) or re-pack each step offline. The second was chosen because it reuses the **sanctioned** packer: `.qa/lengrow-bake.mjs` drives `.qa/bake-rows.mjs` → `inscribe.mjs`'s raster-EDT greedy union-inscribed regen, the same code that baked all 22 shipped rows. A boot-time densifier would have been a new packing heuristic inside the sim whose failure mode is exactly the gap hit the owner made top priority. Feasibility was probed to **5× length before anything was offered**: the re-baked covering passes the gate at +50/+100/+200/+300/+400% with miss 4.10/4.09/3.99/3.94/3.83px and 29/37/51/68/86 circles — the bow already ships 69, so even +300% is inside the roster's normal range.
*(3) Art and circles agree, and here is the argument rather than the assertion.* The stretch is injected **post-normalisation and pre-weaponScale** during the bake, while the boot ladder maps the **booted** (post-weaponScale) polys. Those coincide exactly because the rigid map is homogeneous of degree 1 in (d, J): with `J = maxD * lenJ` in both spaces and `maxD_boot = 1.3 * maxD_ingr`, `map(1.3d) = 1.3*map(d)`. So a row baked against the stretched INGR art *is* a row for the stretched booted art. Verified from the live boot rather than trusted: the ladder's step-s circles are the baked objects **by identity**, step 0 is still the shipped row and shipped polys by identity, and `bladeCircles` at step 7 emits exactly 36 circles whose radii match `LEN_LADDER.circles[7]` element for element.
*(4) Every step gated against its OWN art — `node .qa/lengrow-bake.mjs --verify`, 12/12 PASS.* poke **-0.30px at all twelve steps** (band +0.12); miss **2.30 / 4.18 / 4.08 / 3.90 / 2.30 / 3.55 / 4.25 / 4.16 / 4.00 / 3.95 / 4.08 / 4.11px** (band 4.6) — every one at or better than the shipped base row's 4.25, because a fresh pack fits the stretched silhouette instead of inheriting the base row's spacing. This is now the shipped-ladder proof; `.qa/lengrow-gate.mjs` has changed role and proves the **ceiling of the remap route** instead.
*(5) The rebuilt ladder, measured — `.qa/lengrow-build-proof.mjs`, **46/46**, exit 0.* Drawn tip 147.472 → **442.548px (+200.09%)** · top shaft factor **4.2769**, tip factor 3.0009, derived per step and never authored · the tip grows exactly `(1.0959)^s`, worst deviation **8.88e-16** · **the art never thickens**: no poly point's lateral coordinate moves at any step (the full l-set compared across all 12 steps, 0 drift) and the drawn lateral envelope is `|l|max 12.8118` at every step · no gap opens (shaft end **393.12** ≥ head rear **372.27**) · circle counts **19 / 22 / 24 / 26 / 28 / 31 / 33 / 36 / 38 / 40 / 45 / 48 / 50**, never shrinking as the spear lengthens · every circle respects the packer's 2.0 floor and none is wider than the shipped row's widest · `REACH.spear` 210.412 → **505.488** (+295.08px) · the shared row unmutated (`len` 147.4720, tip 147.4720, outermost `d` 141.8) · `grow()` clamps at 12 and the round resets (`[12, 0] → [0, 0]`, non-vacuous) · a non-grower ignores a bogus step (25 sword circles identical at steps 0 and 9).
*(6) MINR changed meaning, and that is a correctness fix rather than a detail.* `lengrow-r26` recorded "MINR needs no correction — every radius is held". That was true of the remap ladder and is **false** of a re-baked one: step 5 packs a circle at **r 2.00** where the shipped row's thinnest is 2.20. `MINR` sizes the parry substep delta at :1344 and the shot window at :1368, so keeping the base value would have made the budget too coarse for exactly the step that needs it finest. `MINR.spear` is now the **ladder-wide minimum, 2.00** — conservative at every step. The build-proof asserts it as `min(base, ladder)` rather than as an unchanged value, so a future re-bake that packs thinner still cannot slip through.
*(7) The rest of the battery, and the fingerprints.* `inscribe.mjs` exits **0** on the landed canonical with the spear's **base** row unchanged (n=19, minR 2.20, poke -0.30px, miss 4.25px) — the baked rows sit beside the shipped data, not on top of it. leadproof (33s), flipproof (24s) and chromecheck (4s) all PASS, exit 0; both script blocks re-parse under `new Function`. Fingerprints: **0 of 8 moved for the third consecutive build**, `.qa/fp-stall-r25.json` md5 unchanged, no twin file written. A 3× reach change that leaves all eight hashes bit-identical is the sharpest demonstration yet that those hashes measure the suite's coverage and not the build's behaviour — the suite never rolls a spear, and `f.lenStep` is not in the hashed field list.
*(8) The 50-cell balance sweep, and the hole it found in `lengrow-r27`.* Same harness, same ten opponents (hammer, sword, scythe, axe, mace, katana, dagger, shield, flail, staff) × seeds 1-5, forced spear in slot 0, run against **both** builds. **Spear win rate: 3 of 50 (6%) at +35%, 33 of 50 (66%) at +200%.** The 484-cell baseline recorded the spear's holder win rate as **65.9%** when its `dmg` was 0.7 — so tripling the length has put the spear back at roughly its historical strength at `dmg` 0.3, while +35% had left it **the weakest weapon in the game**. The `dmg` 0.7 → 0.3 cut and the growth rate are therefore **coupled**, and `lengrow-r27` shipped an under-powered spear that nothing in the house suite could see. *Two honesty limits on that comparison:* the 65.9% figure comes from the full 484-cell natural matrix under `measure.mjs`, whereas this is 50 forced cells at 5 seeds, so the agreement is suggestive rather than exact; and the sweep's "growth OFF" control zeroes `trait.len` while leaving `dmg` at 0.3, which is a **crippled** spear (it wins **0 of 50** on both builds) — so ON-vs-OFF deltas conflate growth with the damage cut and must not be read as the value of growth alone. The r27-vs-r28 ON comparison is the clean one.
*(9) Stalls went DOWN, and the small-sample scare that said otherwise.* Undecided at the 18,000-tick budget: **+35% → 18/50 (36%)**, **+200% → 14/50 (28%)**. The triple-length spear relieves stalls rather than causing them, which matters because `REACH` grew 2.40× and the law-3 diagnosis says the resolver's translation-only fixed point scales with reach. This **corrects a scare from mid-build**: the 3-cell differential in `.qa/lengrow-build-proof.mjs` section N showed two of three cells running the full budget undecided at +200%, and at +35% the same three cells had all decided. Three cells is not a sample; fifty says the opposite. The lesson is the one already on record from the proof-slice bias — a differential proves *divergence*, and must not be read as a *trend*.
*(10) A boot guard, so the ceiling cannot be exceeded silently.* `LEN_REMAP_CEIL = 1.395` is the swept +39.5% wall, and the ladder builder now **throws at boot** if a growing weapon's top step exceeds it without baked rows, and throws if `LEN_CIRCLES` carries any number of rows other than `LEN_STEPS`. The failure mode being guarded is a silent gap hit — the one thing the owner made top priority — so it is worth a hard stop rather than a comment. `.qa/lengrow-bake.mjs` reads `trait.len`, `lenJ` and `LEN_STEPS` **out of the canonical**, so the baked rows cannot drift from the row that consumes them, and it names the **build stamp** rather than a date in its provenance line because this sandbox's clock runs ahead of the owner's local date (a recorded trap) and would otherwise bake a wrong day into the file.
*(11) Disclosures. (a)* The step count was delegated and stays **12**: the ceiling bounds *total* growth, so more steps at 9.6% would need re-baking anyway, and 24 would halve the per-hit drama and double the baked data; 6 would plateau at hit 6 and stop being a snowball. *(b)* The substep budget is now computed from `REACH` 505.488 for the spear, **2.40× base**, so spear battles cost more substeps than they did — unmeasured in wall-clock terms here, and the 484-cell re-run will show it. *(c)* `POPC`'s pop-in centre is still not remapped: cosmetic, and pop-in happens at round start where `lenStep` is 0. *(d)* A grown spear's tip reaches **505px** from the body centre in a 1000px arena, so from mid-arena it touches the wall and the art will overhang the rim — nothing clips it, and at +300/+400% it would extend past the arena entirely. That is a look decision, not a correctness one, and it is now the owner's to call. *(e)* `.qa/lengrow-gate.mjs` and `.qa/lengrow-proof.mjs` still default to a `rate` that tracks the row, but their **remap** measurements no longer describe the shipped ladder past +39.5%.
*(12) Docs and tooling.* The length-growth section's heading, rate paragraph, trilemma and "pre-baked ladder" paragraph carry dated amendments — including one that **reverses r26's claim that `bake-rows.mjs` is not on this build's path** — and the status line is rewritten. Current state and "Next" are updated, and the standing-baseline paragraph records the third no-move. New tool **`.qa/lengrow-bake.mjs`** (dry run / `--verify` / `--write`, marker-delimited splice that preserves every other byte; verified: only the 22-line block changed on re-run). `.qa` goes 10 → **11 files**, workspace 28 → **29**. `scratch/docreflow.mjs` FIRST, then `scratch/navgen.mjs` (46 → 47 entries).
*(13) Owed — and then DEFERRED by owner ruling the same day, hours after this entry was written: **no balancing during the skills pass** (*"we are not trying to balance the game at all right now, we are making the skills/mechanics, a new balancing will come afterwards"*), so items 1-4 below move to the END of the pass and the pass ends with one new fingerprint baseline rather than two. They are recorded here as they stood, because the deferral does not cancel them.* The **484-cell re-run** covering `stall-r25` + `lengrow-r28`, whose law-3 band should be expected to widen substantially at 2.40× spear reach, and which is the only instrument that can confirm the 6%-to-66% win-rate finding on a real matrix · **a spear-bearing fingerprint cell**, owed three times over · the shot-cross gap · the **rotational-DOF resolver rework**, now against a 505.488px spear reach · **re-measure the spear's `dmg` 0.3 against its growth rate as one coupled pair** in the damage pass, since (8) shows they cannot be tuned independently · the first special skill · elemental design · the kit-route rebuild. If the owner wants past +400%, the same bake route extends; the next real constraint is the arena, not the covering.

**The first special skill — `skill-r29`: a fighter's skill raises the spear's growth cap from 12 steps to 18, and the carrier was chosen by measurement (2026-10-28; owner picked "C2 then C3 staged", then rejected C2 outright with *"i dont like this at all, each round weapons traits should reset"*, then picked cap 18 from a measured menu and deferred the carrier to *"decide after I see it"*. Canonical md5 `4d0467dc5faafeb4480a72e2dcbbd8e5` → **`11f87c88b2bb091988fd0c34abdac298`**, 3,279 → **3,332 lines**, stamp `lengrow-r28` → **`skill-r29`**.)**
**[RETIRED at `nocombo-r34`, 2026-10-31 — the owner's NO COMBOS ruling (see "Fighter skill vs weapon trait"): `skill.lenSteps` is deleted from the row and from the sim, every cap is uniform at `LEN_STEPS` 12, the six carrier-era rows (steps 13–18) are purged from `LEN_CIRCLES`, `REACH.spear` returns to 505.488, and Speedy's label returns to '+SPD / Hit'. Everything below is history. What survives it: the authored-label machinery, the carrier MEASUREMENT ( Heavy is self-defeating — his own damage walls his growth at hit ~13), and the live doctrine that damage-coupled growth is self-limiting. The retirement's own record is the `nocombo-r34` entry below.]**
*The mechanic.* `skill.lenSteps` on a fighter row **adds ladder steps** to the weapon trait's growth: `LEN_CAP[i] = LEN_STEPS + skill.lenSteps`, the ladder is built once per weapon to `LEN_STEPS_TOP` (18), and `grow()` clamps each fighter at their own cap. This is the first real **hook** — r26's comment "no hook machinery" was true of the *rate* (a weapon-row number) and false of the *cap*, which is now fighter data reaching into what the trait can do. Carrier **Speedy**, `lenSteps: 6` → cap 18 = **+420%**, drawn tip **766.631px**, shaft k 7.8760, **89 circles** at the top step. Six new baked rows; rows 1-12 are **byte-identical to r28** by diff, so a non-carrier's geometry is literally unchanged. Legend labels are now **authored** per row (`short`) with a boot guard — the old `spin ? '+SPD' : '+DMG'` derivation would have printed a wrong label for any third axis.
*Sizing was measured, not guessed.* The spear already **saturates the 12-step base in 85% of rounds** (115 rounds, 50 forced-spear cells, mean max step 11.28), so the only headroom a special could use was steps 13-24. Damaging hits per round: median **24**, and 24 is a **lethality wall** rather than a coincidence — base dmg 1 plus the trait's +0.3 per hit crosses 100 hp exactly at hit 24 (cumulative 106.8), so *"no cap let it snowball"* already had a natural stop and any cap above 24 is unreachable by construction. Cap 18 was chosen as reachable in ~75% of rounds and inside the covering feasibility already probed to +400%; the bake then confirmed it (all 18 steps gate-PASS on their own stretched art, poke -0.30px throughout, worst miss **4.35px** of the 4.6 band at step 15).
*The carrier was chosen by measurement and the obvious pick was WRONG.* Heavy was tried first and is **self-defeating**: his own +0.9 dmg per hit makes cumulative damage cross 100 hp at hit ~13, so his spear walls at step 13 — measured **0/68 rounds saturating, median top step 13**. Speedy's damage stays at base, so he lives long enough in the round to grow it out: **median 18, 54/68 (79%) saturating**. The rule this leaves behind: **a length skill belongs on the low-damage cast**, and every future carrier needs the same check, because a fighter's damage growth sets the hit budget the ladder has to fit inside.
*One measurement retracted, one misattribution corrected — both by cross-check rather than by argument.* (a) A hit counter derived from `|f.spin| / 0.30` reported a plausible median but was **unsound**: round 1 always read exactly 36 and later rounds read 0 while the sim's own `lenStep` said 7 and 12. Spin is not monotonic and the per-round base was mis-sampled. It was replaced by lifting the clamp in a scratch copy and reading `f.lenStep` directly — a derived counter must be validated against sim state before it is believed. (b) The r28→r29 differential showed **20/20** forced-spear cells diverging at tick 286, which read as "the skill changes battles". The **non-carrier control** — spear in Heavy's slot, cap 12 in *both* builds, rows 1-12 byte-identical — diverges **10/10 at the same tick with `lenStep 0/0` in both**. The cause is `REACH.spear` rising 505.488 → **829.571**: REACH is a per-**weapon** table feeding the substep budget, so a taller ladder changes the numerics of **every** spear battle, not just the carrier's. Recorded consequences: build-proof section N's claim that "any divergence is the growth itself" was **false** and is corrected, the side effect is now an assertion rather than a footnote, and **no spear cell can be claimed behaviour-identical across a ladder raise**. Measured cost: 0.2590 → **0.3561 ms/tick, +37.5%** on spear battles.
*Proofs (Tier 0+1, since geometry moved):* build-proof **58/58** · `inscribe` exit 0 · `lengrow-bake --verify` **18/18** · leadproof / flipproof / chromecheck PASS · the art never thickens across all 18 steps (the full lateral l-set compared step by step) · `MINR.spear` still **2.00** · fingerprints **0 of 8** for the fourth consecutive build, no twin file written, still blind.
*Arena, flagged not assumed.* REACH 829.571 is **1.66×** the 500px half-arena, so a fully grown carrier spear hangs ~330px past the wall from mid-arena. The owner accepted +200% (505px) on playtest — *"it looks good now"* — but +420% is 1.66× that and is his call on sight, not mine by extrapolation.
*Machinery for the remaining 21 weapons, which is the point of landing it this way.* `LEN_CAP` is computed from the rows, the ladder is built once per weapon to the max cap, and **both the bake tool and the build-proof now loop over growers and fighters instead of naming the spear and Speedy** — so a second carrier is a row edit and a second growing weapon is a row edit plus a re-bake. `lengrow-bake.mjs` gained `--emit FILE` (write the block without touching the canonical, so a code edit and a geometry edit land **atomically** — otherwise the boot guard rejects the file mid-transition) and `--steps N` (probe a taller ladder; refuses `--write`). **Scope kept deliberately narrow:** length is the spear's alone by owner ruling (max weapon length = the spear's), so the tool's one-grower guard stays and fails loudly rather than silently baking one weapon's rows for another. The other weapons' skills — shot multipliers, knock — need **no baking at all**: a row field plus a hook, with no covering to re-pack and no gate to pass.
*Owed:* the deferred bundle is unchanged (484-cell re-run, resolver rework, spear-bearing fingerprint cell — all at the END of the skills pass). New: the carrier is data-decided but the owner may overrule on playtest; law-3's band at REACH 829.571 is unmeasured; and the +37.5% spear-battle sim cost is disclosed but not optimised.

**Two skill seams land ahead of the remaining 21 weapons — `seam-r30`, behaviour-identical, and the readiness finding that made them necessary (2026-10-28; owner, told the code was not yet ready for 22 unique skills: *"build it first, and note it down in the docs and clean the docs and codebase, i wanna start a new chat"*. Canonical md5 `11f87c88b2bb091988fd0c34abdac298` → **`50adefa2aefaa5d8fe1aca1e5c8479cb`**, 3,332 → **3,362 lines**, stamp `skill-r29` → **`seam-r30`**.)**
*The readiness finding.* `grow()` (sim :1703) was the **only** consumer of `skill`/`trait` data in the entire sim, so a "skill" could only ever mean one of three numbers on one trigger ("damaging hit"). Three gaps stood between that and 22 unique skills: **(1)** one trigger only; **(2)** `emit()` is an append-only log, not a bus — nine kinds (bounce, hit ×2, ko, parry, roll, swat, wall, **shotFire**) pushed to `G.events` for VFX and audit, with no subscriber mechanism, so nothing can *react* to a moment; **(3)** knock has no single seam at all — there is no `CFG.knock`, it is applied diffusely at shot landings (:2132), parry shoves and wall bounces, which makes it the expensive one of the three owner-authorized hook targets. **Deliberately NOT built:** a general hook or plugin framework. That is the over-engineering the owner rejects; a seam gets added when a skill needs it.
*Seam A — authored `brief`.* `APP.meta.weapons[].brief` (sim :3237) was derived from the same binary `trait.spin ? '+SPD / Hit' : '+DMG / Hit'` test that `skill-r29` had just removed from `SKILL_SHORT` — a second site of the same class, missed at the time. And it was **already wrong**: the spear's trait is length growth, and its exported brief read `'+DMG / Hit'` with no mention of growing. Now an authored `brief` on the row wins, the derivation survives as a fallback for plain spin/dmg rows (so this cost no 22-row churn), and a boot guard **throws** if a row carries a trait axis outside `{spin, dmg}` with no authored brief. The spear ships `'+DMG +LEN / Hit'`, matching the fighter-row `short` convention.
*Seam B — shot cadence.* `shot.mult` on the weapon row and `skill.shotMult` on the fighter **add** (owner ruling: stacking adds), and the effective value divides the launch accumulator's `need`, so 2 fires the same volley twice as often. Cadence rather than extra projectiles per release, because `shot.revs` already owns "how much spin per volley" and `shot.volley` already owns the fan — a third way to mean "more shots" would be one too many. A boot guard rejects a non-positive effective multiplier for **every (weapon, fighter) pair**, since the release loop divides by it and would otherwise hang. **No row declares it yet**: the seam ships inert, which is exactly why the landing is behaviour-identical.
*Measured, because an inert seam is indistinguishable from dead code.* New **`.qa/seam-proof.mjs`** (**8/8**, Tier 0, ~2s) assigns a synthetic `shot.mult` and counts throws over 3000 ticks of bow-vs-hammer: **13 → 28 → 92** for mult 0 → 1 → 3. It also reports the **superlinearity**: 2.15× realised for a declared 2×, ~7× for a declared 4×, because more shots → more damaging hits → more spin growth → more shots. Any future carrier must be sized on the realised factor, not the declared one. Both guards are proven to **FIRE** by injecting a bad row into the extracted source and requiring the boot error — a guard that never throws reads as coverage and is worse than no guard. Rest of Tier 0: build-proof **58/58** unchanged, chromecheck PASS (legend 812B), fingerprints **0 of 8** and meaningful for the first time in five builds.
*A correction, disclosed before it entered the record.* I told the owner the shot launch loop "emits nothing at all". It emits `shotFire` at both the volley and single-shot paths, with `by/sid/x/y/dmg/fx/fy/fAng`; my grep pattern `[a-z0-9]*` had missed the capital F, so I reported 8 event kinds instead of 9 and drew the wrong conclusion about the launch site. The "not a subscribable bus" half of the assessment stands and is what Seam B works around.
*Owed:* unique skills for the remaining **21 weapons**, each now a row field plus one consumer site plus one assertion — the `lenSteps` pattern, not a per-weapon investigation · **knock** needs a decision about *which* knock (shot landing, parry shove, wall bounce) before it can be a seam at all · a **second trigger** only when a skill needs one, never speculatively · more bots, after the weapons · the deferred bundle unchanged (484-cell re-run, resolver rework, spear fingerprint cell — all at the END of the skills pass).

**The pairing step — a match can now pick 2 of N cast members. `pair-r31`, behaviour-identical on the shipped pairing (2026-10-28; owner, after the assistant recommended building it ahead of more bots: *"go"*. Canonical md5 `18096bdbf0f5cb80d33a8d32cee86609` → **`5a2ce808ad92097f6389b516075f0a68`**, 3,410 → **3,446 lines**, stamp `seam-r30` → **`pair-r31`**.)**

*What changed.* `newGame(seed, { cast: [i, j] })` picks which two cast members play and on which side; `DEFAULT_CAST = [0, 1]` is the shipped pairing, so every battle so far is the default and the landing is behaviour-identical by construction. The mechanism is a **field split**: `f.idx` is the CAST IDENTITY (the row in `FIGHTERS`) and `f.slot` is the SIDE (0 = the left spawn, 1 = the right). They were one field, and 28 sites now read `slot` where they mean left/right while 7 keep reading `idx` where they mean *who*. Slot-keyed: `SPAWN`, the roll order `w0`/`w1`, the launch direction sign, `wins`/`winner`/`roundWinner`, `1 - f.slot`, `view`/`flashV`/`flipAnim`/`koStill.pose`, the `C0`/`C1` circle scratch, `fs[st.o]`, and every event actor ref (`by`/`to`/`o`/`winner`/`loser`). Idx-keyed: `COLS`, `TINTS`, `LEN_CAP`, `SKILL_SHORT`, `FIGHTERS[idx].skill`, `meta.fighters`. **`G.cast[slot]` is the only map between them.** Guarded at boot *and* on every construction (wrong length, out-of-range, non-integer and non-array all throw; `null`/`undefined` mean "the default", which is what the lever relies on). `APP.setCast([i, j])` is the console lever — no UI chip, deliberately, while the cast is two; it persists across seed changes, so the pairing is a session choice and the seed is per-battle.

*The crux, which is why this was not a nine-site rename.* One field was already being read **both ways**: the `hit` event's `to` fed `flashV[e.to]` (an identity-coloured flash, idx-keyed) *and* `koStill.pose[e.to]` (a slot-keyed array, because `view[i]` is built by iterating `fighters[i]`). Those coincide only while idx === slot, so a swapped pairing would have aliased two actors into one scratch entry — the kill still would have frozen the wrong body. The same ambiguity sat on `st.o`, which is read as `fs[st.o]` in `shotHitBody` *and* as `fs[1 - st.o]` in `shotStep` *and* as `G.fighters[st.o]` in the copy-weapon draw. The rule adopted and documented in `newFighter`: **event actor refs carry SLOTS**, because every consumer of them is a slot-keyed array (`measure.mjs` included — it indexes `curW = [e.w0, e.w1]` with `e.by`). A cast identity in an event is recoverable as `G.cast[slot]`; the reverse is not.

*Measured.* New **`.qa/pair-proof.mjs`** (**31/31**, Tier 0, ~25s) runs pairings where slot ≠ idx — the half the fingerprint suite cannot see, since it only ever plays the default. It sweeps **103,076 ticks** across 14 battles (10 natural-roll + 4 forced-mirror that really finish, so the `ko`/`winner` path is not vacuous: 285 hits, 10 K.O.s, 4/4 decided) and asserts the foe invariant `fs[f.slot] === f && fs[1 - f.slot] !== f` every tick, that every event actor ref is a slot with `to === 1 - by`, that `st.o` indexes `fs`, that each fighter wears the weapon its SLOT was rolled, and that rule 6's counter-rotation holds per side. **The decisive number:** with forced mirror hammers the weapon trait is identical on both sides, so damage-per-hit isolates the actor's skill — it measures exactly **0.70/hit on Speedy's slot and 1.60/hit on Heavy's** (hammer `trait.dmg` 0.7 + skill 0.0 / 0.9), and under `cast: [1, 0]` those two rates **exchange sides**. The skill travels with the actor. Same seed, swapped pairing: 13,767 ticks, wins 0–2, winner slot 1 → 17,770 ticks, wins 2–1, winner slot 0. A **mirror pairing `[0, 0]` is legal** and fights to a decision (14,157 ticks, 2–0), which matters because nothing may key the foe lookup off `idx`. `chromecheck.mjs` grew a **pairing section** (still PASS, ~26s) covering the one surface `pair-proof` cannot see: under `setCast([1, 0])` the scorebug names exchange (`Speedy / Heavy` → `Heavy / Speedy`), the identity swatches exchange with them (`#a8892f #ff6b4a` → `#ff6b4a #a8892f`, so colour travels with the actor and not with the side), the legend rows exchange, the slot-keyed score stays put, the winner overlay names the ACTOR (`"Heavy"` = `cast[0]`), and `setCast(null)` restores the default. Rest of Tier 0/1: build-proof **58/58**, seam-proof **8/8**, bake `--verify` **18/18**, inscribe GATE PASS, leadproof PASS (354 hits / 424 parries / 100 swats recomputed from the stored event fields — which is independent evidence the slot-keyed `by`/`to` are self-consistent), flipproof PASS.

*Fingerprints: 8/8, 0 of 8 diverged — the sixth consecutive, and meaningful for exactly one half of the change.* The suite runs the default pairing, where `slot` and `idx` hold the same number, so 8/8 is real evidence that **the shipped pairing did not move** (the change is inert by construction, and the cells do cover shots, kills and the wins path). It is **silent by construction on the swapped pairing** — no fingerprint cell can ever exercise it, which is the whole reason `pair-proof` exists. `measure.mjs` stays valid untouched for the same reason: it reads `FIGHTERS[G.winner]` and `curW[e.by]`, both correct while `cast` is `[0, 1]`. **Recorded as an assumption, not a fix:** `measure.mjs` therefore reports the default pairing only, and would misname winners under any other; it is owner-gated and no other pairing has ever been run through it.

*Owed:* the cast is still two — **more bots need an owner-chosen colour each** (`COLS`/`TINTS` hold exactly two entries, as do `SPAWN`, `view`, `flashV`, `flipAnim` and `C0`/`C1`), so a third bot is a row + a colour + a two-entry-array widening, not a structural change · no cast-selection UI (the console lever is deliberate while N = 2; a picker is a chrome design question) · a 3-way brawl remains structural · the deferred bundle is unchanged (484-cell re-run, resolver rework, spear fingerprint cell — all at the END of the skills pass).

**Whole-file code-review cleanup #5 — and the `pair-r31` renderer bug it caught. `review-r32` (2026-10-28, owner order: *"Review spinclash preview.html for bugs, code smells, dead/unused code, duplicate or redundant logic, unnecessary complexity, unused imports/references, and potential performance issues, remove unused code. Refactor and consolidate where appropriate, simplify the implementation, improve readability and maintainability, optimize inefficient code, and fix any issues found without changing the intended behavior. Read the docs and see if they align with everything also, and trim old text or sections we no longer need."* Canonical md5 `5a2ce808ad92097f6389b516075f0a68` → **`77eaf6b2eb2cfa4a8ae36200bc92a35d`**, 3,446 → **3,457 lines**, stamp `pair-r31` → **`review-r32`**.)**

*The bug — one landing old, and three proofs walked past it.* `pair-r31` split `f.slot` (side) from `f.idx` (cast identity) and converted 28 sites. It missed the **renderer's cast-table reads**: the VS-poster reveal indexes `COLS[i]`, `TINTS[i]` and `FIGHTERS[i]` by `i`, the poster *half*, and `drawShot`/`drawCastFlash` index `COLS[st.o]` by the shot's owner — and both `i` and `st.o` are **slots**, while `COLS`/`TINTS`/`FIGHTERS` are **cast**-keyed. Thirteen sites. Under `DEFAULT_CAST = [0, 1]` the two coincide, so the shipped game is correct and nothing observable was wrong; call `APP.setCast([1, 0])` and the reveal poster names, colours and describes the **wrong bot on each half** — Speedy's name, gold disc and skill prose over the side Heavy actually fights from. **Why nothing caught it:** fingerprints hash *sim state* and only ever play the default pairing; chromecheck's default section reads the scorebug and legend (which `pair-r31` did convert correctly); `pair-proof` sections A–H are sim-level and never look at a canvas. The class is *renderer reads of a cast table keyed by a slot*, and every existing instrument was blind to it by construction.

*The fix, and the proof that the fix is not decorative.* All thirteen reads now go through `G.cast` — `vsHalf` and the poster loop take a documented `const ci = G.cast[i]` local, and the shot sigils read `COLS[G.cast[st.o]]`. `pair-proof.mjs` grew **section I**, which loads block 1 against a **recording canvas stub** (capturing `fillStyle`/`fillText` with coordinates, `measureText` returning a real width so the poster takes its measured centring branch), captures `requestAnimationFrame` and hand-steps the real frame loop 120 frames into the reveal at a fixed seed. It asserts the left half's name is `FIGHTERS[G.cast[0]].name` and the right's `G.cast[1]` — default reads SPEEDY/HEAVY, `[1, 0]` reads HEAVY/SPEEDY — that the skill prose travels with the same actor, and it adds a **comment-aware static tripwire**: a string-and-comment-stripping scanner audits all **23** code reads of `COLS`/`TINTS`/`FIGHTERS`/`SKILL_SHORT`/`LEN_CAP` and requires every index to be an identity (`…idx`, `…cast…`, the poster's `ci`, or the roster build's own `FIGHTERS.map((f, i) => …)`). **Proven to fire, per the house rule:** run against the pre-fix `pair-r31` canonical, section I reports **4 FAILs** and the tripwire names all thirteen offending sites by line. `pair-proof` is now **37/37**.

*Dead code, duplication and the correction to cleanup #4's scan.* **Removed:** `const rect = (d0, d1, l0, l1) => …` (canonical :220), an art helper from the original weapon-authoring trio `rect`/`star`/`build` that no row calls — `star` and `build` are both live. **This falsifies cleanup #4's headline claim** that "all 383 top-level declarations were scanned… exactly ONE has none — `geom`". `geom` *is* load-bearing and stays; but the scan that cleared `rect` counted bare tokens, and `rect` has four look-alikes that are not references to it (`g.rect(…)`, two `ctx.rect(…)`, and the CSS `clip: rect(0,0,0,0)` in `.sr-only`). A strict scan — word-bounded and excluding property access — leaves only the declaration. **Recorded so the next scan does not repeat it: token-counting a short identifier that is also a canvas method name and a CSS function will always produce a false "referenced".** **Consolidated:** the reveal/fight branch in `draw()` opened with a *byte-identical* `for (const f of G.fighters) drawBody(f, view[f.slot].x, view[f.slot].y);` in both arms; bodies draw first in every phase, so the loop is hoisted above the branch and each arm keeps only its own layer. Draw order is unchanged (bodies, then poster **or** weapons + shots), and the weapon arm now takes one `const v = view[f.slot]` local instead of five property chains. Cleanup #4's normalised 3-line-window scan had missed this because the two copies are separated by `drawReveal()` / the branch line rather than being adjacent.

*Audited and found clean — recorded so the next pass does not redo it.* All **34** `CFG` keys are read. No weapon-row field is unread (the full read set is `brief, chain, chainTie, dmg, headLead, hold, lead, len, lenJ, line, name, omega, poly, shot, trait, tuck`). No dead CSS selectors (19 classes, all with consumers). No `console.*`, no `debugger`, no TODO/FIXME, no loose `==` outside the four idiomatic `!= null` guards (which are *correct* — `st.o` can legitimately be `0`, so a truthiness test would drop slot 0). The per-substep hot path (`stepPhysics` → `bladeVsBlade` → `fireShots` → `shotStep`) allocates nothing but event payloads, and `bladeCircles` still writes into a caller-supplied array. **Deliberately NOT consolidated:** `bladeVsBody`'s damage block and `shotHitBody` look like duplicates and are not — they knock along different axes (the body line vs the shot's flight line), emit different payloads (attacker pose fields vs shot fields), only the blade path carries the `W.shot || hold === 'tangential'` gate, and `measure.mjs`'s law-4 replica mirrors that gate exactly. Merging them would buy ten lines and cost a branch-heavy signature over two different physical events.

*Deliberately NOT removed.* `APP.setShotArt`, `APP.setAccent` and `APP.shotArtOf` have **zero callers** in the file and zero in any harness — their consumer was the arena review bench, retired at the 2026-10-28 checkpoint clean. They are kept because the **kit-route rebuild is owed** and that bench is its judge; deleting the hooks would make the rebuild re-derive them. Flagged here rather than silently kept. Also kept, extending #3/#4's lists: `geom` (the harness export surface) · `W.chainTie.worstI` (the diagnostic a red chain-tie gate needs) · `info().flipT` (flipproof reads it) · the orb's `tuck: false` (a documented no-op clip, kept in case its art grows inward) · the four largest functions · `drawPolySet`'s eleven positional parameters (an options object would allocate in the draw path).

*Perf — one boundary worth stating, because "optimize inefficient code" has a hard limit here.* **No sim arithmetic was touched, and none should be.** Replacing `Math.hypot` with `Math.sqrt(dx*dx + dy*dy)`, reassociating a sum, or hoisting a multiplication out of a loop can all change the last ulp, and the sim is fingerprint-load-bearing: a one-ulp drift diverges a battle within ticks and invalidates the standing baseline. The only legitimate optimisations are ones that cannot change a float result — avoiding allocation, caching a lookup, removing dead work — and the allocation scan says there is nothing left to win. The measured cost of the one thing that *did* move geometry this pass (`skill-r29`'s taller ladder) is already disclosed at +37.5% ms/tick on spear battles.

*Proofs (Tier 0 + Tier 1, since renderer draw order moved).* **fingerprints 8/8, and the generated file is byte-identical to the standing baseline** (both md5 `d120f6c525a1bd5f3d35459af699a171`) — the seventh consecutive 0-of-8, so no twin file was written · **`pair-proof` 37/37**, and **4 FAILs against the pre-fix canonical** (the guard-fires proof) · build-proof **58/58** · seam-proof **8/8** · bake `--verify` **18/18** · inscribe **GATE: PASS** · leadproof **ALL PASS** (354 hits / 424 parries / 100 swats recomputed from stored fields) · flipproof **ALL PASS** · chromecheck **PASS** on both sections (default chrome + the `pair-r31` pairing section). `measure.mjs` neither run nor edited (standing rule).

*Docs — aligned, and 24,497 bytes of stale text retired verbatim.* **Five live claims corrected:** the CURRENT BUILD identity and stamp list · the **"Standing baseline:" line in the *Fingerprints — how to re-run* section, which still named `.qa/fp-lead-r22.json` as the standing baseline** — a direct contradiction of the same file's entry #51 item (7) and of README, and the kind of stale-identity error the doc pass exists to catch · the "Next:" headline that still said the pass was *moving on to* the first special skill · the dagger's roster-row ⚠ replaced with its discharge · the testing recipe's `pair-proof` entry, which now names section I. **Four blocks retired VERBATIM** to the chronicle archive (since removed — `git show docs-precleanup:docs/CHRONICLE-archive.md`) under "Retired Measured-facts entries — the pre-`review-r32` closed-design ledger", each leaving an indexed tombstone naming its live successor so no number is orphaned: the **skills-prep cost map** (a pre-build estimate superseded by the canonical's own REGISTRY block and by three measured landings; its spin-headroom paragraph survives in the dedicated `omegaCap` entry) · **the dagger's growth-axis ruling** (closed with no code change, so no mechanism left to describe; its discharge notes live in Open design questions 6) · **`lengrow-r27`** (a same-day rate raise superseded within hours by `r28`; the rate history stays in the doctrine section) · and — not an entry but the largest single line in the file — **the 8,927-byte `[Extended …]`/`[Unchanged …]`/`[Still unchanged …]` annotation chain on the "Standing baseline:" line**, one bracketed per-build annotation for nine consecutive landings (`lead-r22` → `pair-r31`) in a single paragraph.
Its `fp-lead-r22` claims had been reversed by the `stall-r25` re-baselining, its four "vacuous 8/8" warnings are restated compactly in item (7) above, and its `seam-r30`/`pair-r31` "meaningful vs silent-by-construction" distinctions are preserved in their own entries; the live question that section is asked — *which baseline describes the shipped build, and when may a new one be written?* — needed four lines, not nine thousand characters. Byte-preservation of all four blocks was asserted programmatically against the pre-edit file, not eyeballed. **Honest arithmetic on the trim:** GAMES.md went 361,094 → **≈354.9 KB**, a net **≈ −6.2 KB**, *not* the −24,497 that was retired (stated approximately because this very sentence is inside the file it describes) — this entry is itself 13,373 bytes and the five alignment amendments, the four tombstones and the archive pointers all add text, so they offset most of the retirement. **The win is content currency, not file size**, and any claim that this pass "slimmed the docs" substantially would be false. The archive grew 418,260 → 446,393 bytes, which is the point: dated records move to the archive rather than being deleted. **One tool-hygiene leak found and left UNFIXED pending the owner's call:** `.qa/bake-rows.mjs` writes a 310 KB intermediate `scratch/.bake-temp.html` and never removes it, while its sibling `.qa/lengrow-bake.mjs` cleans up its own `.lengrow-bake-temp.html` — so every `bake --verify` run leaves a dotfile behind that `ls scratch` will not even show.
The stray copy was deleted to restore the known 31-file workspace; the leak itself was **not** patched, because that temp may be a deliberate debugging aid (it is the exact input `inscribe --regen` saw) and an automatic `unlink` would destroy it — that is the owner's call, not a cleanup's. A further sweep of the file's longest lines was run and found **no** other accretion worth retiring — the remaining multi-kilobyte lines (the deferred balance pass, the CURRENT BUILD block, the M2 rulings, the axis-placement ruling, the probe index) are all live doctrine, not stale record. `SPEC.md`, `CONTRACT.md` and `BUILD.md` were re-checked and need nothing: their build mentions are accurate history about the art convention, not current-build claims.

*Stamp bumped, and why that is consistent rather than an exception.* Cleanups #1–#4 did not bump, and this one does, because it is not only a cleanup: it **fixes a bug the owner can observe** through the lever `pair-r31` gave him. `APP.setCast([1, 0])` on `pair-r31` posters the wrong bots; on `review-r32` it posters the right ones. Under the clarification recorded at `pair-r31` — "visible" means *observable by the owner*, not "changes a pixel on load" — that is a visible landing. The dead-code removal and the loop hoist ride along and are, on their own, stamp-neutral.

*Owed:* unchanged — unique traits for the other **21 weapons** · more bots (now a row + an owner-chosen colour + widening the seven two-entry arrays) · **knock** still needs a decision about *which* knock · no cast-selection UI (the console lever is deliberate while N = 2) · the deferred bundle (484-cell re-run, resolver rework, spear-bearing fingerprint cell) all at the END of the pass. New, from this pass: **the arena-bench rebuild should reclaim or delete `setShotArt`/`setAccent`/`shotArtOf`** — they are the file's only caller-less public API and should not survive another review unmarked.

**The staff's orb growth lands as `shotgrow-r33` — the second weapon trait of the pass, and the first one the fingerprint suite can see (2026-10-31; owner proposal: *"i hink its orbs/projectiles should get bigger each time they hit"*, balance deferred by his same word: *"dont think about balance etc and numbers, we will balance the game out once all the skill mechanincs are in place"*; owner picked shape A "each landing grows the NEXT orb" — pierce rejected, an orb is consumed by its hit and pierce would be a whole new mechanic; rate **20% per landing, PROVISIONAL**, his pick from a measured menu {12, 20, 35}. Canonical md5 `77eaf6b2eb2cfa4a8ae36200bc92a35d` → **`9f340f9d9c23c2e6804c1b553b219433`**, 3,457 → **3,506 lines**, stamp `review-r32` → **`shotgrow-r33`**.)**
*Mechanic — the `lenSteps` recipe, cheaper still: no ladder, no bake.* `trait.shotR: 0.20` on the staff row · `f.shotStep` (initialised in newFighter, reset per round in rollWeapons — the LAW) · `grow()` advances it — the trigger IS the orb landing, because the projectile rule means the staff's spine deals no damage, so its holder's damaging hits are exactly its landings · **ONE consumer**: the birth site computes `effR = W.shot.r × (1+rate)^f.shotStep` once per fighter-tick, used by both push paths and the volley rim clamp (without shotR, `effR === W.shot.r` bit-identically — `pow(x,0)===1`, `x×1===x` — so the other 21 weapons are untouched).
